        )

    return "\n\n".join(context_blocks)


prioritized_context = format_context(
    ordered_documents
)


# =========================================================
# 8. CREATE THE LANGCHAIN GENERATION PIPELINE
# =========================================================

prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You are an enterprise policy assistant.

Answer using only the prioritized evidence.

Rules:
1. Address every part of the question.
2. Preserve all numerical values and approval conditions.
3. Do not use unsupported or archived information.
4. Cite the source supporting the answer.
5. State clearly if a required answer aspect is missing.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Prioritized evidence:
{context}

Question:
{question}
""",
        ),
    ]
)

generation_chain = (
    prompt
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)

answer = generation_chain.invoke(
    {
        "context": prioritized_context,
        "question": question,
    }
)


# =========================================================
# 9. DISPLAY PRIORITY RESULTS
# =========================================================

print("PRIORITIZED CANDIDATES")
print("=" * 70)

for document in prioritized_candidates:
    print(
        document.metadata["source"],
        "| Topic:",
        document.metadata["topic"],
        "| Relevance:",
        round(document.metadata["relevance_score"], 3),
        "| Priority:",
        round(document.metadata["priority_score"], 3),
    )


print("\nFINAL CONTEXT ORDER")
print("=" * 70)

for position, document in enumerate(
    ordered_documents,
    start=1,
):
    print(
        position,
        document.metadata["source"],
        "| Topic:",
        document.metadata["topic"],
    )


print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
print("=" * 70)
print(answer)

vi) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Context Prioritization in a RAG system?

Context Prioritization is the process of assigning importance to selected information and deciding how that information should be ordered inside the final LLM prompt. It occurs after retrieval and usually after eligibility filtering, because invalid or unauthorized evidence should be removed before priority is calculated.

Priority may depend on query relevance, source authority, freshness, document status, evidence coverage, confidence, and token cost. Its goal is to ensure that the most important and reliable evidence is retained and prominently positioned when the context window is limited.

Q2. What is the difference between Context Selection and Context Prioritization?

Context Selection determines which retrieved documents are eligible to enter the prompt. It removes irrelevant, duplicated, outdated, unauthorized, low-quality, or oversized information based on the user’s question and application requirements.

Context Prioritization operates on the selected information and determines its relative importance and placement. Selection answers, “What should be included?” while prioritization answers, “What should receive the greatest attention and appear first?”

Q3. Which factors should be used to prioritize context?

The most common factors are query relevance, Re-Ranking score, source authority, freshness, policy version, confidence, security classification, evidence coverage, and token size. The application should also identify whether a document contains a direct answer, a necessary exception, supporting evidence, or only optional background.

The weights depend on the use case. A policy assistant may heavily prioritize document status and authority, while a research assistant may prioritize recency, citation quality, and coverage of different viewpoints. Weights should be evaluated rather than selected only by intuition.

Q4. Should the most relevant document always be placed first?

For a small, strongly Re-Ranked evidence set, placing the most relevant document first is a reasonable default. It provides the model with answer-bearing evidence early and keeps the prompt structure simple and predictable.

For a very long context, position-aware ordering may distribute important documents near the beginning and end to reduce Lost-in-the-Middle effects. However, ordering should not become a substitute for proper filtering, Re-Ranking, and compression. The preferred solution is usually to send fewer, stronger documents rather than attempting to arrange a large noisy context.

Topic 5: Long Context Handling

i) Definition

* Long Context Handling is the process of managing documents, conversation history, retrieved evidence, tool outputs, and intermediate results that are too 
  large or too noisy to send directly to an LLM. It ensures that important information remains available without exceeding the model’s token limit or 
  overwhelming it with unnecessary content.

* Long Context Handling involves more than choosing a model with a large context window. Even when a prompt technically fits, large inputs can increase 
  latency, token cost, distraction, and Lost-in-the-Middle effects, causing the model to overlook important evidence or rely on stale information.

* Common strategies include message trimming, conversation summarization, hierarchical summarization, retrieval over long-term memory, Context Compression, 
  document Re-Ranking, map-reduce processing, and recursive information synthesis. The appropriate strategy depends on whether the long context comes from 
  chat history, documents, tool calls, or multi-step agent execution.

* The main objective is to provide the model with the smallest sufficient working context for the current task. Complete historical data can remain in 
  persistent storage, while only recent, relevant, or summarized information is selected and added to the model’s prompt during each invocation.

ii) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Long Context Handling is to prevent context-window overflow as conversations, retrieved documents, and agent observations grow. If 
  the complete input exceeds the model’s supported limit, the provider may reject the request, truncate information, or leave insufficient capacity for 
  generating the final answer.

* Long Context Handling also improves response quality by removing stale, repeated, or off-topic information. LangChain notes that long conversations can 
  distract models even when they fit within the supported context, while also creating slower responses and higher processing costs.

* Another purpose is to preserve continuity without repeatedly sending every earlier message. Older interactions can be summarized, important user facts can 
  be stored as structured memory, and historical events can be retrieved only when they are relevant to the current question.

* Long Context Handling is essential for document assistants, research systems, legal analysis, long-running chatbots, coding agents, and multi-step 
  workflows. These applications may accumulate hundreds of pages, messages, or tool observations, making direct prompt concatenation impractical and 
  unreliable.

iii) Architectural Diagram

                   LONG CONTEXT HANDLING WORKFLOW
                   ==============================

                  Long Conversation or Documents
                               |
                               v
                     Context Classification
          Recent Messages | Old History | Documents
              Tool Results | Persistent Memories
                               |
               +---------------+---------------+
               |               |               |
               v               v               v
         Recent Context    Older Context    Long Documents
               |               |               |
               v               v               v
        Token Trimming    Summarization     Chunking and Indexing
               |               |               |
               +---------------+---------------+
                               |
                               v
                      Relevance Retrieval
                               |
                               v
                 Re-Ranking and Compression
                               |
                               v
                 Token-Budget-Aware Selection
                               |
                               v
                    Prioritized Final Context
                               |
                               v
                         LLM Generation
                               |
                               v
                  Update Summary and Memory

iv)  Core Components

1. Token Monitor and Context Classifier
The token monitor calculates the size of system instructions, conversation history, retrieved documents, tool results, and reserved output capacity. The classifier determines which information is recent, persistent, task-specific, document-based, or no longer relevant to the current request.

2. Trimming and Summarization Components
Trimming removes older or lower-priority messages when the history exceeds its allocated budget. Summarization replaces older interactions with a shorter representation that preserves important decisions, entities, constraints, unresolved questions, and user requirements.

3. External Memory and Retrieval Layer
Information that does not need to remain inside every prompt can be stored in a vector database, relational database, document store, or LangGraph store. Relevant memories or document chunks are retrieved only when the current question requires them, reducing repeated token usage.

4. Context Selector and Update Manager
The selector combines recent messages, summaries, retrieved memories, and document evidence within the available budget. After the response, the update manager decides whether new information should remain in short-term state, update the running summary, or be stored as long-term memory.

5. Long Context Handling Strategies

1. Message Trimming

Message trimming removes older conversation turns when the history exceeds a configured token limit. A common strategy keeps the system instructions and most recent messages while dropping older interactions, but critical decisions should first be stored separately or summarized.

LangChain’s trim_messages utility can trim history according to token count while preserving valid message order. It can retain the system message, keep recent messages, and ensure the resulting conversation begins with an appropriate human message.

2. Conversation Summarization

Conversation summarization replaces older messages with a compact running summary. The summary should retain entities, decisions, user requirements, tasks already completed, unresolved issues, and constraints that may influence future responses.

LangMem supports maintaining a running summary once conversation tokens cross a threshold. Its guidance recommends keeping the original message history separate from the summary supplied to the model, allowing the interface to display complete history while the LLM receives compressed context.

3. Retrieval-Based Memory

Retrieval-based memory stores historical information externally and retrieves only the memories relevant to the current input. This approach is more scalable than repeatedly transmitting the complete history and is suitable when conversations contain many facts that are not relevant to every request.

LangGraph stores can preserve long-term memories as structured JSON documents organized by namespace and key. Memories can be separated by user, organization, or application context and searched when required in later conversations.

4. Hierarchical and Map-Reduce Processing

Hierarchical processing divides very long documents into smaller sections, processes each section independently, and combines intermediate summaries into a final result. This avoids requiring the model to read the entire document collection in one prompt.

In map-reduce processing, the map stage extracts or summarizes information from individual chunks, while the reduce stage combines those intermediate outputs. Multiple reduction levels may be required when even the combined summaries exceed the context budget.

6. Long Context Handling vs Related Concepts

Long Context Handling vs Token Trimming
Token Trimming is one specific strategy that removes content once a configured limit is reached. Long Context Handling is the broader architecture that may combine trimming, summarization, retrieval, memory storage, compression, prioritization, and hierarchical processing.

Long Context Handling vs Context Selection
Context Selection determines which available information should enter the prompt for the current query. Long Context Handling additionally determines how large histories and documents should be stored, summarized, divided, retrieved, updated, and preserved over time.

Long Context Handling vs Long-Term Memory
Long-term memory stores information across conversations and sessions, while Long Context Handling controls what portion of that stored information is presented to the LLM. Persistent storage does not automatically mean that all memories should be inserted into every prompt.

Long Context Handling vs Large Context Windows
A large context window provides more token capacity, but it does not guarantee efficient or reliable use of every token. Long Context Handling remains necessary to control cost, latency, contextual noise, evidence placement, and Lost-in-the-Middle effects.

v) Code Example

from typing import Annotated, TypedDict

from langchain_core.messages import (
    AIMessage,
    BaseMessage,
    HumanMessage,
    SystemMessage,
)
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI

from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages


# =========================================================
# 1. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class LongContextState(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[list[BaseMessage], add_messages]
    summary: str
    working_messages: list[BaseMessage]


# =========================================================
# 2. CONFIGURE THE MODELS AND TOKEN THRESHOLDS
# =========================================================

chat_model = ChatOpenAI(
    model="gpt-4.1-mini",
    temperature=0,
)

summary_model = ChatOpenAI(
    model="gpt-4.1-mini",
    temperature=0,
)

SUMMARY_TRIGGER_TOKENS = 700
RECENT_MESSAGE_COUNT = 4


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE A TOKEN-COUNTING HELPER
# =========================================================

def count_message_tokens(
    messages: list[BaseMessage],
) -> int:
    return chat_model.get_num_tokens_from_messages(
        messages
    )


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE CONTEXT-MANAGEMENT NODE
# =========================================================

def manage_context_node(
    state: LongContextState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Summarize older messages when the complete history crosses
    the configured threshold. Keep recent messages unchanged.
    """

    complete_history = state["messages"]
    current_summary = state.get("summary", "")

    total_tokens = count_message_tokens(
        complete_history
    )

    # No summarization is required while history is small.
    if total_tokens <= SUMMARY_TRIGGER_TOKENS:
        return {
            "working_messages": complete_history,
        }

    # Preserve the latest messages in their original form.
    recent_messages = complete_history[
        -RECENT_MESSAGE_COUNT:
    ]

    older_messages = complete_history[
        :-RECENT_MESSAGE_COUNT
    ]

    older_history_text = "\n".join(
        (
            f"{message.type}: {message.content}"
        )
        for message in older_messages
    )

    summary_instruction = f"""
Create an accurate running summary of the conversation.

Preserve:
1. Important entities, names, dates and numerical values.
2. User requirements, preferences and constraints.
3. Decisions already made and tasks already completed.
4. Unresolved questions and information needed later.
5. Previously established policy facts and source references.

Do not introduce facts that are not present.

Existing summary:
{current_summary or "No existing summary."}

Older conversation:
{older_history_text}

Updated summary:
""".strip()

    updated_summary_message = summary_model.invoke(
        [
            SystemMessage(
                content=(
                    "You summarize long conversations accurately "
                    "without adding unsupported information."
                )
            ),
            HumanMessage(
                content=summary_instruction
            ),
        ]
    )

    updated_summary = str(
        updated_summary_message.content
    )

    working_messages = [
        SystemMessage(
            content=(
                "Conversation summary:\n"
                f"{updated_summary}"
            )
        ),
        *recent_messages,
    ]

    return {
        "summary": updated_summary,
        "working_messages": working_messages,
    }


# =========================================================
# 5. CREATE THE RESPONSE-GENERATION NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_response_node(
    state: LongContextState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Generate from the managed working context rather than
    transmitting the complete conversation history.
    """

    system_instruction = SystemMessage(
        content=(
            "You are an enterprise knowledge assistant. "
            "Use the conversation summary and recent messages. "
            "Do not invent information that is not available."
        )
    )

    model_input = [
        system_instruction,
        *state["working_messages"],
    ]

    response = chat_model.invoke(
        model_input
    )

    return {
        "messages": [response],
    }


# =========================================================
# 6. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(LongContextState)

workflow.add_node(
    "manage_context",
    manage_context_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_response",
    generate_response_node,
)

workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "manage_context",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "manage_context",
    "generate_response",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_response",
    END,
)


# =========================================================
# 7. ADD A CHECKPOINTER FOR THREAD-LEVEL MEMORY
# =========================================================

checkpointer = InMemorySaver()

long_context_graph = workflow.compile(
    checkpointer=checkpointer
)


# =========================================================
# 8. CONFIGURE THE CONVERSATION THREAD
# =========================================================

conversation_config = {
    "configurable": {
        "thread_id": "policy-conversation-001",
    }
}


# =========================================================
# 9. DEFINE A HELPER FOR CHAT INVOCATION
# =========================================================

def chat(user_message: str):
    result = long_context_graph.invoke(
        {
            "messages": [
                HumanMessage(content=user_message)
            ],
            "summary": "",
            "working_messages": [],
        },
        config=conversation_config,
    )

    latest_message = result["messages"][-1]

    print("\nUSER")
    print(user_message)

    print("\nASSISTANT")
    print(latest_message.content)

    print("\nRUNNING SUMMARY")
    print(result.get("summary", "No summary yet."))

    return result


# =========================================================
# 10. SIMULATE A LONG-RUNNING CONVERSATION
# =========================================================

chat(
    "I am studying the 2026 employee travel policy."
)

chat(
    "The policy says hotel accommodation is available for "
    "seven consecutive days."
)

chat(
    "An extension requires written approval from the "
    "project manager."
)

chat(
    "Meal expenses use destination-specific limits."
)

chat(
    "Travel-expense reports must be submitted within fifteen "
    "working days after the journey."
)

chat(
    "Remind me of the hotel limit and extension condition."
)


vi) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Long Context Handling?

Long Context Handling is the process of managing information that is too large, expensive, or noisy to pass directly to an LLM. The information may include conversation history, large documents, retrieved chunks, tool responses, or intermediate agent observations.

It combines techniques such as trimming, summarization, retrieval-based memory, Context Compression, prioritization, and hierarchical processing. The goal is to provide the model with sufficient working context while remaining within its token limit and avoiding unnecessary cost or distraction.

Q2. How do you manage a long-running conversation?

A common approach is to preserve recent messages in their original form while summarizing older conversation turns. Important facts such as user preferences, identifiers, decisions, and unresolved tasks can also be stored as structured memory rather than depending entirely on a generated summary.

During every request, the application combines the running summary, recent messages, relevant long-term memories, and current user input. The complete original history remains in persistent storage, but it is not transmitted to the model on every invocation.

Q3. What is the difference between short-term and long-term memory?

Short-term memory contains information needed within the current conversation or thread, such as recent messages, temporary decisions, tool outputs, and workflow state. LangGraph stores this as part of thread-scoped graph state and can persist it through a checkpointer.

Long-term memory stores information that should remain available across conversations or sessions. LangGraph stores organize such memories using namespaces and keys, allowing information to be separated by user or application context and retrieved when relevant.

Q4. What are the risks of conversation summarization?

Summarization may omit an important requirement, alter a numerical value, remove a negation, merge unrelated facts, or introduce information that was never stated. Repeatedly summarizing previous summaries can gradually amplify these errors and create summary drift.

Critical information should therefore be stored as structured state, and summaries should preserve source references where possible. Applications can also validate summaries, periodically regenerate them from original messages, and keep the full unmodified history for auditing and recovery.

Q5. Is a large-context model enough for long conversations?

No. A larger context window delays overflow but does not remove token cost, latency, distraction, stale information, or Lost-in-the-Middle problems. Sending the complete history on every request is inefficient even when the model accepts it.

A production system should still trim irrelevant information, summarize older interactions, retrieve historical memories selectively, and reserve sufficient output capacity. Large context windows provide useful capacity, but they do not replace deliberate context engineering.


Chapter 6: Agentic RAG

Topic 1: Agentic RAG Fundamentals

a) Definition

* Agentic RAG is an advanced Retrieval-Augmented Generation architecture in which an AI agent dynamically controls the retrieval and generation workflow. 
  Instead of following a fixed sequence for every question, the agent can decide whether retrieval is required, which knowledge source or tool to use, and 
  whether more information must be collected before answering.

* A traditional RAG pipeline usually follows a predefined flow such as query → retrieve → augment → generate. Agentic RAG introduces reasoning and 
  conditional control around these stages, allowing the system to rewrite an unclear query, perform multiple searches, grade retrieved documents, call 
  external tools, and repeat retrieval when the available evidence is insufficient.

* The word agentic means that the LLM does more than generate the final answer. It acts as a decision-making component that observes the current state,   
  selects an action, executes a tool, evaluates the result, and determines the next step. The process continues until sufficient evidence is available or a 
  configured stopping condition is reached.

* Agentic RAG is especially suitable for complex requests where the answer may be distributed across multiple sources or where the appropriate source is 
  unknown in advance. However, it introduces more model calls, tool executions, latency, cost, and unpredictable control flow than a straightforward two-
  step RAG pipeline.

b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Agentic RAG is to overcome the rigidity of fixed retrieval pipelines. Traditional RAG retrieves information for every question using the same method, even when retrieval is unnecessary, the query is poorly formed, or the answer requires another source. An agent can inspect the request and select an appropriate retrieval path instead.

* Agentic RAG supports dynamic evidence gathering. If the first retrieval returns weak or irrelevant documents, the agent can grade the evidence, rewrite the query, change the retrieval strategy, search a different source, or perform another retrieval attempt. This makes the workflow more adaptable to ambiguous and multi-step questions.

* Another purpose is to integrate different tools and knowledge sources within one reasoning workflow. An agent might search a vector database for policy text, query SQL for structured business data, call an API for current status, or traverse a knowledge graph for entity relationships before combining the collected evidence.

* Agentic RAG should be used when questions require planning, source selection, iterative retrieval, or validation. A simple document question-answering system may not need this complexity because the extra reasoning loops can increase latency and cost without significantly improving answer quality.

c) Architectural Diagram

                 AGENTIC RAG ARCHITECTURE
                 ========================

                       User Question
                              |
                              v
                         AI Agent
              Understand Intent and Plan Action
                              |
                              v
                    Is Retrieval Required?
                      /               \
                    No                 Yes
                    |                   |
                    v                   v
             Generate Directly     Select a Tool
                                  /      |       \
                                 v       v        v
                           Vector DB    SQL      API
                                 \       |        /
                                  \      |       /
                                   v     v      v
                                  Retrieved Evidence
                                          |
                                          v
                                  Grade the Evidence
                                   /              \
                              Relevant          Not Relevant
                                 |                  |
                                 v                  v
                        Sufficient Evidence?   Rewrite Query
                           /          \              |
                         Yes           No            |
                          |             |            |
                          v             v            |
                    Build Prompt   Retrieve Again <--+
                          |
                          v
                    Generate Answer
                          |
                          v
                 Reflection and Validation
                     /              \
                  Pass              Fail
                   |                 |
                   v                 v
              Final Answer      Correct or Retry


d) Core Components

1. Agent and Decision-Making Model
The agent interprets the user’s request and determines the next action. It may answer directly, call a retriever, choose another tool, rewrite the query, or stop the process. The LLM provides reasoning capability, while the surrounding application restricts actions through defined tools, graph edges, retry limits, and policies.

2. Retrieval Tools and Knowledge Sources
Retrieval tools provide controlled access to vector databases, keyword indexes, SQL databases, APIs, web search, and knowledge graphs. Each tool should have a clear description, input schema, output structure, authorization boundary, timeout, and error-handling policy so the agent can use it reliably.

3. Evidence Grader and Self-Correction Loop
The evidence grader determines whether retrieved documents are relevant to the question and whether they contain enough information for generation. If evidence is weak, the workflow can rewrite the query and retrieve again, but it should enforce a maximum retry count and stop safely when sufficient evidence cannot be found.

4. Shared State and Workflow Orchestrator
Shared state stores the original question, current query, selected action, retrieved evidence, tool results, retry count, and generated answer. LangGraph supports this design through state, nodes, edges, and conditional edges, allowing developers to create explicit and inspectable Agentic RAG workflows.

5. Major Capabilities of Agentic RAG

Dynamic Retrieval
The agent decides whether retrieval is required and which source should be searched. It may skip retrieval for a simple conversational message but use vector search, SQL, or an API when the request depends on private, structured, or current information.

Retrieval Planning
The agent decomposes a complex request into smaller information requirements and determines the order in which they should be resolved. Later searches may depend on entities or facts obtained from earlier searches, creating a multi-step evidence-gathering workflow.

Reflection and Self-Correction
Reflection evaluates whether retrieved evidence and the generated answer satisfy the request. Self-correction performs an action based on that evaluation, such as rewriting the query, changing tools, retrieving again, or revising an unsupported response.

Multi-Tool and Multi-Agent Collaboration
A single agent may call several specialized tools, or multiple agents may divide responsibilities such as planning, retrieval, evidence validation, and answer generation. This provides specialization but requires clear state management, termination rules, and conflict-resolution mechanisms.

e) Code Example

import json
from typing import Annotated, Literal, TypedDict

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

from langchain_core.documents import Document
from langchain_core.messages import (
    AIMessage,
    BaseMessage,
    HumanMessage,
    SystemMessage,
    ToolMessage,
)
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.tools import tool

from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbeddings
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages
from langgraph.prebuilt import ToolNode


# ============================================================
# 1. CONFIGURATION
# ============================================================

MAX_REWRITES = 2
RETRIEVAL_TOP_K = 3

LLM_MODEL = "gpt-4.1-mini"

EMBEDDING_MODEL = (
    "sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2"
)


# ============================================================
# 2. CREATE THE ENTERPRISE KNOWLEDGE BASE
# ============================================================

documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees travelling for approved business purposes "
            "can claim hotel accommodation for a maximum of seven "
            "consecutive days. Any extension beyond seven days "
            "requires written approval from the project manager."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "TRAVEL-001",
            "source": "travel_policy_2026.txt",
            "department": "Finance",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "travel accommodation",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees must submit completed business-travel "
            "expense reports within fifteen working days after "
            "completing the journey. Original or digital receipts "
            "must be attached to the report."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "TRAVEL-002",
            "source": "expense_submission_policy_2026.txt",
            "department": "Finance",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "travel expense submission",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Business travel must receive written approval from "
            "the employee's reporting manager before flights and "
            "accommodation are booked. International business-class "
            "travel additionally requires director approval."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "TRAVEL-003",
            "source": "travel_approval_policy_2026.txt",
            "department": "Finance",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "travel approval",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
            "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications. The employee must obtain manager "
            "approval before enrolling in the certification."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "LEARNING-001",
            "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
            "department": "Learning and Development",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "certification reimbursement",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Certification reimbursement requests must include "
            "proof of payment, the completion certificate, and the "
            "manager's approval. Claims must be submitted within "
            "thirty days after certification completion."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "LEARNING-002",
            "source": "certification_claim_process_2026.txt",
            "department": "Learning and Development",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "certification claim process",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
            "days during each calendar year. Unused sick leave "
            "cannot be carried forward into the following year."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "LEAVE-001",
            "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
            "department": "Human Resources",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "sick leave",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "When sick leave exceeds three consecutive working "
            "days, the employee must provide valid medical "
            "documentation to Human Resources."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "LEAVE-002",
            "source": "medical_leave_requirements_2026.txt",
            "department": "Human Resources",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "medical documentation",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees replacing their mobile device must "
            "re-register multi-factor authentication through the "
            "security information portal before accessing company "
            "applications from the replacement device."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "IDENTITY-001",
            "source": "mfa_device_replacement_guide.txt",
            "department": "Information Technology",
            "year": 2026,
            "status": "active",
            "topic": "multi-factor authentication",
        },
    ),
]


# ============================================================
# 3. CREATE THE EMBEDDING MODEL
# ============================================================

embedding_model = HuggingFaceEmbeddings(
    model_name=EMBEDDING_MODEL,
    encode_kwargs={
        "normalize_embeddings": True,
    },
)


# ============================================================
# 4. CREATE THE FAISS VECTOR STORE
# ============================================================

vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)


# ============================================================
# 5. CREATE THE BASE RETRIEVER
# ============================================================

retriever = vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": RETRIEVAL_TOP_K,
    },
)


# ============================================================
# 6. DEFINE THE RETRIEVAL TOOL
# ============================================================

@tool
def retrieve_enterprise_policies(query: str) -> str:
    """
    Search the approved enterprise policy knowledge base.

    Use this tool for questions about employee travel,
    expense claims, certification reimbursement, sick leave,
    medical documentation, and multi-factor authentication.

    Args:
        query:
            A clear standalone search query describing the
            enterprise-policy information required.

    Returns:
        A JSON string containing retrieved policy documents,
        source metadata, and document identifiers.
    """

    retrieved_documents = retriever.invoke(query)

    results = []

    for document in retrieved_documents:
        results.append(
            {
                "id": document.metadata.get(
                    "id",
                    "unknown",
                ),
                "source": document.metadata.get(
                    "source",
                    "unknown",
                ),
                "department": document.metadata.get(
                    "department",
                    "unknown",
                ),
                "year": document.metadata.get(
                    "year",
                    "unknown",
                ),
                "status": document.metadata.get(
                    "status",
                    "unknown",
                ),
                "topic": document.metadata.get(
                    "topic",
                    "unknown",
                ),
                "content": document.page_content,
            }
        )

    return json.dumps(
        results,
        indent=2,
        ensure_ascii=False,
    )


# ============================================================
# 7. CONFIGURE THE CHAT MODEL
# ============================================================

llm = ChatOpenAI(
    model=LLM_MODEL,
    temperature=0,
)


# ============================================================
# 8. BIND THE RETRIEVAL TOOL TO THE MODEL
# ============================================================

tools = [retrieve_enterprise_policies]

agent_model = llm.bind_tools(tools)


# ============================================================
# 9. DEFINE STRUCTURED GRADING OUTPUT
# ============================================================

class EvidenceGrade(BaseModel):
    relevant: bool = Field(
        description=(
            "True when the retrieved policy evidence is "
            "relevant to the user's original question."
        )
    )

    sufficient: bool = Field(
        description=(
            "True when the evidence contains enough information "
            "to answer all important parts of the question."
        )
    )

    reason: str = Field(
        description=(
            "A brief explanation of why the evidence is or is "
            "not relevant and sufficient."
        )
    )


# ============================================================
# 10. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# ============================================================

class AgenticRAGState(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[
        list[BaseMessage],
        add_messages,
    ]

    original_question: str
    search_query: str

    rewrite_count: int
    max_rewrites: int

    evidence_relevant: bool
    evidence_sufficient: bool
    grading_reason: str

    final_answer: str


# ============================================================
# 11. HELPER: FIND THE MOST RECENT TOOL RESULT
# ============================================================

def get_latest_tool_message(
    messages: list[BaseMessage],
) -> ToolMessage | None:
    for message in reversed(messages):
        if isinstance(message, ToolMessage):
            return message

    return None


# ============================================================
# 12. HELPER: FORMAT THE TOOL EVIDENCE
# ============================================================

def parse_tool_evidence(
    tool_message: ToolMessage | None,
) -> listif tool_message is None:
        return []

    try:
        data = json.loads(
            str(tool_message.content)
        )

        if isinstance(data, list):
            return data

        return []

    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        return []


def format_evidence(
    evidence_items: list[dict],
) -> str:
    if not evidence_items:
        return "No valid policy evidence was retrieved."

    evidence_blocks = []

    for index, item in enumerate(
        evidence_items,
        start=1,
    ):
        evidence_blocks.append(
            "\n".join(
                [
                    f"Evidence {index}",
                    f"Document ID: {item.get('id')}",
                    f"Source: {item.get('source')}",
                    f"Department: {item.get('department')}",
                    f"Year: {item.get('year')}",
                    f"Status: {item.get('status')}",
                    f"Topic: {item.get('topic')}",
                    f"Content: {item.get('content')}",
                ]
            )
        )

    return "\n\n".join(evidence_blocks)


# ============================================================
# 13. AGENT DECISION NODE
# ============================================================

AGENT_SYSTEM_PROMPT = """
You are an enterprise knowledge assistant.

You have access to a tool named:
retrieve_enterprise_policies

Use the tool when the user asks about:
- employee travel policies
- hotel accommodation
- expense claims
- certification reimbursement
- sick leave
- medical documentation
- multi-factor authentication
- any other organization-specific policy

Do not use the retrieval tool for:
- greetings
- casual conversation
- basic arithmetic
- requests that do not require enterprise information

When retrieval is required:
1. Call the tool with a clear standalone search query.
2. Preserve names, years, amounts, dates and negations.
3. Do not answer the policy question before receiving tool evidence.

When retrieval is unnecessary:
Answer the user directly.
""".strip()


def agent_decision_node(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    The agent decides whether to call the retrieval tool.

    When the graph loops after query rewriting, the rewritten
    query is presented to the agent as the current search request.
    """

    if state["search_query"]:
        active_query = state["search_query"]
    else:
        active_query = state["original_question"]

    model_messages = [
        SystemMessage(
            content=AGENT_SYSTEM_PROMPT
        ),
        HumanMessage(
            content=active_query
        ),
    ]

    response = agent_model.invoke(
        model_messages
    )

    return {
        "messages": [response],
    }


# ============================================================
# 14. ROUTE AFTER THE AGENT DECISION
# ============================================================

def route_after_agent(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> Literal["tools", "direct_answer"]:
    """
    If the latest AI message contains a tool call, execute it.
    Otherwise, treat the AI message as a direct response.
    """

    if not state["messages"]:
        return "direct_answer"

    latest_message = state["messages"][-1]

    if (
        isinstance(latest_message, AIMessage)
        and latest_message.tool_calls
    ):
        return "tools"

    return "direct_answer"


# ============================================================
# 15. TOOL EXECUTION NODE
# ============================================================

tool_node = ToolNode(tools)


# ============================================================
# 16. EVIDENCE-GRADING CHAIN
# ============================================================

grading_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You are an evidence-quality evaluator for an enterprise
Retrieval-Augmented Generation system.

Evaluate whether the retrieved evidence is:
1. Relevant to the original user question.
2. Sufficient to answer all important parts of the question.

Rules:
- Do not answer the user's question.
- Judge only the supplied evidence.
- Relevant but incomplete evidence is not sufficient.
- Empty or malformed evidence is neither relevant nor sufficient.
- An active policy is preferable to an archived policy.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Original user question:
{question}

Search query:
{search_query}

Retrieved evidence:
{evidence}
""",
        ),
    ]
)

grading_chain = (
    grading_prompt
    | llm.with_structured_output(EvidenceGrade)
)


def grade_evidence_node(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> dict:
    latest_tool_message = get_latest_tool_message(
        state["messages"]
    )

    evidence_items = parse_tool_evidence(
        latest_tool_message
    )

    formatted_evidence = format_evidence(
        evidence_items
    )

    grade = grading_chain.invoke(
        {
            "question": state["original_question"],
            "search_query": state["search_query"],
            "evidence": formatted_evidence,
        }
    )

    return {
        "evidence_relevant": grade.relevant,
        "evidence_sufficient": grade.sufficient,
        "grading_reason": grade.reason,
    }


# ============================================================
# 17. ROUTE AFTER EVIDENCE GRADING
# ============================================================

def route_after_grading(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> Literal[
    "generate",
    "rewrite",
    "insufficient",
]:
    if (
        state["evidence_relevant"]
        and state["evidence_sufficient"]
    ):
        return "generate"

    if (
        state["rewrite_count"]
        < state["max_rewrites"]
    ):
        return "rewrite"

    return "insufficient"


# ============================================================
# 18. QUERY-REWRITING CHAIN
# ============================================================

class RewrittenSearchQuery(BaseModel):
    query: str = Field(
        description=(
            "A clear standalone query optimized for searching "
            "the enterprise policy knowledge base."
        )
    )

    reason: str = Field(
        description=(
            "A short explanation of how the rewrite improves "
            "retrieval without changing the user's intention."
        )
    )


rewrite_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You rewrite weak enterprise-policy search queries.

Create one clear standalone search query.

Rules:
1. Preserve the original user intention.
2. Preserve names, dates, years, amounts and negations.
3. Include appropriate policy terminology.
4. Remove unnecessary conversational phrasing.
5. Do not answer the question.
6. Do not invent a policy, amount, condition or date.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Original question:
{question}

Previous search query:
{search_query}

Reason the evidence was inadequate:
{grading_reason}

Rewrite number:
{rewrite_number}
""",
        ),
    ]
)

rewrite_chain = (
    rewrite_prompt
    | llm.with_structured_output(
        RewrittenSearchQuery
    )
)


def rewrite_query_node(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> dict:
    rewritten = rewrite_chain.invoke(
        {
            "question": state["original_question"],
            "search_query": (
                state["search_query"]
                or state["original_question"]
            ),
            "grading_reason": state["grading_reason"],
            "rewrite_number": (
                state["rewrite_count"] + 1
            ),
        }
    )

    return {
        "search_query": rewritten.query,
        "rewrite_count": (
            state["rewrite_count"] + 1
        ),
    }


# ============================================================
# 19. GROUNDED GENERATION CHAIN
# ============================================================

generation_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You are an enterprise policy assistant.

Answer the user's original question using only the retrieved
policy evidence.

Rules:
1. Do not use unsupported information.
2. Preserve all numbers, dates, durations and conditions.
3. Answer every part of the question supported by evidence.
4. Cite the source document after the relevant statement.
5. Do not cite a source that was not retrieved.
6. If part of the question is unsupported, identify that part.
7. Prefer active and current policy evidence.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Original question:
{question}

Retrieved policy evidence:
{evidence}

Produce a clear, concise, grounded answer.
""",
        ),
    ]
)

generation_chain = (
    generation_prompt
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)


def generate_answer_node(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> dict:
    latest_tool_message = get_latest_tool_message(
        state["messages"]
    )

    evidence_items = parse_tool_evidence(
        latest_tool_message
    )

    formatted_evidence = format_evidence(
        evidence_items
    )

    answer = generation_chain.invoke(
        {
            "question": state["original_question"],
            "evidence": formatted_evidence,
        }
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": answer,
        "messages": [
            AIMessage(content=answer)
        ],
    }


# ============================================================
# 20. DIRECT-ANSWER NODE
# ============================================================

def direct_answer_node(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Use the agent's latest response when no tool call was made.
    """

    if not state["messages"]:
        answer = (
            "I could not process the request."
        )
    else:
        latest_message = state["messages"][-1]
        answer = str(latest_message.content)

    return {
        "final_answer": answer,
    }


# ============================================================
# 21. INSUFFICIENT-EVIDENCE NODE
# ============================================================

def insufficient_evidence_node(
    state: AgenticRAGState,
) -> dict:
    answer = (
        "I could not find sufficient approved policy evidence "
        "to answer the question after "
        f"{state['rewrite_count']} retrieval rewrite attempt(s). "
        "Please provide a more specific policy name, department, "
        "year, or business process."
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": answer,
        "messages": [
            AIMessage(content=answer)
        ],
    }


# ============================================================
# 22. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# ============================================================

workflow = StateGraph(AgenticRAGState)

workflow.add_node(
    "agent",
    agent_decision_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "tools",
    tool_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "grade_evidence",
    grade_evidence_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "rewrite_query",
    rewrite_query_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "direct_answer",
    direct_answer_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "insufficient_evidence",
    insufficient_evidence_node,
)


# Start with the agent decision.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "agent",
)


# Decide whether to execute the retrieval tool.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "agent",
    route_after_agent,
    {
        "tools": "tools",
        "direct_answer": "direct_answer",
    },
)


# Grade evidence after tool execution.
workflow.add_edge(
    "tools",
    "grade_evidence",
)


# Generate, rewrite, or stop according to evidence quality.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "grade_evidence",
    route_after_grading,
    {
        "generate": "generate_answer",
        "rewrite": "rewrite_query",
        "insufficient": "insufficient_evidence",
    },
)


# After rewriting, let the agent call the retrieval tool again.
workflow.add_edge(
    "rewrite_query",
    "agent",
)


# Terminal paths.
workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "direct_answer",
    END,
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "insufficient_evidence",
    END,
)


# Compile the graph.
agentic_rag_graph = workflow.compile()


# ============================================================
# 23. HELPER TO RUN A QUERY
# ============================================================

def run_agentic_rag(
    question: str,
) -> AgenticRAGState:
    initial_state: AgenticRAGState = {
        "messages": [
            HumanMessage(content=question)
        ],
        "original_question": question,
        "search_query": question,
        "rewrite_count": 0,
        "max_rewrites": MAX_REWRITES,
        "evidence_relevant": False,
        "evidence_sufficient": False,
        "grading_reason": "",
        "final_answer": "",
    }

    result = agentic_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    return result


# ============================================================
# 24. DISPLAY GRAPH EXECUTION DETAILS
# ============================================================

def display_result(
    result: AgenticRAGState,
) -> None:
    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("ORIGINAL QUESTION")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(result["original_question"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("FINAL SEARCH QUERY")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(result["search_query"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("REWRITE COUNT")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(result["rewrite_count"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("EVIDENCE RELEVANT")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(result["evidence_relevant"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("EVIDENCE SUFFICIENT")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(result["evidence_sufficient"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("GRADING REASON")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(
        result["grading_reason"]
        or "Retrieval was not required."
    )

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("FINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 80)
    print(result["final_answer"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 80)
    print("MESSAGE TRACE")
    print("=" * 80)

    for index, message in enumerate(
        result["messages"],
        start=1,
    ):
        message_type = type(message).__name__

        print(f"\nMessage {index}: {message_type}")
        print("-" * 80)

        if isinstance(message, AIMessage):
            if message.tool_calls:
                print("Tool calls:")
                print(
                    json.dumps(
                        message.tool_calls,
                        indent=2,
                        ensure_ascii=False,
                    )
                )
            elif message.content:
                print(message.content)

        elif isinstance(message, ToolMessage):
            print("Tool name:", message.name)
            print("Tool call ID:", message.tool_call_id)
            print("Tool result:")
            print(message.content)

        else:
            print(message.content)


# ============================================================
# 25. RUN EXAMPLE POLICY QUERY
# ============================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    policy_question = (
        "What is the annual reimbursement limit for approved "
        "professional certifications, and what must an employee "
        "do before registering?"
    )

    policy_result = run_agentic_rag(
        policy_question
    )

    display_result(
        policy_result
    )


    # Optional second example showing a direct-answer route.
    casual_question = "Hello, how are you?"

    casual_result = run_agentic_rag(
        casual_question
    )

    display_result(
        casual_result
    )


f) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Agentic RAG?

Agentic RAG is an advanced RAG architecture in which an AI agent controls how information is retrieved, evaluated, and used. Instead of following a fixed retrieve-and-generate sequence, the agent can decide whether retrieval is needed, select an appropriate tool, evaluate retrieved evidence, rewrite the query, retrieve again, or produce a final response.

The architecture combines an LLM’s reasoning ability with retrieval tools, shared state, conditional routing, correction loops, and termination rules. It is useful for complex questions where one fixed retrieval operation cannot reliably gather all required evidence.

Q2. How is Agentic RAG different from traditional RAG?

Traditional RAG normally follows a predefined pipeline in which every query is embedded, documents are retrieved, context is added to a prompt, and the LLM generates an answer. The pipeline does not necessarily evaluate whether retrieval was needed or whether the retrieved documents were sufficient.

Agentic RAG introduces dynamic decision-making. The agent may skip retrieval, choose from multiple tools, perform several searches, grade evidence, rewrite weak queries, or self-correct an unsupported response. This flexibility improves adaptability but also increases complexity, latency, and cost.

Q3. What components are required in an Agentic RAG system?

The main components are a decision-making agent, one or more retrieval tools, knowledge sources, shared workflow state, an evidence grader, a query rewriter, a grounded response generator, and stopping conditions. More advanced systems may also include planning, reflection, self-correction, memory, and multiple specialized agents.

A production implementation additionally requires tool authorization, timeout controls, iteration limits, observation tracing, token budgets, error handling, grounding evaluation, citation validation, and safe fallback responses.

Q4. Why is LangGraph suitable for Agentic RAG?

LangGraph represents an Agentic RAG workflow as a graph containing shared state, computation nodes, edges, and conditional routing. This allows developers to explicitly define when retrieval occurs, how documents are graded, when a query should be rewritten, and when the system must stop.

Its stateful architecture is useful for preserving intermediate queries, retrieved evidence, tool outputs, iteration counts, and generated answers across multiple workflow steps. The official LangGraph Agentic RAG tutorial demonstrates a custom retrieval agent that decides when to search and uses graph primitives for retrieval-oriented control flow.

Q5. What are the main risks of Agentic RAG?

The main risks are uncontrolled loops, incorrect tool selection, poor query rewriting, acceptance of irrelevant evidence, excessive model calls, unauthorized tool access, and unpredictable latency. Because the agent influences control flow, a reasoning error can affect several later stages.

These risks can be controlled through typed state, structured model outputs, allowed-tool lists, deterministic routing rules, maximum retry counts, timeouts, permission checks, token budgets, tracing, and insufficient-evidence fallbacks. The agent should operate within predefined boundaries rather than receiving unlimited autonomy.

Topic 2: Dynamic Retrieval

a) Definition

* Dynamic Retrieval is an Agentic RAG technique in which the system decides at runtime whether retrieval is required, which knowledge source should be searched, and how retrieval should be configured. Unlike a fixed RAG pipeline, every user question does not automatically pass through the same retriever with the same Top-K value and search method.

* The retrieval decision can depend on the user’s intent, query complexity, conversation history, required freshness, data format, and available tools. A policy question may be routed to a vector database, a numerical request to SQL, and a current operational-status question to an API.

* Dynamic Retrieval can also modify the retrieval strategy during execution. The agent may begin with semantic search, switch to Hybrid Search when an exact identifier is detected, increase Top-K when evidence is incomplete, or rewrite the query if the first search returns irrelevant documents.

* LangGraph is suitable for Dynamic Retrieval because it combines shared state, deterministic nodes, LLM-driven decisions, conditional edges, and bounded loops. It allows the application to remain controlled while still adapting its retrieval path to runtime information.


b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Dynamic Retrieval is to avoid performing unnecessary retrieval. Greetings, basic arithmetic, and general conversation may not require enterprise knowledge, while company-policy questions must be grounded in authorized documents. Skipping unnecessary retrieval reduces latency, search workload, and token consumption.

* It also helps select the correct source for each information requirement. Unstructured policies are suitable for vector search, structured employee records are better suited to SQL, and current application data should come from an API. Sending every query to one vector database can produce incomplete or inappropriate evidence.

* Dynamic Retrieval improves retrieval quality by adapting parameters to the query. A simple factual question may need only three chunks, while a comparison may require a larger candidate set, Metadata Filtering, Re-Ranking, or searches across multiple sources.

* The technique is most useful when an application contains several knowledge sources and receives varied queries. However, LLM-based routing can select the wrong source, so production workflows need typed outputs, permitted-route lists, fallback behaviour, and limits on repeated retrieval attempts.

c) Architectural Diagram

                    DYNAMIC RETRIEVAL WORKFLOW
                    ==========================

                         User Question
                               |
                               v
                    Intent and Query Analysis
                               |
                               v
                   Is Retrieval Necessary?
                       /               \
                     No                 Yes
                     |                   |
                     v                   v
              Direct Response       Select Source
                                   /      |       \
                                  v       v        v
                            Vector DB    SQL      API
                                  \       |        /
                                   \      |       /
                                    v     v      v
                                  Retrieved Evidence
                                           |
                                           v
                              Evidence Quality Evaluation
                                  /                 \
                          Sufficient              Insufficient
                              |                       |
                              v                       v
                     Prompt Construction       Adapt Retrieval
                                               Strategy/Top-K/
                                               Query/Source
                              |                       |
                              |              Retrieve Again
                              |                       |
                              +-----------<-----------+
                              |
                              v
                         Generate Answer
                              |
                              v
                    Grounding and Citation Check
                              |
                              v
                          Final Response


d) Core Components

1. Intent Classifier and Retrieval Router
The classifier determines whether retrieval is needed and identifies the most appropriate route. It can return structured values such as direct, vector, sql, or api, preventing the model from inventing unsupported tool names or arbitrary execution paths.

2. Specialized Retrieval Tools
Each tool accesses one approved information source and has a clear input schema, description, timeout, permission boundary, and output format. Tool descriptions are especially important because the agent uses them to understand which source is appropriate for a request.

3. Evidence Grader and Adaptation Controller
The evidence grader checks whether the retrieved material is relevant and sufficient. The adaptation controller determines whether to rewrite the query, modify Top-K, switch sources, or stop after the configured attempt limit.

4. Shared State and Conditional Orchestrator
Shared state stores the original question, selected route, search query, retrieved evidence, attempt count, and final answer. LangGraph uses nodes and conditional routing to combine predictable business logic with flexible LLM decisions in one workflow.

5. Dynamic Retrieval Decisions

Retrieval Requirement
The system first decides whether external information is needed. A greeting can be answered directly, while a question about an internal reimbursement policy must retrieve authoritative enterprise evidence before generation.

Source Selection
The source is selected according to the required data type and freshness. Vector search handles unstructured documents, SQL handles structured records, and APIs handle live operational or application information.

Search-Strategy Selection
Semantic search is useful for conceptual questions, while BM25 or Hybrid Search is stronger for exact identifiers and technical terms. Metadata Filtering is added when the query contains restrictions such as year, region, policy status, or department.

Retrieval-Depth Selection
Dynamic Top-K allows the system to control the number of retrieved candidates. Direct factual questions can use a small K, while comparisons and multi-part questions may require a broader candidate set followed by Re-Ranking and Context Selection.

e) Code Example

from typing import Literal, TypedDict

from langchain_core.documents import Document
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbeddings

from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE SAMPLE KNOWLEDGE-BASE DOCUMENTS
# =========================================================

documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees travelling for approved business purposes "
            "can claim hotel accommodation for a maximum of seven "
            "consecutive days. Any extension requires written "
            "approval from the project manager."
        ),
        metadata={
            "source": "travel_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "travel",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees can claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
            "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
            "registration."
        ),
        metadata={
            "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "certification",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
            "days during each calendar year. Unused sick leave "
            "cannot be carried forward."
        ),
        metadata={
            "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "leave",
        },
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE THE EMBEDDING MODEL
# =========================================================

embedding_model = HuggingFaceEmbeddings(
    model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2",
    encode_kwargs={
        "normalize_embeddings": True,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 3. CREATE THE FAISS VECTOR STORE
# =========================================================

vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE RETRIEVER
# =========================================================

retriever = vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": 1,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 5. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class DynamicRetrievalState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    route: str
    retrieved_documents: list[Document]
    answer: str


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE THE ROUTER NODE
# =========================================================

def route_question_node(
    state: DynamicRetrievalState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Determine whether the question needs enterprise retrieval.

    This simplified example uses keywords. A production system
    could use an LLM with structured output for classification.
    """

    question = state["question"].lower()

    policy_keywords = [
        "policy",
        "hotel",
        "travel",
        "certification",
        "reimbursement",
        "sick leave",
        "employee leave",
    ]

    retrieval_required = any(
        keyword in question
        for keyword in policy_keywords
    )

    if retrieval_required:
        selected_route = "retrieve"
    else:
        selected_route = "direct"

    return {
        "route": selected_route,
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. DEFINE CONDITIONAL ROUTING
# =========================================================

def choose_next_node(
    state: DynamicRetrievalState,
) -> Literal["retrieve", "direct"]:
    """
    Select the next graph node based on the route decided
    by route_question_node().
    """

    if state["route"] == "retrieve":
        return "retrieve"

    return "direct"


# =========================================================
# 8. CREATE THE RETRIEVAL NODE
# =========================================================

def retrieve_documents_node(
    state: DynamicRetrievalState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Search the vector database using the user's question.
    """

    retrieved_documents = retriever.invoke(
        state["question"]
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_documents": retrieved_documents,
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. CREATE THE GROUNDED-ANSWER NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_grounded_answer_node(
    state: DynamicRetrievalState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Create a simple answer using retrieved evidence.

    This is intentionally kept provider-independent.
    In a full application, the context would be passed to an LLM.
    """

    retrieved_documents = state["retrieved_documents"]

    if not retrieved_documents:
        return {
            "answer": (
                "The required information was not found in the "
                "enterprise knowledge base."
            )
        }

    best_document = retrieved_documents[0]

    answer = (
        f"Based on the retrieved enterprise document:\n\n"
        f"{best_document.page_content}\n\n"
        f"Source: {best_document.metadata['source']}"
    )

    return {
        "answer": answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. CREATE THE DIRECT-ANSWER NODE
# =========================================================

def direct_answer_node(
    state: DynamicRetrievalState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Handle requests that do not need knowledge-base retrieval.
    """

    question = state["question"].lower()

    if "hello" in question or "hi" in question:
        answer = (
            "Hello! How can I help you with your GenAI learning?"
        )
    else:
        answer = (
            "This question does not require enterprise document "
            "retrieval."
        )

    return {
        "answer": answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(DynamicRetrievalState)

workflow.add_node(
    "route_question",
    route_question_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "retrieve",
    retrieve_documents_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_grounded_answer",
    generate_grounded_answer_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "direct",
    direct_answer_node,
)


# Start with query classification.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "route_question",
)


# Dynamically select the retrieval or direct route.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "route_question",
    choose_next_node,
    {
        "retrieve": "retrieve",
        "direct": "direct",
    },
)


# Retrieval path.
workflow.add_edge(
    "retrieve",
    "generate_grounded_answer",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_grounded_answer",
    END,
)


# Direct-response path.
workflow.add_edge(
    "direct",
    END,
)


# Compile the workflow.
dynamic_retrieval_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 12. CREATE A FUNCTION TO RUN THE GRAPH
# =========================================================

def run_dynamic_retrieval(question: str) -> None:
    initial_state: DynamicRetrievalState = {
        "question": question,
        "route": "",
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "answer": "",
    }

    result = dynamic_retrieval_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("Question:")
    print(result["question"])

    print("\nSelected route:")
    print(result["route"])

    print("\nAnswer:")
    print(result["answer"])

    print()


# =========================================================
# 13. TEST BOTH WORKFLOW PATHS
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # This question requires enterprise retrieval.
    run_dynamic_retrieval(
        "What is the annual certification reimbursement limit?"
    )

    # This message does not require retrieval.
    run_dynamic_retrieval(
        "Hello, how are you?"
    )


f) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Dynamic Retrieval in Agentic RAG?

Dynamic Retrieval is a runtime retrieval strategy in which an agent decides whether retrieval is required, which source should be searched, and how the search should be configured. The retrieval path is selected according to the current query rather than being fixed for every request.

For example, a policy question may use a vector database, an employee-record question may use SQL, and a live service-status question may use an API. The agent may also adjust Top-K, rewrite a weak query, or retry retrieval when evidence is insufficient.

Q2. How does Dynamic Retrieval differ from traditional RAG?

Traditional RAG generally sends every question through the same predefined retriever and uses a fixed retrieval strategy. The pipeline may perform retrieval even when it is unnecessary or use the same data source for fundamentally different information requirements.

Dynamic Retrieval introduces conditional source selection and adaptive search behaviour. It can skip retrieval, route to different tools, change search parameters, evaluate evidence, and repeat retrieval with limits. This makes it more flexible but also more complex and expensive.

Q3. How should an agent choose between vector search, SQL, and an API?
