Vector search should be used for unstructured natural-language information such as policies, manuals, and knowledge articles. SQL should be used when the answer depends on structured records, aggregations, joins, exact identifiers, or tabular business data.

An API should be selected when information is operational, current, application-specific, or accessible only through an approved service interface. Source descriptions, query intent, required freshness, and authorization rules should guide the decision.

Q4. How can incorrect routing be controlled?

Routing should use structured outputs containing a limited set of permitted routes. Deterministic rules should handle obvious cases such as employee IDs, error codes, or known service names, while the LLM handles ambiguous requests.

The workflow should also validate source compatibility, grade retrieved evidence, provide a fallback source, enforce retry limits, and trace routing decisions. Evaluation datasets should contain examples for every route, boundary case, and expected failure path.

Q5. When should Dynamic Retrieval be avoided?

Dynamic Retrieval may be unnecessary when an application has one small, stable knowledge source and receives only straightforward document questions. A fixed retriever is simpler, faster, easier to evaluate, and often sufficient in such situations.

It should be introduced when several data sources, query types, or retrieval strategies are genuinely required. The additional router, grader, retries, and tool integrations should provide measurable improvements that justify their latency and operational complexity.

Topic 3: Retrieval Planning

a) Definition

* Retrieval Planning is the process of creating a structured search plan before retrieving information from external knowledge sources. Instead of sending the complete user question directly to one retriever, the agent analyzes the goal, identifies the required facts, and breaks the request into smaller retrieval steps.

* A retrieval plan normally specifies what information must be found, which source or tool should be used, and in what order the searches should be performed. The plan can contain independent searches that run separately or dependent searches where one result is required before the next query can be created.

* Retrieval Planning commonly follows the Planner-Executor pattern. The planner creates an ordered list of retrieval tasks, the executor performs each task, and a controller checks whether the plan is complete or needs modification. LangGraph supports this through shared state, cyclic graphs, and conditional routing.

* Unlike Query Decomposition, Retrieval Planning covers more than splitting a question. It can also select tools, define dependencies, determine execution order, track completed tasks, evaluate collected evidence, and decide whether replanning is necessary.

b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Retrieval Planning is to handle complex questions that cannot be reliably answered through one search. For example, a user may ask for the policy applicable to the department that owns a project, requiring the agent to identify the department before retrieving the appropriate policy.

* Planning reduces the chance that the agent will retrieve information randomly or repeatedly. By identifying required facts before execution, the system creates focused subqueries, avoids unnecessary searches, and maintains a clear relationship between each retrieved document and the original question.

* Retrieval Planning also enables the agent to use different sources for different subtasks. One step may search project documents through a vector database, another may query structured employee records through SQL, and a final step may retrieve current operational information through an API.

* Explicit planning can improve long-horizon task completion because the agent considers the complete information requirement before acting. Plan-and-execute designs can also use a stronger model for planning and smaller or deterministic components for execution, improving control and potentially reducing cost.

c) Types of Retrieval Plans

1. Sequential Retrieval Plan
* In sequential planning, retrieval tasks are performed in a fixed order because later steps depend on earlier results. For example, the agent must first identify which department owns Project Orion before it can search for that department’s certification policy.

2. Parallel Retrieval Plan
* In parallel planning, independent retrieval tasks can run at the same time. A question comparing the travel and certification policies can retrieve both policies independently and combine the results after both searches are completed.

3. Conditional Retrieval Plan
* A conditional plan selects the next retrieval action according to the evidence already collected. If vector search returns sufficient evidence, the agent proceeds to generation; otherwise, it may rewrite the query, search another source, or request additional information.

4. Iterative or Replanning Workflow
* In an iterative plan, the agent evaluates progress after one or more steps and revises the remaining plan when required. Replanning is useful when the initial assumptions were incorrect, a tool returned no evidence, or a newly discovered entity changes the required search path.

d) Architectural Diagram

                    RETRIEVAL PLANNING WORKFLOW
                    ===========================

                         User Question
                               |
                               v
                        Planner Agent
             Understand Goal, Facts and Constraints
                               |
                               v
                    Create Structured Plan
           Step 1 -> Step 2 -> Step 3 -> Final Answer
                               |
                               v
                    Select Current Plan Step
                               |
                               v
                       Retrieval Executor
              Vector Search | SQL | API | Graph
                               |
                               v
                     Store Retrieved Evidence
                               |
                               v
                     Is the Plan Complete?
                         /             \
                       No               Yes
                       |                 |
                       v                 v
              Execute Next Step   Evaluate Evidence
                                        |
                              +---------+---------+
                              |                   |
                         Sufficient           Insufficient
                              |                   |
                              v                   v
                      Generate Answer          Replan
                              |                   |
                              v                   |
                             END <----------------+

e) Components

1. Planner Agent
The planner interprets the original goal and converts it into a structured list of retrieval tasks. Each task should contain a clear search query, expected information, appropriate source, and any dependency on earlier plan steps.

2. Retrieval Executor
The executor performs the current plan step using an approved retriever or tool. Unlike the planner, the executor should be focused and predictable, because its responsibility is to execute a specific search rather than redesign the complete reasoning process.

3. Shared State and Controller
Shared state stores the original question, plan, current step, completed tasks, retrieved evidence, and execution status. The controller reads this state and decides whether to execute another step, evaluate the evidence, replan, or proceed to final generation.

4. Evidence Evaluator and Replanner
The evaluator checks whether the accumulated evidence covers every requirement in the original question. If important information is missing, the replanner creates additional tasks or modifies the existing plan while respecting maximum-step, timeout, and cost limits.

5. Retrieval Planning vs Related Concepts

Retrieval Planning vs Query Decomposition
Query Decomposition separates a complex request into smaller questions. Retrieval Planning includes decomposition but also determines source selection, execution order, dependencies, state updates, stopping conditions, and possible replanning.

Retrieval Planning vs Dynamic Retrieval
Dynamic Retrieval decides whether retrieval is needed and which retrieval strategy or source should be used at runtime. Retrieval Planning organizes several retrieval actions into a complete sequence designed to satisfy a complex information goal.

Retrieval Planning vs Multi-Hop Retrieval
Multi-Hop Retrieval performs dependent searches where later queries use information from earlier evidence. Retrieval Planning is the broader control process that can deliberately create, coordinate, and evaluate those retrieval hops.

Retrieval Planning vs ReAct
A ReAct-style agent normally chooses one action, observes the result, and then decides the next action. A plan-and-execute agent creates a broader plan first, which can improve global coordination and reduce repeated calls to a large planning model.

f) Code Example

from typing import TypedDict

from langchain_core.documents import Document
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbeddings

from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE THE KNOWLEDGE BASE
# =========================================================

documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees travelling for approved business purposes "
            "can claim hotel accommodation for a maximum of seven "
            "consecutive days. Any extension requires written "
            "approval from the project manager."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "TRAVEL-001",
            "source": "travel_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "travel accommodation",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees can claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
            "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
            "registration."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "CERT-001",
            "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "certification reimbursement",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
            "days during each calendar year."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "LEAVE-001",
            "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "sick leave",
        },
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE THE LANGCHAIN VECTOR STORE
# =========================================================

embedding_model = HuggingFaceEmbeddings(
    model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2",
    encode_kwargs={
        "normalize_embeddings": True,
    },
)

vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)

retriever = vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": 1,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class RetrievalPlanningState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    plan: list[str]
    current_step: int
    retrieved_documents: list[Document]
    completed_steps: list[str]
    answer: str


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE PLANNER NODE
# =========================================================

def planner_node(
    state: RetrievalPlanningState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Create a retrieval plan for the original question.

    This simple example uses rules. In an Agentic RAG system,
    an LLM can generate the same plan as structured output.
    """

    question = state["question"].lower()
    plan = []

    if "hotel" in question or "travel" in question:
        plan.append(
            "Retrieve the current hotel-accommodation policy."
        )

    if (
        "certification" in question
        or "reimbursement" in question
    ):
        plan.append(
            "Retrieve the professional certification "
            "reimbursement policy."
        )

    if "sick leave" in question:
        plan.append(
            "Retrieve the current employee sick-leave policy."
        )

    if not plan:
        plan.append(
            "Search the enterprise knowledge base for information "
            "related to the user's question."
        )

    return {
        "plan": plan,
        "current_step": 0,
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "completed_steps": [],
    }


# =========================================================
# 5. CONVERT A PLAN STEP INTO A SEARCH QUERY
# =========================================================

def create_search_query(plan_step: str) -> str:
    """
    Convert the current plan step into a focused search query.
    """

    step_lower = plan_step.lower()

    if "hotel-accommodation" in step_lower:
        return (
            "maximum hotel accommodation days during approved "
            "business travel and extension approval requirement"
        )

    if "certification" in step_lower:
        return (
            "annual professional certification reimbursement "
            "limit and approval requirement"
        )

    if "sick-leave" in step_lower:
        return (
            "annual paid sick leave entitlement for employees"
        )

    return plan_step


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE THE RETRIEVAL EXECUTOR NODE
# =========================================================

def retrieval_executor_node(
    state: RetrievalPlanningState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Execute the current retrieval step and update graph state.
    """

    current_step_index = state["current_step"]
    current_plan_step = state["plan"][
        current_step_index
    ]

    search_query = create_search_query(
        current_plan_step
    )

    results = retriever.invoke(
        search_query
    )

    updated_documents = (
        state["retrieved_documents"] + results
    )

    updated_completed_steps = (
        state["completed_steps"]
        + [current_plan_step]
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_documents": updated_documents,
        "completed_steps": updated_completed_steps,
        "current_step": current_step_index + 1,
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. CHECK WHETHER MORE PLAN STEPS REMAIN
# =========================================================

def route_after_execution(
    state: RetrievalPlanningState,
) -> str:
    """
    Continue executing until every plan step is completed.
    """

    if state["current_step"] < len(state["plan"]):
        return "execute_next_step"

    return "generate_answer"


# =========================================================
# 8. REMOVE DUPLICATE DOCUMENTS
# =========================================================

def remove_duplicate_documents(
    documents_to_process: list[Document],
) -> list"""
    Remove repeated documents using their metadata ID.
    """

    unique_documents = {}

    for document in documents_to_process:
        document_id = document.metadata["id"]

        if document_id not in unique_documents:
            unique_documents[document_id] = document

    return list(unique_documents.values())


# =========================================================
# 9. CREATE THE FINAL ANSWER NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: RetrievalPlanningState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Generate a simple grounded response from retrieved evidence.

    An LLM can replace this formatting logic in a full RAG system.
    """

    unique_documents = remove_duplicate_documents(
        state["retrieved_documents"]
    )

    if not unique_documents:
        return {
            "answer": (
                "The required information was not found in the "
                "enterprise knowledge base."
            )
        }

    answer_parts = [
        "The retrieval plan produced the following evidence:"
    ]

    for document in unique_documents:
        answer_parts.append(
            "\n".join(
                [
                    "",
                    document.page_content,
                    (
                        "Source: "
                        f"{document.metadata['source']}"
                    ),
                ]
            )
        )

    return {
        "answer": "\n".join(answer_parts),
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(RetrievalPlanningState)

workflow.add_node(
    "create_plan",
    planner_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "execute_step",
    retrieval_executor_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)


# Create the plan first.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "create_plan",
)


# Begin executing plan steps.
workflow.add_edge(
    "create_plan",
    "execute_step",
)


# Continue execution or move to final generation.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "execute_step",
    route_after_execution,
    {
        "execute_next_step": "execute_step",
        "generate_answer": "generate_answer",
    },
)


# End after answer generation.
workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)


# Compile the workflow.
retrieval_planning_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 11. RUN THE RETRIEVAL PLANNING WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

def run_retrieval_plan(question: str) -> None:
    initial_state: RetrievalPlanningState = {
        "question": question,
        "plan": [],
        "current_step": 0,
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "completed_steps": [],
        "answer": "",
    }

    result = retrieval_planning_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("ORIGINAL QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\nRETRIEVAL PLAN")
    print("=" * 70)

    for position, step in enumerate(
        result["plan"],
        start=1,
    ):
        print(f"{position}. {step}")

    print("\nCOMPLETED STEPS")
    print("=" * 70)

    for position, step in enumerate(
        result["completed_steps"],
        start=1,
    ):
        print(f"{position}. {step}")

    print("\nRETRIEVED SOURCES")
    print("=" * 70)

    unique_documents = remove_duplicate_documents(
        result["retrieved_documents"]
    )

    for document in unique_documents:
        print("-", document.metadata["source"])

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["answer"])


# =========================================================
# 12. TEST THE WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    user_question = (
        "Compare the hotel-accommodation allowance with the "
        "professional certification reimbursement policy. "
        "Include the limits and approval requirements."
    )

    run_retrieval_plan(
        user_question
    )

g) Interview Questions & Answers

Q1. What is Retrieval Planning in Agentic RAG?

Retrieval Planning is the process of analyzing a complex question and creating a structured plan that identifies what information must be retrieved, where it should be retrieved from, and in what order the retrieval steps should be executed.

It commonly uses a Planner-Executor architecture. The planner creates tasks, the executor performs them, shared state stores the evidence and progress, and a controller decides whether another step, replanning, or final generation is required.

Q2. How is Retrieval Planning different from Query Decomposition?

Query Decomposition divides a complex question into smaller subquestions. It primarily focuses on breaking the information requirement into more manageable retrieval queries.

Retrieval Planning includes decomposition but goes further by selecting tools, establishing dependencies, ordering tasks, tracking execution, evaluating evidence, and deciding whether the plan should be revised. Decomposition is therefore one activity within Retrieval Planning.

Q3. What is the Planner-Executor pattern?

The Planner-Executor pattern separates high-level task reasoning from low-level execution. The planner analyzes the complete goal and produces an ordered set of tasks, while the executor carries out one task at a time using retrievers, tools, databases, or APIs.

This separation improves modularity and makes execution easier to observe and control. Plan-and-execute agents may also reduce repeated calls to a large model by using the stronger model mainly for planning and smaller components for individual steps.

Q4. When should Retrieval Planning be used?

Retrieval Planning should be used for compound questions, multi-source requests, comparison questions, and tasks containing dependencies among information requirements. It is particularly useful when the appropriate retrieval sequence cannot be represented reliably as one search query.

It may be unnecessary for direct factual questions whose answers exist in one easily retrievable chunk. In those situations, a simple retrieve-and-generate pipeline provides lower latency, lower cost, and easier evaluation.

Q5. What happens when a retrieval plan fails?

The system should identify which step failed and whether the failure resulted from a weak query, incorrect source, missing evidence, or an invalid assumption. It can then rewrite the failing step, select another tool, add a new task, or revise the remaining plan.

Replanning must be bounded by maximum attempts, execution time, and token or cost budgets. If sufficient evidence remains unavailable, the agent should return a transparent insufficient-evidence response instead of inventing missing information.


Topic 4: Tool Calling

a) Definition

* Tool Calling is the mechanism through which an LLM requests the execution of an external function, retriever, database query, API, or computational 
  service. The model does not execute the function itself. It produces a structured tool-call request containing the selected tool name and the input 
  arguments required by that tool.

* Tools extend an LLM beyond its internal parametric knowledge by allowing it to retrieve private information, obtain current data, perform calculations, 
  execute controlled operations, and interact with external systems. In Agentic RAG, a retriever is usually exposed as a tool so the agent can decide when 
  and how to search the knowledge base.

* Tool Calling generally follows a Reason, Act, Observe, and Respond cycle. The LLM analyzes the request and selects a tool, the application executes that 
  tool, the result is returned as a tool message, and the LLM examines the observation before generating an answer or requesting another tool.

* A tool must have a clear name, description, input schema, and output format. The description helps the model understand when to use the tool, while type 
  hints or Pydantic models define the expected arguments and allow the application to validate tool calls before execution.

b) Purpose

* The main purpose of Tool Calling is to connect the LLM with information and capabilities unavailable inside the model’s parameters. For example, an enterprise-policy retriever can provide internal documents, a SQL tool can return structured records, and an API tool can obtain current application status.

* Tool Calling allows the agent to select capabilities dynamically instead of following one fixed retrieval pipeline. A policy question may invoke a vector-search tool, a calculation may invoke a calculator, and a structured employee query may invoke a database tool, depending on the request’s intent.

* Tool schemas create a controlled boundary between probabilistic LLM reasoning and deterministic application execution. The model proposes a function name and arguments, while the application validates and executes the request, preventing the LLM from directly accessing unrestricted databases, file systems, or services.

* Tool Calling also enables multi-step Agentic RAG workflows. The agent can retrieve an entity from one source, use that entity as input to another tool, evaluate both results, and then construct a grounded answer from the combined evidence.

c) Tool Calling Architectural Diagram

                      TOOL CALLING WORKFLOW
                      =====================

                         User Question
                               |
                               v
                      Tool-Enabled LLM
              Understand Request and Available Tools
                               |
                               v
                    Is a Tool Required?
                       /               \
                     No                 Yes
                     |                   |
                     v                   v
              Generate Response    Create Tool Call
                                Tool Name + Arguments
                                         |
                                         v
                                  Validate Request
                                         |
                                         v
                                    ToolNode
                      Execute Retriever / SQL / API
                                         |
                                         v
                                  ToolMessage Result
                                         |
                                         v
                                Tool-Enabled LLM
                      Interpret Result and Decide Next Step
                                /                   \
                         Another Tool             Final Answer
                              |                       |
                              +-------- Loop ---------+

d) Core Components

1. Tool Definition and Input Schema
A tool definition contains a unique name, a precise description, typed arguments, and an output format. LangChain’s @tool decorator creates a tool from a Python function, using the type hints as the input schema and the docstring to help the model understand the tool’s purpose.

2. Tool-Enabled Language Model
The tool-enabled model is created by binding approved tools to a compatible chat model. The model receives each tool’s schema and can produce structured tool-call requests, but the application remains responsible for authorization, argument validation, execution, and error handling.

3. Tool Executor and Tool Messages
The executor maps the requested tool name to an approved callable, validates the arguments, executes the function, and returns its output as a tool observation. LangGraph’s ToolNode provides this execution layer and works with graph state containing conversation messages.

4. Conditional Router and Agent Loop
After the model responds, a router checks whether the message contains tool calls. LangGraph’s tools_condition routes such messages to the tool node; otherwise, it ends the workflow. After execution, the result returns to the agent so it can produce an answer or request another tool.

5. Tool Calling Process

Step 1: Provide Tool Definitions
The application exposes only approved tools to the model. Every tool should have a narrow responsibility and a clear description, such as searching employee policies, retrieving an order, checking service status, or performing a mathematical calculation.

Step 2: Generate a Tool-Call Request
The model analyzes the request and decides whether one of the available tools can provide the required information. It returns a structured request containing the function name and input arguments instead of attempting to fabricate the external result.

Step 3: Execute and Return the Observation
The application validates the tool name, input arguments, user permissions, and operational limits before execution. The tool output is converted into a ToolMessage connected to the original tool-call ID and returned to the model.

Step 4: Continue or Generate
The model examines the tool observation and decides whether sufficient information is available. It may produce a grounded answer, request another tool, or report that the available tools could not provide the required information.

6. Tool Calling vs Related Concepts

Tool Calling vs Function Execution

Tool Calling is the model’s structured request to use a function, while function execution is performed by the application or tool executor. The LLM proposes the action, but it does not directly execute Python code, database queries, or API requests.

Tool Calling vs Retrieval

Retrieval is the specific activity of searching for relevant information. Tool Calling is a broader mechanism through which retrieval, calculation, database access, API requests, or controlled business actions can be requested by the model.

Tool Calling vs Dynamic Retrieval

Dynamic Retrieval decides whether and where to search at runtime. Tool Calling provides the technical mechanism by which the selected retriever or data-source function is invoked and its result returned to the agent.

Tool Calling vs Agent

A tool is a capability available to the system, while an agent manages reasoning and control flow around those capabilities. The agent decides which tool to call, interprets the observation, and determines whether another action or final response is required.

Code Example

import json
from typing import Annotated, TypedDict

from langchain_core.documents import Document
from langchain_core.messages import (
    BaseMessage,
    HumanMessage,
    SystemMessage,
)
from langchain_core.tools import tool

from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbeddings
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI

from langgraph.graph import START, StateGraph
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages
from langgraph.prebuilt import ToolNode, tools_condition


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE ENTERPRISE POLICY DOCUMENTS
# =========================================================

documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees travelling for approved business purposes "
            "can claim hotel accommodation for a maximum of seven "
            "consecutive days. Any extension requires written "
            "approval from the project manager."
        ),
        metadata={
            "source": "travel_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "travel accommodation",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
            "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
            "registration."
        ),
        metadata={
            "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "certification reimbursement",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
            "days during each calendar year. Unused sick leave "
            "cannot be carried forward."
        ),
        metadata={
            "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "sick leave",
        },
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE THE EMBEDDING MODEL AND VECTOR STORE
# =========================================================

embedding_model = HuggingFaceEmbeddings(
    model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2",
    encode_kwargs={
        "normalize_embeddings": True,
    },
)

vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)

retriever = vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": 2,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE THE POLICY-SEARCH TOOL
# =========================================================

@tool
def search_policy(query: str) -> str:
    """
    Search the enterprise policy knowledge base.

    Use this tool for questions about business travel,
    hotel accommodation, professional certification
    reimbursement, and employee sick leave.
    """

    retrieved_documents = retriever.invoke(query)

    if not retrieved_documents:
        return json.dumps(
            {
                "status": "not_found",
                "results": [],
            }
        )

    results = []

    for document in retrieved_documents:
        results.append(
            {
                "source": document.metadata["source"],
                "topic": document.metadata["topic"],
                "content": document.page_content,
            }
        )

    return json.dumps(
        {
            "status": "success",
            "results": results,
        },
        indent=2,
    )


# =========================================================
# 4. DEFINE A CALCULATOR TOOL
# =========================================================

@tool
def calculate(first_number: float, second_number: float) -> float:
    """
    Multiply two numerical values.

    Use this tool when the user asks for multiplication
    involving two numbers.
    """

    return first_number * second_number


# =========================================================
# 5. DEFINE AVAILABLE TOOLS
# =========================================================

tools = [
    search_policy,
    calculate,
]


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE A TOOL-ENABLED CHAT MODEL
# =========================================================

llm = ChatOpenAI(
    model="gpt-4.1-mini",
    temperature=0,
)

llm_with_tools = llm.bind_tools(
    tools
)


# =========================================================
# 7. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class ToolCallingState(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[
        list[BaseMessage],
        add_messages,
    ]


# =========================================================
# 8. CREATE THE AGENT NODE
# =========================================================

SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION = """
You are an enterprise assistant with access to tools.

Rules:
1. Use search_policy for questions about internal policies.
2. Use calculate for multiplication requests.
3. Do not invent policy content or calculation results.
4. After receiving a tool result, answer the user's question.
5. Cite the source returned by the policy-search tool.
6. If a tool reports that information was not found, say so.
""".strip()


def agent_node(
    state: ToolCallingState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Ask the model to either call a tool or provide
    a final response.
    """

    messages_for_model = [
        SystemMessage(
            content=SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION
        ),
        *state["messages"],
    ]

    response = llm_with_tools.invoke(
        messages_for_model
    )

    return {
        "messages": [response],
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. CREATE THE TOOL-EXECUTION NODE
# =========================================================

tool_node = ToolNode(
    tools=tools,
    handle_tool_errors=True,
)


# =========================================================
# 10. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(
    ToolCallingState
)

workflow.add_node(
    "agent",
    agent_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "tools",
    tool_node,
)


# The model runs first.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "agent",
)


# If the model requests a tool, execute it.
# If no tool call exists, the graph ends.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "agent",
    tools_condition,
)


# After tool execution, return the observation
# to the agent so it can answer or call another tool.
workflow.add_edge(
    "tools",
    "agent",
)


# Compile the graph.
tool_calling_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 11. RUN THE TOOL-CALLING GRAPH
# =========================================================

def run_agent(
    question: str,
) -> None:
    result = tool_calling_graph.invoke(
        {
            "messages": [
                HumanMessage(
                    content=question
                )
            ]
        }
    )

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(question)

    print("\nMESSAGE TRACE")
    print("=" * 70)

    for index, message in enumerate(
        result["messages"],
        start=1,
    ):
        print(
            f"\n{index}. {type(message).__name__}"
        )

        if getattr(message, "tool_calls", None):
            print("Tool calls:")
            print(
                json.dumps(
                    message.tool_calls,
                    indent=2,
                )
            )

        if message.content:
            print("Content:")
            print(message.content)

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(
        result["messages"][-1].content
    )


# =========================================================
# 12. TEST THE AVAILABLE TOOLS
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_agent(
        "What is the annual professional certification "
        "reimbursement limit, and what approval is required?"
    )

    run_agent(
        "Multiply 25000 by 3."
    )

Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Tool Calling in Agentic RAG?

Tool Calling is the mechanism by which an LLM produces a structured request to invoke an external function or service. The request identifies the tool and supplies its input arguments, while the application validates and executes the operation.

In Agentic RAG, retrieval systems are commonly exposed as tools. The agent can call a vector-search tool, SQL tool, API tool, or another information source, observe the result, and use that evidence to generate a grounded response.

Q2. Does the LLM execute the tool directly?

No. The LLM only generates a structured tool-call request containing the intended function name and arguments. The application or a framework component such as LangGraph’s ToolNode performs the actual execution.

This separation is important for validation and security. The application can reject unsupported tools, invalid parameters, unauthorized actions, excessive calls, or requests that violate operational policies before any external system is accessed.

Q3. What are ToolNode and tools_condition in LangGraph?

ToolNode is a prebuilt LangGraph component that executes tool calls found in the model’s message and returns the results as tool observations. It maps approved function names to their implementations and can manage execution errors and multiple calls.

tools_condition is a routing function that checks whether the latest AI message contains tool calls. If tool calls exist, it routes the graph to the tool node; otherwise, it routes to the graph’s end because the model has produced a normal response.

Q4. How does the model decide which tool to call?

The model receives the tools’ names, descriptions, and argument schemas when the tools are bound. It compares the user’s request with these definitions and selects the tool whose description best matches the required action.

Tool descriptions must therefore be narrow and explicit. Overlapping or vague descriptions can confuse the model, while well-defined names, typed arguments, examples, and deterministic rules improve selection reliability.

Q5. How should Tool Calling be secured?

Tools should expose only the minimum required capability and independently verify the user’s identity, role, tenant, and permissions. Sensitive database operations should use parameterized queries and approved schemas rather than accepting unrestricted SQL from the model.

The application should also validate inputs, restrict tool names, enforce timeouts and rate limits, log executions, redact sensitive outputs, require approval for consequential actions, and limit the number of agent iterations.


Topic 5: Reflection

a) Definition

* Reflection is an Agentic AI technique in which an agent evaluates its retrieved evidence, reasoning process, tool observations, or generated response before accepting it as final. The agent acts as a reviewer of its own work and identifies problems such as missing details, unsupported claims, weak citations, or incomplete answers.

* A reflection workflow commonly contains two roles: a generator that creates the first answer and a reflector or critic that evaluates its quality. If the critic finds problems, its feedback is returned to the generator so that a revised answer can be produced. This cycle continues until the answer passes evaluation or reaches a fixed iteration limit.

* In Agentic RAG, reflection can be applied to the retrieved documents or to the generated answer. Retrieval reflection asks whether the evidence is relevant and sufficient, while response reflection asks whether the answer is accurate, complete, grounded, clearly written, and properly supported by the retrieved sources.

* Reflection does not automatically guarantee that the revised answer is better because the critic can also make mistakes. Reliable workflows combine LLM-based reflection with deterministic validations, source checks, structured evaluation criteria, retry limits, and, for sensitive tasks, human review.

b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of reflection is to prevent the agent from returning the first generated answer without evaluating its quality. A response can be 
  fluent but still omit one part of the question, misinterpret the retrieved evidence, cite the wrong document, or introduce unsupported information.

* Reflection helps detect whether an answer is grounded in the retrieved context. The critic can compare individual claims with the available evidence and 
  identify sentences that are unsupported, contradictory, or based only on the model’s internal knowledge rather than on the retrieved documents.

* Reflection also supports completeness checks for compound questions. If the user asks about a reimbursement limit and an approval condition, the critic 
  can verify that the answer covers both requirements instead of discussing only the part that was easiest to retrieve.

* Reflection is most useful when response quality is more important than minimum latency, such as enterprise analysis, research, document comparison, and 
  complex knowledge tasks. It adds at least one extra evaluation and often another generation call, so it may be unnecessary for straightforward, low-risk 
  questions.

c) Architecture Diagram

                    REFLECTION WORKFLOW
                    ===================

                       User Question
                             |
                             v
                      Retrieve Evidence
                             |
                             v
                     Generate Draft Answer
                             |
                             v
                       Reflection Agent
       Check Accuracy | Completeness | Grounding | Citations
                             |
                             v
                       Does It Pass?
                        /          \
                      Yes           No
                       |             |
                       v             v
                 Final Answer     Feedback
                                     |
                                     v
                              Revise the Answer
                                     |
                                     v
                              Reflection Again
                                     |
                              Maximum Iterations
                                     |
                                     v
                             Best Available Answer


d) Core Components

1. Initial Generator
The generator creates the first answer from the original question and retrieved evidence. It should follow grounding instructions, preserve factual values, address all parts of the request, and include source citations so the reflection agent has a clear response to evaluate.

2. Reflection Agent or Critic
The critic evaluates the draft using predefined quality criteria and produces a structured verdict. It should identify specific problems rather than giving vague feedback, such as stating that the answer omitted an approval requirement or included a claim unsupported by the evidence.

3. Revision Component
The revision component regenerates the answer using the critic’s feedback. It should preserve correct parts of the earlier response, correct only identified weaknesses, and rely on the same verified evidence unless the reflection stage explicitly requests additional retrieval.

4. Controller and Stopping Condition
The controller decides whether the response should be accepted, revised, or escalated. It tracks the reflection count and stops when the response passes, the maximum number of iterations is reached, or the available evidence is insufficient for further improvement.

5. Reflection Criteria in RAG

Accuracy and Grounding
The critic checks whether every important claim is supported by the retrieved evidence and whether factual values have been copied correctly. Numerical amounts, durations, names, dates, policy versions, exceptions, and approval conditions require particular attention.

Completeness and Relevance
The critic checks whether the response answers every important part of the user’s question without introducing unrelated details. A response can be factually correct but still fail evaluation if it addresses only one requirement from a multi-part question.

Citation Correctness
The critic verifies that cited documents were actually retrieved and that each source supports the associated claim. Mentioning a real document name is not sufficient if that document does not contain the information claimed in the answer.

Clarity and Instruction Compliance
The critic evaluates whether the response is understandable, appropriately structured, and compliant with the system instructions. Reflection architectures commonly evaluate qualities such as accuracy, completeness, clarity, helpfulness, and safety.

6. Reflection vs Related Concepts

Reflection vs Evidence Grading
Evidence grading evaluates whether the retrieved documents are relevant and sufficient before answer generation. Reflection evaluates the generated answer or completed action after the model has used the evidence, though a workflow may apply reflection at both stages.

Reflection vs Self-Correction
Reflection identifies and explains problems in the current output. Self-Correction performs the action needed to resolve those problems, such as revising the answer, rewriting the query, retrieving additional evidence, or selecting another tool.

Reflection vs Re-Ranking
Re-Ranking evaluates retrieved documents and reorders them according to relevance. Reflection evaluates the quality of the agent’s answer, reasoning outcome, or actions after those documents have been processed.

Reflection vs Human Review
Reflection uses an LLM or automated evaluator to review output quickly and at scale. Human review provides stronger judgement for ambiguous or high-impact decisions but is slower and more expensive, so workflows may escalate only failed or uncertain cases.

e) Code Example

from typing import Literal, TypedDict

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. CONFIGURATION
# =========================================================

MAX_REFLECTIONS = 2

generator_llm = ChatOpenAI(
    model="gpt-4.1-mini",
    temperature=0,
)

critic_llm = ChatOpenAI(
    model="gpt-4.1-mini",
    temperature=0,
)


# =========================================================
# 2. SAMPLE RETRIEVED EVIDENCE
# =========================================================

retrieved_evidence = """
Source: certification_policy_2026.txt

Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 rupees per
financial year for approved professional certifications.
Manager approval is required before registration.

Source: certification_claim_process_2026.txt

After completing the certification, the employee must submit
proof of payment and the completion certificate within thirty days.
""".strip()


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE STRUCTURED REFLECTION OUTPUT
# =========================================================

class ReflectionResult(BaseModel):
    passed: bool = Field(
        description=(
            "True only when the answer is accurate, complete, "
            "grounded in evidence, and properly cited."
        )
    )

    feedback: str = Field(
        description=(
            "Specific correction instructions when the answer "
            "does not pass. Return a short approval message "
            "when the answer passes."
        )
    )


# =========================================================
# 4. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class ReflectionState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    evidence: str

    draft_answer: str
    reflection_passed: bool
    reflection_feedback: str

    reflection_count: int
    max_reflections: int

    final_answer: str


# =========================================================
# 5. INITIAL GENERATION CHAIN
# =========================================================

generation_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You are an enterprise policy assistant.

Answer only from the retrieved evidence.

Rules:
1. Answer every part of the question.
2. Preserve all amounts, time limits, and conditions.
3. Do not introduce unsupported information.
4. Cite the source documents.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Question:
{question}

Retrieved evidence:
{evidence}
""",
        ),
    ]
)

generation_chain = (
    generation_prompt
    | generator_llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)


def generate_draft_node(
    state: ReflectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Generate the first answer from the retrieved evidence.
    """

    draft = generation_chain.invoke(
        {
            "question": state["question"],
            "evidence": state["evidence"],
        }
    )

    return {
        "draft_answer": draft,
    }


# =========================================================
# 6. REFLECTION CHAIN
# =========================================================

reflection_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You are a strict evaluator of enterprise RAG answers.

Evaluate the draft using these criteria:

1. Accuracy:
   Are all factual values copied correctly?

2. Grounding:
   Is every important claim supported by the evidence?

3. Completeness:
   Does the answer address every part of the question?

4. Citations:
   Do the cited sources support the claims?

5. Clarity:
   Is the answer clear and understandable?

Set passed=true only if all criteria are satisfied.

If it fails, provide specific and actionable feedback.
Do not rewrite the answer yourself.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Original question:
{question}

Retrieved evidence:
{evidence}

Draft answer:
{draft_answer}
""",
        ),
    ]
)

reflection_chain = (
    reflection_prompt
    | critic_llm.with_structured_output(
        ReflectionResult
    )
)


def reflect_node(
    state: ReflectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Evaluate the current draft and return a pass/fail result
    with actionable feedback.
    """

    evaluation = reflection_chain.invoke(
        {
            "question": state["question"],
            "evidence": state["evidence"],
            "draft_answer": state["draft_answer"],
        }
    )

    return {
        "reflection_passed": evaluation.passed,
        "reflection_feedback": evaluation.feedback,
        "reflection_count": (
            state["reflection_count"] + 1
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. ROUTE AFTER REFLECTION
# =========================================================

def route_after_reflection(
    state: ReflectionState,
) -> Literal["finalize", "revise"]:
    """
    Accept the answer if it passes or if the workflow has
    reached the maximum permitted reflection count.
    """

    if state["reflection_passed"]:
        return "finalize"

    if (
        state["reflection_count"]
        >= state["max_reflections"]
    ):
        return "finalize"

    return "revise"


# =========================================================
# 8. REVISION CHAIN
# =========================================================

revision_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages(
    [
        (
            "system",
            """
You revise enterprise RAG answers using critic feedback.

Rules:
1. Use only the supplied evidence.
2. Correct every problem identified in the feedback.
3. Preserve already-correct information.
4. Answer every part of the original question.
5. Preserve amounts, deadlines, and approval conditions.
6. Cite only sources present in the evidence.
""",
        ),
        (
            "human",
            """
Original question:
{question}

Retrieved evidence:
{evidence}

Previous draft:
{draft_answer}

Critic feedback:
{feedback}

Write the improved answer.
""",
        ),
    ]
)

revision_chain = (
    revision_prompt
    | generator_llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)


def revise_answer_node(
    state: ReflectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Revise the draft according to the critic's feedback.
    """

    revised_answer = revision_chain.invoke(
        {
            "question": state["question"],
            "evidence": state["evidence"],
            "draft_answer": state["draft_answer"],
            "feedback": state["reflection_feedback"],
        }
    )

    return {
        "draft_answer": revised_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. FINALIZATION NODE
# =========================================================

def finalize_answer_node(
    state: ReflectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Store the current draft as the final answer.
    """

    return {
        "final_answer": state["draft_answer"],
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(ReflectionState)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_draft",
    generate_draft_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "reflect",
    reflect_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "revise",
    revise_answer_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "finalize",
    finalize_answer_node,
)


# First create a draft.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "generate_draft",
)


# Evaluate the generated draft.
workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_draft",
    "reflect",
)


# Accept or revise based on the critic.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "reflect",
    route_after_reflection,
    {
        "finalize": "finalize",
        "revise": "revise",
    },
)


# Evaluate every revised answer again.
workflow.add_edge(
    "revise",
    "reflect",
)


# End after finalization.
workflow.add_edge(
    "finalize",
    END,
)


# Compile the reflection graph.
reflection_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 11. RUN THE REFLECTION WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

def run_reflection_workflow(
    question: str,
) -> ReflectionState:
    initial_state: ReflectionState = {
        "question": question,
        "evidence": retrieved_evidence,

        "draft_answer": "",
        "reflection_passed": False,
        "reflection_feedback": "",

        "reflection_count": 0,
        "max_reflections": MAX_REFLECTIONS,

        "final_answer": "",
    }

    return reflection_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )


# =========================================================
# 12. DISPLAY THE RESULT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    user_question = (
        "What is the annual certification reimbursement limit, "
        "what approval is required, and which documents must be "
        "submitted after completion?"
    )

    result = run_reflection_workflow(
        user_question
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("REFLECTION COUNT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["reflection_count"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("REFLECTION PASSED")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["reflection_passed"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("LATEST REFLECTION FEEDBACK")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["reflection_feedback"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("FINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])

f). Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Reflection in Agentic RAG?

Reflection is a quality-control process in which an agent evaluates its retrieved evidence, actions, or generated response before accepting the result as final. A critic checks whether the draft is accurate, complete, relevant, grounded, clearly written, and properly cited.

If problems are identified, the critic returns feedback to the generator, which creates a revised answer. The workflow can repeat until the response passes evaluation or reaches a configured reflection limit.

Q2. What is the difference between Reflection and Self-Correction?

Reflection is the evaluation step that identifies weaknesses in an answer, retrieval result, or agent action. It produces a judgement and specific feedback, such as noting that the answer omitted an approval requirement or added an unsupported claim.

Self-Correction is the action taken in response to that feedback. It may revise the answer, rewrite the retrieval query, call another tool, retrieve additional documents, or use a different reasoning strategy.

Q3. Should the generator and critic use the same LLM?

They can use the same LLM with different prompts, which keeps the implementation simple and reduces integration complexity. However, the critic may reproduce the same assumptions or biases that caused the generator’s original mistake.

Using a separate evaluator model, deterministic validation, or task-specific checks can provide more independent feedback. The decision should consider quality requirements, latency, model cost, and availability.

Q4. When should Reflection be used?

Reflection is useful for complex, knowledge-intensive, multi-part, or evidence-sensitive tasks where answer quality is more important than minimum latency. It is especially valuable when responses require accurate citations, complete evidence coverage, or adherence to detailed instructions.

It may be unnecessary for greetings, basic lookups, simple calculations, or low-risk tasks where the result can be validated directly. Applying reflection to every request can create unnecessary cost and response delay.

Q5. How do you prevent an infinite reflection loop?

The workflow should maintain a reflection counter and stop when the response passes or reaches a fixed maximum number of iterations. It can also stop when consecutive revisions show no meaningful improvement or when the critic repeatedly returns the same feedback.

After reaching the limit, the application should return the best grounded response with an appropriate warning or escalate it for human review. LangGraph’s conditional edges and state make these termination rules explicit and observable.

Topic 6: Self-Correction

a) Definition

* Self-Correction is an Agentic RAG capability in which the system detects a problem in retrieval or generation and automatically takes corrective action. Instead of returning an answer from weak evidence, the agent can rewrite the query, retrieve again, remove irrelevant documents, switch sources, or revise the generated response.

* Self-Correction is implemented as a controlled feedback loop. The system retrieves evidence, evaluates its relevance, generates an answer, and validates whether the answer is grounded and complete. If a check fails, the workflow returns to the appropriate earlier stage rather than always restarting the entire pipeline.

* Correction can happen at two main levels: retrieval correction and generation correction. Retrieval correction improves the query or evidence collection, while generation correction revises an answer that contains unsupported claims, missing details, incorrect citations, or inaccurate values.

* Self-Corrective RAG is closely related to Corrective RAG and Self-Reflective RAG. These architectures use evaluators, query rewriting, repeated retrieval, and response validation to correct poor-quality retrieval or generation before returning the final answer.


b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Self-Correction is to prevent silent failure in a RAG pipeline. Vector similarity may return topically related but unusable documents, and the LLM may still generate a confident response. A correction loop identifies this situation and avoids answering from unreliable evidence.

* Self-Correction improves retrieval when the original query does not align well with the indexed documents. If the document grader rejects the initial results, the system can rewrite an informal or ambiguous question into a more explicit search query and attempt retrieval again.

* It also improves generation quality by checking whether the answer is supported by the retrieved evidence. When the answer includes unsupported statements or ignores important conditions, the system can revise the response using evaluator feedback without changing the underlying source facts.

* Self-Correction is valuable for complex and evidence-sensitive tasks, but it increases latency, model usage, and workflow complexity. Every correction loop should therefore have a maximum attempt count, token budget, timeout, and transparent insufficient-evidence fallback.

c) Self-Correction Architecture Diagram

                    SELF-CORRECTIVE RAG
                    ===================

                       User Question
                             |
                             v
                      Retrieve Documents
                             |
                             v
                  Grade Document Relevance
                        /          \
                  Relevant       Irrelevant
                     |               |
                     v               v
              Generate Answer    Rewrite Query
                     |               |
                     |        Retrieve Again
                     |               |
                     +------<--------+
                     |
                     v
                 Validate Answer
           Grounded? Complete? Relevant?
                  /              \
                Yes               No
                 |                 |
                 v                 v
           Final Answer       Correct Answer
                                   |
                            Validate Again
                                   |
                          Maximum Attempts?
                            /            \
                          No              Yes
                          |                |
                          +---- Loop    Return Best
                                      Grounded Answer

d) Core Components

1. Document Relevance Grader
The document grader determines whether retrieved chunks are related to the question and contain usable information. It commonly produces a structured result such as relevant=true/false, allowing LangGraph to route the workflow predictably toward generation or query correction.

2. Query Rewriter and Retrieval Loop
The query rewriter receives the original question and the reason retrieval failed. It generates a clearer search query while preserving names, dates, identifiers, numbers, and negations, after which the retriever performs another search using the improved representation.

3. Answer Validator and Revision Component
The answer validator compares the generated response with both the original question and retrieved evidence. If unsupported claims, missing conditions, or citation errors are detected, the revision component rewrites the answer using the validator’s specific feedback.

4. Controller and Correction Limits
The controller decides which correction path should be followed and tracks the number of attempts. It prevents infinite loops by enforcing maximum retrieval rewrites, answer revisions, model calls, execution time, and token consumption.

5. Types of Self-Correction

Retrieval Correction
Retrieval correction is triggered when the system does not find relevant or sufficient evidence. Corrective actions include Query Rewriting, Query Expansion, increased Top-K, Hybrid Search, Metadata Filtering, source switching, or retrieval from an additional knowledge base.

Context Correction
Context correction improves the documents selected for prompt construction. The system may remove irrelevant chunks, filter archived documents, Re-Rank the candidates, compress long passages, eliminate duplicates, or add missing evidence for another part of the question.

Generation Correction
Generation correction is triggered when sufficient evidence exists but the generated answer is inaccurate, incomplete, or unsupported. The model revises its response using the original evidence and evaluator feedback rather than inventing additional information.

Tool-Selection Correction
Tool-selection correction occurs when the agent chooses an inappropriate source or function. For example, the workflow may switch from vector retrieval to SQL when the question requires exact structured records rather than natural-language policy documents.

6. Reflection vs Self-Correction

Reflection identifies the problem, while Self-Correction performs the action needed to resolve it. A reflection node may report that the answer omitted an approval condition, while a correction node revises the answer and includes the missing condition from the evidence.

Reflection generally produces a quality verdict and feedback. Self-Correction uses that feedback to decide whether to rewrite the query, retrieve again, switch sources, remove weak context, or regenerate the answer.

Reflection can exist without correction when the system only reports quality issues. Self-Correction requires an executable feedback path that changes the pipeline state and produces another retrieval result or answer.

Both processes should be bounded and observable. Repeated reflection and correction do not guarantee continuous improvement, so the system should stop when the answer passes, attempts are exhausted, or no sufficient evidence can be found.

e) Code Example

from typing import Literal, TypedDict

from langchain_core.documents import Document
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbeddings

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE SAMPLE KNOWLEDGE-BASE DOCUMENTS
# =========================================================

documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
            "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
            "registration."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "CERT-001",
            "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "certification",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees travelling for approved business purposes "
            "can claim hotel accommodation for a maximum of seven "
            "consecutive days. Extensions require written approval "
            "from the project manager."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "TRAVEL-001",
            "source": "travel_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "travel",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
            "days during each calendar year. Unused sick leave "
            "cannot be carried forward."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "LEAVE-001",
            "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
            "topic": "leave",
        },
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE THE EMBEDDING MODEL
# =========================================================

embedding_model = HuggingFaceEmbeddings(
    model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2",
    encode_kwargs={
        "normalize_embeddings": True,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 3. CREATE THE FAISS VECTOR STORE
# =========================================================

vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE LANGCHAIN RETRIEVER
# =========================================================

retriever = vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": 1,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 5. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class SelfCorrectionState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    search_query: str

    retrieved_documents: list[Document]
    expected_topic: str
    retrieval_is_relevant: bool

    correction_count: int
    max_corrections: int

    answer: str


# =========================================================
# 6. IDENTIFY THE EXPECTED TOPIC
# =========================================================

def identify_expected_topic(question: str) -> str:
    """
    Identify which business topic the original question concerns.
    """

    normalized_question = question.lower()

    certification_terms = [
        "training refund",
        "course refund",
        "certification",
        "certification reimbursement",
    ]

    travel_terms = [
        "hotel",
        "business travel",
        "accommodation",
    ]

    leave_terms = [
        "sick leave",
        "medical leave",
        "medical off",
    ]

    if any(
        term in normalized_question
        for term in certification_terms
    ):
        return "certification"

    if any(
        term in normalized_question
        for term in travel_terms
    ):
        return "travel"

    if any(
        term in normalized_question
        for term in leave_terms
    ):
        return "leave"

    return "unknown"


# =========================================================
# 7. INITIALIZATION NODE
# =========================================================

def initialize_node(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Initialize the search query and expected topic.
    """

    return {
        "search_query": state["question"],
        "expected_topic": identify_expected_topic(
            state["question"]
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 8. RETRIEVAL NODE
# =========================================================

def retrieve_node(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve the most relevant document using the current query.
    """

    retrieved_documents = retriever.invoke(
        state["search_query"]
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_documents": retrieved_documents,
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. DOCUMENT-GRADING NODE
# =========================================================

def grade_documents_node(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Check whether the retrieved document matches the topic
    expected from the original question.
    """

    expected_topic = state["expected_topic"]
    retrieved_documents = state["retrieved_documents"]

    retrieval_is_relevant = any(
        document.metadata.get("topic") == expected_topic
        for document in retrieved_documents
    )

    return {
        "retrieval_is_relevant": retrieval_is_relevant,
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. CONDITIONAL ROUTING AFTER GRADING
# =========================================================

def route_after_grading(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> Literal[
    "generate_answer",
    "rewrite_query",
    "not_found",
]:
    """
    Decide whether to generate, correct the query, or stop.
    """

    if state["retrieval_is_relevant"]:
        return "generate_answer"

    if (
        state["correction_count"]
        < state["max_corrections"]
    ):
        return "rewrite_query"

    return "not_found"


# =========================================================
# 11. QUERY-REWRITING NODE
# =========================================================

def rewrite_query_node(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Replace informal words with terminology used by the
    enterprise knowledge base.
    """

    current_query = state["search_query"].lower()

    replacements = {
        "training refund": (
            "professional certification reimbursement"
        ),
        "course refund": (
            "professional certification reimbursement"
        ),
        "hotel stay limit": (
            "maximum hotel accommodation days"
        ),
        "medical off": (
            "paid sick leave"
        ),
    }

    rewritten_query = current_query

    for original_term, replacement_term in replacements.items():
        rewritten_query = rewritten_query.replace(
            original_term,
            replacement_term,
        )

    return {
        "search_query": rewritten_query,
        "correction_count": (
            state["correction_count"] + 1
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 12. ANSWER-GENERATION NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Construct an answer using only the accepted document.
    """

    relevant_documents = [
        document
        for document in state["retrieved_documents"]
        if (
            document.metadata.get("topic")
            == state["expected_topic"]
        )
    ]

    if not relevant_documents:
        return {
            "answer": (
                "Relevant evidence was not available."
            )
        }

    selected_document = relevant_documents[0]

    grounded_answer = (
        f"{selected_document.page_content}\n\n"
        f"Source: "
        f"{selected_document.metadata['source']}"
    )

    return {
        "answer": grounded_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 13. NOT-FOUND NODE
# =========================================================

def not_found_node(
    state: SelfCorrectionState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Return a safe response after correction attempts fail.
    """

    return {
        "answer": (
            "The required information could not be found after "
            f"{state['correction_count']} correction attempt(s)."
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 14. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(SelfCorrectionState)

workflow.add_node(
    "initialize",
    initialize_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "retrieve",
    retrieve_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "grade_documents",
    grade_documents_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "rewrite_query",
    rewrite_query_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "not_found",
    not_found_node,
)


# Entry point.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "initialize",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "initialize",
    "retrieve",
)


# Retrieval result is always graded.
workflow.add_edge(
    "retrieve",
    "grade_documents",
)


# Route according to evidence quality.
workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    "grade_documents",
    route_after_grading,
    {
