        "generate_answer": "generate_answer",
        "rewrite_query": "rewrite_query",
        "not_found": "not_found",
    },
)


# Self-correction loop.
workflow.add_edge(
    "rewrite_query",
    "retrieve",
)


# Terminal paths.
workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "not_found",
    END,
)


# Compile the graph.
self_correction_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 15. FUNCTION TO RUN THE GRAPH
# =========================================================

def run_self_correction(question: str) -> None:
    initial_state: SelfCorrectionState = {
        "question": question,
        "search_query": "",
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "expected_topic": "",
        "retrieval_is_relevant": False,
        "correction_count": 0,
        "max_corrections": 2,
        "answer": "",
    }

    result = self_correction_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("ORIGINAL QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\nEXPECTED TOPIC")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["expected_topic"])

    print("\nFINAL SEARCH QUERY")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["search_query"])

    print("\nCORRECTION COUNT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["correction_count"])

    print("\nRETRIEVAL IS RELEVANT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["retrieval_is_relevant"])

    print("\nRETRIEVED SOURCE")
    print("=" * 70)

    for document in result["retrieved_documents"]:
        print(document.metadata["source"])

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["answer"])


# =========================================================
# 16. RUN THE PROGRAM
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_self_correction(
        "What is the annual training refund limit?"
    )

f) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Self-Correction in Agentic RAG?

Self-Correction is the ability of an Agentic RAG system to detect a retrieval or generation failure and automatically perform an appropriate corrective action. The action may include rewriting the query, retrieving again, switching tools, filtering documents, or revising the answer.

The process is implemented as a feedback loop containing evaluation, correction, and re-evaluation. The loop continues until the result passes validation or reaches its configured maximum number of attempts.

Q2. What is the difference between Reflection and Self-Correction?

Reflection evaluates the current evidence, answer, or agent action and identifies specific quality problems. It produces a verdict and feedback but does not necessarily modify the workflow output.

Self-Correction uses that verdict to perform an action. It may rewrite the query, change the retrieval strategy, retrieve additional documents, remove weak context, or revise the generated response.

Q3. What failures can Self-Correction handle?

It can handle vocabulary mismatch, irrelevant retrieval, incomplete context, poor document ranking, wrong tool selection, unsupported answer claims, missing answer components, and incorrect citations. The correction method should match the failure type.

It cannot reliably fix missing source information unless another approved source is available. It also cannot guarantee improvement if the evaluator or correction model produces inaccurate decisions.

Q4. How do you prevent infinite Self-Correction loops?

The graph should maintain separate counters for query rewrites, retrieval attempts, tool calls, and answer revisions. It should stop after a fixed number of attempts, a timeout, token-budget exhaustion, or repeated retrieval of the same evidence.

If the limit is reached, the system should return the best grounded result or clearly state that sufficient evidence could not be found. It should not generate unsupported information merely to avoid returning a failure.

Q5. What is Corrective RAG?

Corrective RAG is an architecture that evaluates retrieval quality and takes corrective action before generation. If retrieved documents are relevant, they are used normally; if they are ambiguous or irrelevant, the system may filter them, rewrite the query, repeat retrieval, or supplement evidence from another source.

Corrective RAG and Self-Reflective RAG use feedback loops to improve retrieval and generation quality. LangGraph is suitable for these patterns because state machines naturally support evaluation nodes, conditional paths, correction loops, and bounded retries.

Topic 7: Multi-Agent RAG

a) Definition

Multi-Agent RAG is an Agentic RAG architecture in which multiple specialized agents collaborate to retrieve, evaluate, and synthesize information. Instead of assigning every responsibility to one general-purpose agent, the workflow separates tasks such as planning, policy retrieval, technical retrieval, evidence validation, and final answer generation.

Each agent can have its own instructions, retrieval tools, knowledge source, model, and output schema. For example, an HR agent may search employee policies, an IT agent may search technical documentation, and a supervisor agent may decide which specialist should handle each part of the user’s question.

The agents communicate through a shared state or structured messages. One agent’s output becomes input for another agent, allowing evidence gathered by several specialists to be combined into one grounded answer. In LangGraph, agents can be represented as graph nodes and their communication paths as edges.

Multi-Agent RAG does not mean that several agents should be used for every request. It is most useful when the application contains multiple knowledge domains, large tool collections, complex questions, or tasks that benefit from specialization and parallel evidence collection.

b) Purpose

The main purpose of Multi-Agent RAG is to divide a complex retrieval task into smaller responsibilities that can be handled by specialized agents. A focused agent is generally easier to instruct, evaluate, and improve than one agent that must understand every domain and choose from dozens of unrelated tools.

Multi-Agent RAG improves source specialization by connecting each agent to the most appropriate knowledge base. An HR agent can retrieve leave and reimbursement policies, while an IT agent can retrieve authentication and troubleshooting documentation without searching unrelated collections.

The architecture can improve evidence coverage for compound questions. If a user asks about certification reimbursement and MFA registration, a supervisor can send the two requirements to different agents and combine their evidence after both specialists have completed retrieval.

Multi-agent systems introduce coordination overhead, additional model calls, and more failure points. The architecture therefore requires clear role definitions, structured communication, shared-state management, source citations, conflict resolution, and explicit workflow termination.

c) Architectural Diagram

                     MULTI-AGENT RAG ARCHITECTURE
                     ============================

                           User Question
                                 |
                                 v
                         Supervisor Agent
               Analyze Intent and Divide the Task
                                 |
                    +------------+------------+
                    |                         |
                    v                         v
               HR/Policy Agent           IT Support Agent
                    |                         |
                    v                         v
             HR Knowledge Base          IT Knowledge Base
          Leave | Travel | Learning    MFA | Access | Errors
                    |                         |
                    v                         v
              Policy Evidence           Technical Evidence
                    |                         |
                    +------------+------------+
                                 |
                                 v
                       Evidence Aggregator
                 Merge | Deduplicate | Resolve Conflicts
                                 |
                                 v
                        Reviewer or Critic
                  Relevance | Grounding | Completeness
                                 |
                          +------+------+
                          |             |
                        Pass           Fail
                          |             |
                          v             v
                    Final Generator   Retrieve Again
                          |
                          v
                Grounded Answer with Citations

d) Core Components

1. Supervisor Agent
The supervisor analyzes the user’s request, decomposes compound questions, and routes each requirement to the appropriate specialist. It should coordinate the workflow rather than directly answer domain questions without consulting the assigned knowledge sources.

2. Specialized Retrieval Agents
Every retrieval agent has a focused role, limited knowledge source, and clear output format. Specialized agents can use different retrievers, prompts, tools, embedding models, or LLMs according to their domain and information requirements.

3. Shared State and Evidence Aggregator
Shared state stores the original question, routing decision, specialist outputs, retrieved documents, and final response. The aggregator combines evidence from multiple agents, removes duplication, and retains source metadata for answer grounding and citations.

4. Reviewer and Final Generator
The reviewer checks relevance, completeness, contradictions, and citation support before generation. The final generator then answers the original request using only the validated evidence returned by the specialist agents.

5. Common Multi-Agent Patterns

Supervisor Pattern
A central supervisor selects and coordinates specialized agents. This pattern provides clear control and is suitable when the application has known domains such as HR, IT, Finance, Legal, and Product Support.

Network Pattern
Agents communicate directly and select the next agent without always returning to a supervisor. This provides flexibility, but the workflow is harder to control and requires strong termination rules to prevent repeated agent handoffs.

Hierarchical Pattern
A top-level supervisor delegates tasks to lower-level supervisors or specialist teams. This is useful for very large enterprise systems but introduces additional communication, latency, and state-management complexity.

Parallel Specialist Pattern
Multiple independent agents retrieve evidence at the same time, and an aggregator combines their results. Parallel execution can reduce latency for compound questions when the retrieval tasks do not depend on one another.

6. Multi-Agent RAG vs Single-Agent RAG

Single-Agent RAG uses one agent to perform routing, retrieval, tool selection, evaluation, and generation. It is simpler to build and monitor, but its prompt and tool collection can become difficult to manage as the number of domains and capabilities increases.

Multi-Agent RAG assigns different responsibilities to specialized agents. Each specialist receives a smaller prompt and a narrower tool collection, which can improve focus and allow each domain workflow to be tested separately.

A single agent is generally suitable for small applications with one knowledge source and a limited number of tools. Multi-Agent RAG becomes useful when the system must coordinate several domains, sources, security boundaries, or independent retrieval tasks.

Multi-Agent RAG is not automatically more accurate. Poor routing, duplicated retrieval, conflicting evidence, or unclear agent responsibilities can make it less reliable than a well-designed single-agent system.

e) Code Example

from typing import TypedDict

from langchain_core.documents import Document
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbeddings

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE HR KNOWLEDGE-BASE DOCUMENTS
# =========================================================

hr_documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
            "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
            "registering for the certification."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "HR-001",
            "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
            "domain": "HR",
            "topic": "certification reimbursement",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
            "days during each calendar year. Unused sick leave "
            "cannot be carried forward."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "HR-002",
            "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
            "domain": "HR",
            "topic": "sick leave",
        },
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE IT KNOWLEDGE-BASE DOCUMENTS
# =========================================================

it_documents = [
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "Employees replacing their mobile device must "
            "re-register multi-factor authentication through "
            "the security information portal."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "IT-001",
            "source": "mfa_device_guide.txt",
            "domain": "IT",
            "topic": "multi-factor authentication",
        },
    ),
    Document(
        page_content=(
            "A locked employee account can be restored through "
            "the self-service account recovery portal or by "
            "contacting the enterprise service desk."
        ),
        metadata={
            "id": "IT-002",
            "source": "account_unlock_guide.txt",
            "domain": "IT",
            "topic": "account unlock",
        },
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 3. CREATE THE EMBEDDING MODEL
# =========================================================

embedding_model = HuggingFaceEmbeddings(
    model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2",
    encode_kwargs={
        "normalize_embeddings": True,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE SEPARATE VECTOR STORES
# =========================================================

hr_vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=hr_documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)

it_vector_store = FAISS.from_documents(
    documents=it_documents,
    embedding=embedding_model,
)


# =========================================================
# 5. CREATE SPECIALIZED RETRIEVERS
# =========================================================

hr_retriever = hr_vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": 1,
    },
)

it_retriever = it_vector_store.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={
        "k": 1,
    },
)


# =========================================================
# 6. DEFINE THE SHARED LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class MultiAgentRAGState(TypedDict):
    question: str

    use_hr_agent: bool
    use_it_agent: bool

    hr_documents: list[Document]
    it_documents: list[Document]

    combined_evidence: str
    answer: str


# =========================================================
# 7. CREATE THE SUPERVISOR AGENT
# =========================================================

def supervisor_agent(
    state: MultiAgentRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Decide which specialized agents should handle the request.

    A production version may use an LLM with structured output.
    This educational version uses deterministic keyword routing.
    """

    question = state["question"].lower()

    hr_keywords = [
        "certification",
        "reimbursement",
        "sick leave",
        "leave policy",
        "employee policy",
    ]

    it_keywords = [
        "mfa",
        "multi-factor",
        "authentication",
        "mobile device",
        "account",
        "password",
    ]

    use_hr_agent = any(
        keyword in question
        for keyword in hr_keywords
    )

    use_it_agent = any(
        keyword in question
        for keyword in it_keywords
    )

    return {
        "use_hr_agent": use_hr_agent,
        "use_it_agent": use_it_agent,
    }


# =========================================================
# 8. CREATE THE HR RETRIEVAL AGENT
# =========================================================

def hr_agent(
    state: MultiAgentRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Search only the HR policy knowledge base.
    """

    if not state["use_hr_agent"]:
        return {
            "hr_documents": [],
        }

    retrieved_documents = hr_retriever.invoke(
        state["question"]
    )

    return {
        "hr_documents": retrieved_documents,
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. CREATE THE IT RETRIEVAL AGENT
# =========================================================

def it_agent(
    state: MultiAgentRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Search only the IT support knowledge base.
    """

    if not state["use_it_agent"]:
        return {
            "it_documents": [],
        }

    retrieved_documents = it_retriever.invoke(
        state["question"]
    )

    return {
        "it_documents": retrieved_documents,
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. CREATE THE EVIDENCE AGGREGATOR
# =========================================================

def evidence_aggregator(
    state: MultiAgentRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Combine evidence returned by the specialist agents.
    """

    all_documents = (
        state["hr_documents"]
        + state["it_documents"]
    )

    if not all_documents:
        return {
            "combined_evidence": "",
        }

    unique_documents = {}

    for document in all_documents:
        document_id = document.metadata["id"]

        if document_id not in unique_documents:
            unique_documents[document_id] = document

    evidence_blocks = []

    for document in unique_documents.values():
        evidence_blocks.append(
            "\n".join(
                [
                    f"Domain: {document.metadata['domain']}",
                    f"Source: {document.metadata['source']}",
                    f"Topic: {document.metadata['topic']}",
                    f"Content: {document.page_content}",
                ]
            )
        )

    return {
        "combined_evidence": "\n\n".join(
            evidence_blocks
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. CREATE THE FINAL GENERATOR
# =========================================================

def final_generator(
    state: MultiAgentRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Produce a simple grounded response from all specialist
    evidence.
    """

    if not state["combined_evidence"]:
        return {
            "answer": (
                "No relevant evidence was found in the HR "
                "or IT knowledge bases."
            )
        }

    answer = (
        "The specialist agents returned the following evidence:\n\n"
        f"{state['combined_evidence']}"
    )

    return {
        "answer": answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 12. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(
    MultiAgentRAGState
)

workflow.add_node(
    "supervisor",
    supervisor_agent,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "hr_agent",
    hr_agent,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "it_agent",
    it_agent,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "aggregate_evidence",
    evidence_aggregator,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    final_generator,
)


# Begin with the supervisor.
workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "supervisor",
)


# Execute both specialist agents.
# Each agent checks whether it was selected.
workflow.add_edge(
    "supervisor",
    "hr_agent",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "hr_agent",
    "it_agent",
)


# Combine specialist results.
workflow.add_edge(
    "it_agent",
    "aggregate_evidence",
)


# Generate the final answer.
workflow.add_edge(
    "aggregate_evidence",
    "generate_answer",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)


# Compile the Multi-Agent RAG graph.
multi_agent_rag_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 13. RUN THE MULTI-AGENT RAG WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

def run_multi_agent_rag(
    question: str,
) -> None:
    initial_state: MultiAgentRAGState = {
        "question": question,

        "use_hr_agent": False,
        "use_it_agent": False,

        "hr_documents": [],
        "it_documents": [],

        "combined_evidence": "",
        "answer": "",
    }

    result = multi_agent_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("ORIGINAL QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\nSELECTED AGENTS")
    print("=" * 70)
    print("HR Agent:", result["use_hr_agent"])
    print("IT Agent:", result["use_it_agent"])

    print("\nHR SOURCES")
    print("=" * 70)

    if result["hr_documents"]:
        for document in result["hr_documents"]:
            print("-", document.metadata["source"])
    else:
        print("HR Agent was not used.")

    print("\nIT SOURCES")
    print("=" * 70)

    if result["it_documents"]:
        for document in result["it_documents"]:
            print("-", document.metadata["source"])
    else:
        print("IT Agent was not used.")

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["answer"])


# =========================================================
# 14. TEST THE COMPLETE WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    user_question = (
        "What is the annual certification reimbursement limit, "
        "and what should an employee do with MFA after replacing "
        "their mobile device?"
    )

    run_multi_agent_rag(
        user_question
    )

f) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Multi-Agent RAG?

Multi-Agent RAG is an architecture in which several specialized agents collaborate to retrieve, validate, and synthesize external knowledge. Each agent may have its own prompt, tools, model, knowledge base, and domain responsibility.

A supervisor normally decomposes or routes the user’s request, specialists retrieve relevant evidence, and an aggregator or generator combines the results into one grounded answer. The architecture is useful when questions span multiple domains or sources.

Q2. Why use multiple agents instead of one agent with all tools?

A single agent with many unrelated tools may struggle to select the correct capability and follow all domain-specific instructions. Its prompt becomes large and difficult to maintain, and failures are harder to isolate.

Multiple agents reduce this complexity by giving each specialist a focused role and limited tool collection. LangChain notes that grouping tools and responsibilities can improve focus, and separate agents can have different prompts, examples, models, and evaluation processes.

Q3. What is the role of a supervisor agent?

The supervisor analyzes the original request and determines which specialist agents should participate. It may divide a compound question into subtasks, assign each task to a domain agent, monitor completion, and send the collected outputs to an aggregator.

The supervisor should coordinate rather than duplicate every specialist’s work. Its output should be structured and restricted to approved agent names so it cannot invent nonexistent routes.

Q4. How do agents communicate in LangGraph?

Agents communicate by reading from and writing to shared graph state. One agent may add retrieved documents, another may add evaluation feedback, and a later node can combine those values into the final response.

Each agent is represented as a graph node, while edges define handoffs and execution order. Conditional edges can choose the next agent dynamically, and cycles can support review or correction loops.

Q5. What are the main challenges of Multi-Agent RAG?

The major challenges are incorrect routing, duplicated effort, contradictory evidence, context growth, coordination overhead, permission management, and uncontrolled handoffs. More agents do not automatically produce a better answer.

A reliable design requires focused roles, structured state, source-aware evidence aggregation, maximum handoff limits, clear termination conditions, tracing, and separate evaluation of each specialist and the overall workflow.


Chapter 7: RAG Evaluation

Topic 1: Context Precision

a) Definition
* Context Precision is a retrieval-quality metric that measures whether the chunks returned by a RAG retriever are relevant to the user’s question. It 
  focuses particularly on ranking quality by checking whether useful chunks appear near the top and irrelevant chunks appear lower in the retrieved list.

* A high Context Precision score means that most of the retrieved chunks are useful and that the retriever places answer-bearing chunks in strong ranking 
  positions. A low score means that the context contains irrelevant, weakly related, duplicate, or incorrectly ranked chunks that may distract the LLM.

* Context Precision evaluates the retrieval component, not the final generated answer. The generator may still produce an incorrect response even when 
  Context Precision is high, so generation metrics such as Faithfulness, Groundedness, and Answer Relevance must be measured separately.

* In RAGAS, Context Precision can be evaluated using a reference answer or, depending on the selected metric variant, using the generated response. The 
  reference-based version judges whether each retrieved chunk contains information useful for producing the expected answer.

b) Purpose
* The primary purpose of Context Precision is to identify how much irrelevant information a retriever places in the context sent to the LLM. Even if one 
  correct chunk is retrieved, several irrelevant chunks can consume tokens, introduce conflicting information, and reduce the generator’s ability to focus 
  on the correct evidence.

* Context Precision also evaluates the ordering of retrieved chunks. A relevant chunk appearing at rank one is more valuable than the same chunk appearing 
  after several distractors, because highly ranked content is more likely to fit within the token budget and receive useful attention from the model.

* This metric helps developers determine whether changes such as Re-Ranking, Metadata Filtering, Hybrid Search, improved chunking, or query transformation 
  reduce retrieval noise. The old and new retrieval pipelines can be compared using the same evaluation dataset to determine whether the optimization 
  improves ranking quality.

* Context Precision should be measured when the application retrieves multiple chunks and only some are useful for answering the question. It is less 
  informative by itself when the main problem is missing evidence, because missing relevant information is better assessed using Context Recall.

c) Evaluation Workflow Diagram

                 CONTEXT PRECISION EVALUATION
                 ============================

                       User Question
                             |
                             v
                       RAG Retriever
                             |
                             v
                  Ranked Retrieved Chunks

              Rank 1 -> Relevant Chunk
              Rank 2 -> Irrelevant Chunk
              Rank 3 -> Relevant Chunk
              Rank 4 -> Irrelevant Chunk
                             |
                             v
                    Relevance Evaluation
            Compare Chunks with Reference Answer
                             |
                             v
                 Calculate Precision at Each Rank
                             |
                             v
             Reward Highly Ranked Relevant Chunks
                             |
                             v
                Context Precision Score: 0 to 1
                             |
               +-------------+-------------+
               |                           |
               v                           v
          Higher Score                Lower Score
      Focused retrieval          Noisy or poorly ranked



d) Core Components

1. User Input
The user input is the original question submitted to the RAG application. It provides the retrieval intent against which every returned chunk is evaluated, so the evaluation dataset should contain realistic queries representing actual user vocabulary and complexity.

2. Retrieved Contexts
Retrieved contexts are the ranked chunks returned by the retriever. Their order must remain unchanged during evaluation because Context Precision checks not only whether relevant evidence was retrieved but also whether it appeared before irrelevant evidence.

3. Reference Answer or Generated Response
Reference-based Context Precision uses an expected answer to determine whether each retrieved chunk contributes useful evidence. When no reference answer exists, a context-utilization variant can evaluate the chunks against the generated response, although that approach depends on the answer’s quality.

4. Relevance Judge and Scoring Logic
The relevance judge assigns each chunk a binary or model-based relevance assessment. RAGAS provides LLM-based variants and non-LLM alternatives, allowing evaluation using reference answers, generated responses, or reference contexts depending on available ground truth.

5. Context Precision Formula

For a ranked list containing K retrieved chunks:
	
		             K
                            Sum [Precision@k x v(k)]
                            k=1
Context Precision@K = --------------------------------
                       Total relevant chunks in Top-K

Where

	Precision@k =
                                     Relevant chunks found from rank 1 to rank k
                                     --------------------------------------------
                                         Total chunks considered up to rank k

And:

	v(k) = 1 if the chunk at rank k is relevant
        v(k) = 0 if the chunk at rank k is irrelevant

RAGAS describes Context Precision as the mean of Precision@k at positions containing relevant chunks, thereby rewarding relevant contexts that occur near the beginning of the ranking.

e) Code Example

import asyncio

from openai import AsyncOpenAI

from ragas.llms import llm_factory
from ragas.metrics.collections import ContextPrecision


# =========================================================
# 1. DEFINE THE EVALUATION SAMPLE
# =========================================================

user_question = (
    "What is the annual professional certification "
    "reimbursement limit?"
)

reference_answer = (
    "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees per financial "
    "year for approved professional certifications."
)

retrieved_contexts = [
    (
        "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
        "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
        "certifications."
    ),
    (
        "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
        "days during each calendar year."
    ),
    (
        "Manager approval is required before registering for "
        "a professional certification."
    ),
    (
        "Employees travelling for approved business purposes "
        "can claim hotel accommodation for seven days."
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE THE ASYNCHRONOUS OPENAI CLIENT
# =========================================================

openai_client = AsyncOpenAI()


# =========================================================
# 3. CREATE THE RAGAS EVALUATOR LLM
# =========================================================

evaluator_llm = llm_factory(
    model="gpt-4o-mini",
    client=openai_client,
)


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE CONTEXT-PRECISION SCORER
# =========================================================

context_precision_scorer = ContextPrecision(
    llm=evaluator_llm
)


# =========================================================
# 5. DEFINE THE ASYNCHRONOUS EVALUATION FUNCTION
# =========================================================

async def evaluate_context_precision() -> None:
    """
    Evaluate whether relevant retrieved chunks are ranked
    before irrelevant chunks.
    """

    evaluation_result = (
        await context_precision_scorer.ascore(
            user_input=user_question,
            reference=reference_answer,
            retrieved_contexts=retrieved_contexts,
        )
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("USER QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(user_question)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("REFERENCE ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(reference_answer)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("RETRIEVED CONTEXTS")
    print("=" * 70)

    for rank, context in enumerate(
        retrieved_contexts,
        start=1,
    ):
        print(f"\nRank {rank}:")
        print(context)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("CONTEXT PRECISION RESULT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(
        "Score:",
        round(evaluation_result.value, 4),
    )


# =========================================================
# 6. RUN THE PROGRAM
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(
        evaluate_context_precision()
    )

f) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Context Precision in RAG?

Context Precision measures whether the chunks returned by a retriever are relevant to the user’s question and whether relevant chunks are ranked above irrelevant chunks. It focuses on the quality and ordering of the context supplied to the LLM.

A high score means the retrieved context is focused and places useful evidence near the top. A low score means the result list contains significant noise or that relevant evidence is buried below unrelated passages.

Q2. What is the difference between Context Precision and Context Recall?

Context Precision measures how much of the retrieved context is relevant and whether useful chunks are well ranked. It answers the question,“Of the information retrieved, how much is useful and how well is it ordered?”

Context Recall measures whether the retrieved context contains all the information required to support the reference answer. A system can have high precision but low recall when it retrieves one highly relevant document while missing additional evidence needed for a complete answer.

Q3. Can Context Precision be high when irrelevant chunks are present?

Yes. Context Precision is ranking-sensitive, so irrelevant chunks placed after all relevant evidence may have limited effect compared with irrelevant chunks appearing near the top. RAGAS documentation illustrates that an irrelevant chunk placed after the relevant answer-bearing chunk can still produce a very high score.

However, unnecessary chunks still consume tokens and may affect generation quality. Therefore, a high Context Precision score should not prevent developers from monitoring context size, redundancy, latency, and token consumption.

Q4. How can Context Precision be improved?

Context Precision can be improved using better chunking, Hybrid Search, Metadata Filtering, Re-Ranking, deduplication, source-authority rules, score thresholds, and query transformation. The appropriate technique depends on why irrelevant content appears in the result list.

If correct chunks are retrieved but ranked poorly, a Re-Ranker is appropriate. If outdated documents appear, metadata filtering is needed. If exact identifiers are missed, BM25 or Hybrid Search may improve retrieval behaviour.

Q5. What inputs are required for RAGAS Context Precision?

For reference-based Context Precision, the primary inputs are the user question, retrieved contexts in ranking order, and a reference answer. The evaluator uses the reference answer to determine whether each retrieved chunk contributes to the expected response.

When a reference answer is unavailable, Context Utilization can evaluate retrieved chunks against the generated response. Non-LLM Context Precision can also be used when reference contexts or reliable relevance labels are already available.

Topic 2: Context Recall

a) Definition

* Context Recall is a retrieval-quality metric that measures whether the retrieved context contains all the information required to support the expected answer. It evaluates completeness rather than ranking purity, helping determine whether the retriever missed important facts or supporting documents.

In the LLM-based RAGAS approach, the reference answer is divided into individual claims. Each claim is checked against the retrieved contexts to determine whether it can be supported by the available evidence, and the final score represents the proportion of supported reference claims.

A high Context Recall score indicates that most or all information required by the reference answer was successfully retrieved. A low score means that one or more required facts are absent, even if the chunks that were retrieved are highly relevant to part of the question.

Context Recall normally requires a reference answer or reference contexts because the evaluator needs to know which facts should have been retrieved. Without some representation of the expected evidence, it is not possible to determine reliably what important information was missed. 

b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Context Recall is to identify missing evidence in a RAG pipeline. A retriever may return one highly relevant document, but the 
  answer may require several facts distributed across multiple chunks, such as a reimbursement limit, approval requirement, required documents, and 
  submission deadline.

* Context Recall helps diagnose whether an incomplete answer originated from retrieval or generation. If the required facts are absent from the retrieved 
  contexts, the retriever is primarily responsible; if all facts are present but the answer omits them, the problem is more likely related to prompting or 
  generation.

* The metric is useful for evaluating Query Expansion, Multi-Query Retrieval, Multi-Hop Retrieval, Hybrid Search, Top-K changes, and broader candidate 
  retrieval. These techniques attempt to increase the probability that all required supporting evidence appears in the retrieved candidate set.

* Recall should not be optimized independently because retrieving many chunks can increase evidence coverage while reducing Context Precision. A production 
  RAG system must retrieve enough information for completeness while preventing excessive irrelevant content from reaching the LLM.

c) Context Recall Evaluation Workflow

                  CONTEXT RECALL EVALUATION
                  =========================

                       User Question
                             |
                             v
                      Reference Answer
                             |
                             v
                Break Reference into Claims

          Claim 1: Reimbursement limit is 25,000
          Claim 2: Manager approval is required
          Claim 3: Receipt must be submitted
          Claim 4: Certificate must be submitted
                             |
                             v
                    Retrieved Contexts
                             |
                             v
                Check Support for Each Claim

          Claim 1 -> Supported
          Claim 2 -> Supported
          Claim 3 -> Not Supported
          Claim 4 -> Not Supported
                             |
                             v
                 Calculate Supported Proportion
                             |
                             v
                     Context Recall = 0.50

d) Core Components

1. User Input
The user input identifies the information requirement being evaluated. It helps the evaluator interpret the reference claims and prevents unrelated statements in the retrieved context from being treated as useful evidence for the current request.

2. Reference Answer or Reference Contexts
The reference answer describes the facts expected in a correct response and can act as a proxy for manually annotated reference contexts. RAGAS uses this approach because creating reference-context labels for every evaluation example can require considerable annotation effort.

3. Retrieved Contexts
Retrieved contexts are the document chunks returned by the RAG retriever. Unlike Context Precision, claim-based Context Recall mainly evaluates whether the required evidence exists somewhere in these contexts rather than strongly emphasizing its ranking position.

4. Claim Extractor and Support Evaluator
The claim extractor separates the reference into individually verifiable statements. The support evaluator then examines whether each statement can be attributed to the retrieved contexts and uses those decisions to calculate the final recall score.

5. Context Recall Formula


	                                 Number of reference claims supported
                                         by the retrieved contexts
                    Context Recall =  ---------------------------------------
                                         Total number of claims in the
                                         reference answer

For a context-labelled dataset: 

		 Number of relevant reference contexts
                 successfully retrieved
Context Recall = ---------------------------------------
                 Total number of relevant
                 reference contexts

The first formula evaluates fact coverage by breaking the expected answer into claims. The second evaluates document or context coverage when the correct supporting contexts are already manually labelled.

e) Code Example

import asyncio
from openai import AsyncOpenAI
from ragas.llms import llm_factory
from ragas.metrics.collections import ContextRecall


# =========================================================
# 1. DEFINE THE USER QUESTION
# =========================================================

user_question = (
    "What is the certification reimbursement limit, "
    "what approval is required, and which documents "
    "must be submitted after completion?"
)


# =========================================================
# 2. DEFINE THE REFERENCE ANSWER
# =========================================================

reference_answer = (
    "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees per financial "
    "year for approved professional certifications. Manager "
    "approval is required before registration. After completing "
    "the certification, the employee must submit proof of "
    "payment and the completion certificate within thirty days."
)


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE THE RETRIEVED CONTEXTS
# =========================================================

retrieved_contexts = [
    (
        "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees per financial "
        "year for approved professional certifications."
    ),
    (
        "Manager approval is required before registering for "
        "an approved professional certification."
    ),
    (
        "Permanent employees receive twelve paid sick-leave "
        "days during each calendar year."
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE OPENAI CLIENT
# =========================================================

openai_client = AsyncOpenAI()


# =========================================================
# 5. CREATE THE RAGAS EVALUATOR LLM
# =========================================================

evaluator_llm = llm_factory(
    model="gpt-4o-mini",
    client=openai_client,
)


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE THE CONTEXT-RECALL SCORER
# =========================================================

context_recall_scorer = ContextRecall(
    llm=evaluator_llm
)


# =========================================================
# 7. DEFINE THE EVALUATION FUNCTION
# =========================================================

async def evaluate_context_recall() -> None:
    """
    Evaluate how many claims from the reference answer
    are supported by the retrieved contexts.
    """

    result = await context_recall_scorer.ascore(
        user_input=user_question,
        reference=reference_answer,
        retrieved_contexts=retrieved_contexts,
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("USER QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(user_question)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("REFERENCE ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(reference_answer)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("RETRIEVED CONTEXTS")
    print("=" * 70)

    for position, context in enumerate(
        retrieved_contexts,
        start=1,
    ):
        print(f"\nContext {position}:")
        print(context)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("CONTEXT RECALL RESULT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(
        "Score:",
        round(result.value, 4),
    )


# =========================================================
# 8. RUN THE PROGRAM
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(
        evaluate_context_recall()
    )

Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Context Recall in RAG?

Context Recall measures how much of the information needed for the expected answer is present in the retrieved context. RAGAS commonly calculates it by decomposing the reference answer into claims and checking how many claims are supported by the retrieved passages.

A score near 1 means the retriever found nearly all required evidence. A low score indicates that important facts or supporting documents were missed, even if the retrieved chunks are relevant to part of the question.

Q2. What is the difference between Context Precision and Context Recall?

Context Precision measures whether retrieved chunks are relevant and well ranked. It focuses on retrieval noise and asks whether useful chunks appear before irrelevant chunks.

Context Recall measures whether all required information was retrieved. A system can produce high precision and low recall when it retrieves one focused document but misses other evidence required for a complete answer.

Q3. Why does Context Recall require a reference?

Recall measures what proportion of required information was successfully retrieved. To calculate this proportion, the evaluator needs a definition of the complete expected evidence, usually represented by a reference answer or manually labelled reference contexts.

Without a reference, the evaluator cannot reliably know which facts are missing. The retrieved results may look relevant, but there is no trustworthy basis for deciding whether they represent the complete answer.

Q4. Can a system have high Context Recall and low Context Precision?

Yes. The retriever may include all required evidence but also return many irrelevant chunks. This produces high recall because nothing important is missing, but low precision because a large proportion of the retrieved context is noise.

The appropriate solution is normally to retrieve broadly, then use Metadata Filtering, Re-Ranking, deduplication, and Context Selection to retain complete evidence while removing irrelevant content.

Q5. How can Context Recall be improved?

Context Recall can be improved through better chunking, larger candidate retrieval, Query Expansion, Multi-Query Retrieval, Hybrid Search, metadata corrections, and Multi-Hop Retrieval. The appropriate technique depends on why the required evidence was missed.

The changes should be evaluated alongside Context Precision, latency, and token usage. Increasing recall by retrieving a very large amount of irrelevant context may improve the metric while reducing end-to-end answer quality.


Topic 3: Faithfulness

a) Definition

Faithfulness measures whether the factual claims in a generated response are supported by the retrieved context. A response is faithful when the model uses only the supplied evidence and does not introduce claims that cannot be inferred from the retrieved documents.

Faithfulness mainly evaluates the generation component of a RAG pipeline rather than retrieval quality. The correct documents may have been retrieved, but the LLM can still misinterpret them, alter a numerical value, combine unrelated statements, or add unsupported information.

RAGAS calculates Faithfulness by breaking the generated response into individual claims and checking each claim against the retrieved contexts. The score is the proportion of generated claims supported by the evidence and generally ranges from 0 to 1.

A high Faithfulness score indicates that the response remains closely grounded in the context. A low score indicates potential hallucination or factual inconsistency, but it does not automatically mean that the response is irrelevant or that the retrieved evidence was complete.

b) Purpose

The primary purpose of Faithfulness is to detect unsupported claims or hallucinations in a RAG-generated answer. An LLM can produce a fluent and convincing response even when some statements are absent from or contradicted by the retrieved evidence.

Faithfulness helps separate retrieval failures from generation failures. If the right evidence is present but the answer introduces incorrect information, the generator or prompt is responsible; if the evidence itself is missing, Context Recall is the more appropriate diagnostic metric.

The metric is particularly important for enterprise assistants where numerical values, deadlines, approval conditions, policy versions, and exceptions must remain accurate. Even a minor alteration can make the answer operationally incorrect despite being generally related to the source.

Faithfulness should be evaluated together with Answer Relevance and Context Recall. A response can be fully faithful but incomplete or unrelated, while an answer can be relevant and factually plausible but unsupported by the retrieved evidence.

c) Faithfulness Evaluation Workflow

                    FAITHFULNESS EVALUATION
                    =======================

                       User Question
                             |
                             v
                    Retrieved Contexts
                             |
                             v
                  RAG-Generated Response
                             |
                             v
              Break Response into Atomic Claims

        Claim 1: Limit is 25,000 rupees
        Claim 2: Manager approval is required
        Claim 3: Reimbursement is paid immediately
                             |
                             v
            Compare Every Claim with the Context

        Claim 1 -> Supported
        Claim 2 -> Supported
        Claim 3 -> Unsupported
                             |
                             v
                    Calculate Faithfulness
                             |
                             v
                 Supported Claims / All Claims

                      Score = 2 / 3
                      Score = 0.67

d) Components

1. User Input
The user input provides the intent and scope of the original request. Although the Faithfulness score primarily compares the response with the contexts, the question helps the evaluator interpret claims and determine whether the answer is responding within the expected task.

2. Generated Response
The generated response is the primary object being evaluated. It is divided into independently verifiable claims so that a partly grounded answer can receive an intermediate score instead of being classified only as completely correct or incorrect.

3. Retrieved Contexts
Retrieved contexts represent the evidence that was available to the generator. A claim is faithful only when it can be supported by these contexts, even if the claim happens to be factually correct according to outside knowledge.

4. Claim Extractor and Entailment Evaluator
The claim extractor converts the response into atomic statements, while the evaluator checks whether each statement is entailed by the retrieved evidence. RAGAS can use an LLM or a specialized hallucination-detection model for this support-verification stage.

5. Faithfulness Formula

                   Number of generated claims supported
                   by the retrieved context
Faithfulness = --------------------------------------------
                   Total number of factual claims
                   in the generated response

Where:

	Supported claim   = The claim can be inferred from the context
	Unsupported claim = The context does not establish the claim

The score usually ranges from 0 to 1. A score of 1 indicates that all extracted claims are supported, while a score of 0 indicates that none of the claims can be established from the retrieved evidence.

e) Code Example

import asyncio
from openai import AsyncOpenAI
from ragas.llms import llm_factory
from ragas.metrics.collections import Faithfulness


# =========================================================
# 1. DEFINE THE USER QUESTION
# =========================================================

user_question = (
    "What is the professional certification "
    "reimbursement policy?"
)


# =========================================================
# 2. DEFINE THE RETRIEVED CONTEXTS
# =========================================================

retrieved_contexts = [
    (
        "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
        "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
        "certifications."
    ),
    (
        "Manager approval is required before registering for "
        "an approved professional certification."
    ),
]


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE THE GENERATED RESPONSE
# =========================================================

generated_response = (
    "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees per financial "
    "year for approved professional certifications. Manager "
    "approval is required before registration. The reimbursement "
    "is always paid within five working days."
)


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE OPENAI CLIENT
# =========================================================

openai_client = AsyncOpenAI()


# =========================================================
# 5. CREATE THE RAGAS EVALUATOR LLM
# =========================================================

evaluator_llm = llm_factory(
    model="gpt-4o-mini",
    client=openai_client,
)


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE THE FAITHFULNESS SCORER
# =========================================================

faithfulness_scorer = Faithfulness(
    llm=evaluator_llm
)


# =========================================================
# 7. DEFINE THE EVALUATION FUNCTION
# =========================================================

async def evaluate_faithfulness() -> None:
    """
    Evaluate whether every claim in the generated response
    is supported by the retrieved contexts.
    """

    result = await faithfulness_scorer.ascore(
        user_input=user_question,
        response=generated_response,
        retrieved_contexts=retrieved_contexts,
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("USER QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(user_question)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("RETRIEVED CONTEXTS")
    print("=" * 70)

    for position, context in enumerate(
        retrieved_contexts,
        start=1,
    ):
        print(f"\nContext {position}:")
        print(context)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("GENERATED RESPONSE")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(generated_response)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("FAITHFULNESS RESULT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(
        "Score:",
        round(result.value, 4),
    )


# =========================================================
# 8. RUN THE PROGRAM
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(
        evaluate_faithfulness()
    )

f) Code Example

Interview Questions and Answers
Q1. What is Faithfulness in RAG evaluation?

Faithfulness measures whether the factual claims made in a generated response are supported by the retrieved context. It evaluates the generation stage and helps detect claims that the LLM invented, altered, or imported from its internal knowledge without evidence.

The response is decomposed into individual claims, and every claim is checked against the retrieved contexts. The final score is the proportion of generated claims supported by the available evidence.

Q2. What is the difference between Faithfulness and Context Recall?

Context Recall measures whether the retriever supplied all information required by the reference answer. It evaluates retrieval completeness and identifies evidence that was missed.

Faithfulness measures whether the generator stays within the information that was actually retrieved. High Faithfulness with low Context Recall means the model carefully used incomplete evidence, resulting in an answer that may be grounded but incomplete.

Q3. Does a Faithfulness score of 1 mean the answer is correct?

No. A score of 1 means that every generated claim is supported by the retrieved contexts. If those contexts contain inaccurate, outdated, or incomplete information, the answer can be completely faithful to the evidence while still being wrong in the real world.

Faithfulness should therefore be combined with source-quality checks, answer correctness, Context Recall, Answer Relevance, and document-freshness validation.

Q4. Does Faithfulness require a reference answer?

Basic RAGAS Faithfulness generally does not require a reference answer. It requires the user input, generated response, and retrieved contexts, and it determines whether the contexts support the claims made in the response.

This reference-free characteristic makes it useful for evaluating production responses. However, reference answers may still be needed for answer correctness or Context Recall evaluation.

Q5. How can Faithfulness be improved in production?

Faithfulness can be improved by using strict evidence-only prompts, better Re-Ranking, Context Compression, authoritative metadata filters, citation requirements, lower generation randomness, and post-generation claim verification.

A self-corrective workflow can evaluate the answer before returning it. If unsupported claims are identified, the generator can revise the answer using the same retrieved evidence or retrieve additional context when the evidence itself is insufficient.

Topic 4: Answer Relevance

a) Definition

* Answer Relevance, also called Answer Relevancy or Response Relevancy, measures how directly and appropriately a generated response addresses the user’s original question. It evaluates whether the answer remains focused on the requested information instead of discussing unrelated or unnecessary topics.

* A highly relevant answer covers the important parts of the user’s request with minimal unnecessary information. A low-relevance answer may be incomplete, overly general, evasive, repetitive, or filled with details that do not contribute to answering the original question.

* Answer Relevance primarily evaluates the quality and alignment of the generated response, not the quality of retrieval. It does not determine whether the answer is factually correct or supported by retrieved evidence, so it should be evaluated together with Faithfulness, Context Precision, and Context Recall.

* In RAGAS, Answer Relevance is generally a reference-free metric that uses the user input and generated response. The evaluator generates several artificial questions from the response and compares their embeddings with the original question to estimate how closely the response matches the user’s intent.

b) Purpose

* The primary purpose of Answer Relevance is to identify responses that are factually plausible but fail to satisfy the user’s actual request. For example, an answer may correctly explain professional certifications while failing to state the annual reimbursement limit asked by the user.

* It detects incomplete responses to multi-part questions. If the user asks for the reimbursement amount, approval requirement, and submission deadline, an answer covering only the amount may receive a lower relevance score because it does not address the complete information need.

* Answer Relevance also identifies unnecessarily verbose or off-topic responses. Additional details are not automatically harmful, but excessive background information can obscure the direct answer and reduce alignment with the original query.

* The metric helps developers improve prompts, response structure, query understanding, and answer synthesis. If Context Recall and Faithfulness are high but Answer Relevance is low, the evidence is probably available and correctly used, but the generator is not presenting it in a sufficiently direct or complete form.

c) Answer Relevance Evaluation Workflow

                   ANSWER RELEVANCE EVALUATION
                   ===========================

                      Original Question
                             |
                             v
                    Generated Response
                             |
                             v
              Generate Artificial Questions
                   from the Response

           Generated Question 1
           Generated Question 2
           Generated Question 3
                             |
                             v
            Embed the Original and Generated
                        Questions
                             |
                             v
               Calculate Cosine Similarities

          Original Query <-> Generated Query 1
          Original Query <-> Generated Query 2
          Original Query <-> Generated Query 3
                             |
                             v
                 Average Similarity Scores
                             |
                             v
                    Answer Relevance Score

d) Core Components

Core Components
1. User Input
The user input represents the question or information need against which response alignment is evaluated. Compound questions should preserve all subrequirements because an answer that covers only one part may otherwise appear relevant despite being incomplete.

2. Generated Response
The response is the output produced by the RAG generator. The metric evaluates whether its content directly corresponds to the user’s request and penalizes answers that omit important requirements or introduce excessive unrelated details.

3. Artificial-Question Generator
The evaluator LLM generates questions that could reasonably be answered by the response. If those generated questions are semantically similar to the original user question, the response is considered well aligned with the original intent.

4. Embedding Model and Similarity Calculator
The embedding model converts the original and generated questions into vectors, while cosine similarity measures their semantic closeness. The average similarity across artificial questions becomes the final Answer Relevance score.

5. Answer Relevance Formula

If N artificial questions are generated from the response:

	                      N
                             Sum cosine(Egi, Eo)
                             i=1
Answer Relevance Score = ---------------------------
                                      N

Where:

	Eo  = Embedding of the original user question
	Egi = Embedding of generated question i
	N   = Number of artificial questions

Cosine similarity is calculated as:

			                         Egi . Eo
			cosine(Egi, Eo) = -------------------------
                    			     ||Egi|| x ||Eo||

RAGAS typically generates three artificial questions by default, although this can be adjusted through the metric’s strictness configuration. The final result is the average cosine similarity between those questions and the original user input.

Scores normally appear between 0 and 1, with higher values indicating stronger question-answer alignment. However, cosine similarity mathematically ranges from -1 to 1, so a 0 to 1 result is common in practice but not theoretically guaranteed.

e) Code Example

import asyncio
from openai import AsyncOpenAI
from ragas.llms import llm_factory
from ragas.embeddings.base import embedding_factory
from ragas.metrics.collections import AnswerRelevancy


# =========================================================
# 1. DEFINE THE USER QUESTION
# =========================================================

user_question = (
    "What is the annual certification reimbursement limit, "
    "and what approval is required?"
)


# =========================================================
# 2. DEFINE THE GENERATED RESPONSE
# =========================================================

generated_response = (
    "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
    "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
    "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
    "registration."
)


# =========================================================
# 3. CREATE THE ASYNCHRONOUS OPENAI CLIENT
# =========================================================

openai_client = AsyncOpenAI()


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE THE RAGAS EVALUATOR LLM
# =========================================================

evaluator_llm = llm_factory(
    model="gpt-4o-mini",
    client=openai_client,
)


# =========================================================
# 5. CREATE THE EMBEDDING MODEL
# =========================================================

evaluator_embeddings = embedding_factory(
    provider="openai",
    model="text-embedding-3-small",
    client=openai_client,
)


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE THE ANSWER-RELEVANCY SCORER
# =========================================================

answer_relevancy_scorer = AnswerRelevancy(
    llm=evaluator_llm,
    embeddings=evaluator_embeddings,
)


# =========================================================
# 7. DEFINE THE EVALUATION FUNCTION
# =========================================================

async def evaluate_answer_relevance() -> None:
    """
    Evaluate how directly the generated response addresses
    the original user question.
    """

    result = await answer_relevancy_scorer.ascore(
        user_input=user_question,
        response=generated_response,
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("USER QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(user_question)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("GENERATED RESPONSE")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(generated_response)

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("ANSWER RELEVANCE RESULT")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(
        "Score:",
        round(result.value, 4),
    )


# =========================================================
# 8. RUN THE PROGRAM
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    asyncio.run(
        evaluate_answer_relevance()
    )

f) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Answer Relevance in RAG evaluation?

Answer Relevance measures how directly and completely the generated response addresses the user’s original question. It penalizes responses that are incomplete, off-topic, unnecessarily repetitive, or dominated by information unrelated to the request.

In RAGAS, the evaluator generates artificial questions from the response and compares their embeddings with the original user question. Strong similarity suggests that the answer is well aligned with the information need.

Q2. Does Answer Relevance measure factual accuracy?

No. Answer Relevance evaluates alignment between the question and response, not whether the factual statements are true. A response can directly answer the question using an incorrect amount and still receive a high relevance score.

Factual correctness should be measured against a reference answer or trusted source, while Faithfulness should verify that generated claims are supported by the retrieved context.

Q3. What is the difference between Answer Relevance and Faithfulness?

Answer Relevance asks whether the response addresses the user’s question. Faithfulness asks whether the claims in the response are supported by the retrieved context.

A relevant but unfaithful answer responds directly using unsupported information. A faithful but irrelevant answer accurately repeats retrieved evidence that does not address the user’s actual request.

Q4. Does Answer Relevance require a reference answer?

The RAGAS Answer Relevance metric is generally reference-free. It uses the user input and generated response, generates artificial questions from the response, and compares them semantically with the original question.

A reference answer is required for metrics such as Answer Correctness or Context Recall, where evaluation depends on comparing the system output or retrieved evidence with expected factual content.

Q5. How can Answer Relevance be improved?

Answer Relevance can be improved by using clear generation instructions, decomposing compound questions, retrieving evidence for every subrequirement, reducing noisy context, and requiring a direct answer before optional explanation.

A post-generation evaluator can compare the response with the original question, identify missing requirements or irrelevant detail, and send corrective feedback to the generator before returning the final answer.

Chapter 7: RAG Evaluation

Topic 6: RAGAS

a) Definition

RAGAS stands for Retrieval-Augmented Generation Assessment and is an evaluation framework for measuring the quality of RAG and other LLM applications. It provides metrics that evaluate retrieval quality, response quality, factual consistency, semantic similarity, tool usage, and agent behaviour.

RAGAS evaluates a RAG pipeline at the component level instead of relying only on one overall correctness score. For example, Context Precision and Context Recall evaluate the retriever, while Faithfulness and Answer Relevancy evaluate how effectively the generator uses the retrieved information.

Many RAGAS metrics use an LLM-as-a-judge, an embedding model, or both to evaluate natural-language outputs. LLM-based metrics interpret claims and semantic relationships, while non-LLM metrics can use string comparison, contextual similarity, exact match, BLEU, ROUGE, or other deterministic methods.

RAGAS can evaluate individual interactions or complete datasets containing questions, generated answers, retrieved contexts, and reference answers. It helps teams compare retrievers, LLMs, prompts, chunking methods, embedding models, and RAG architectures using measurable results rather than subjective observation.

b) Purpose

The primary purpose of RAGAS is to identify which part of a RAG pipeline is responsible for poor output. A single end-to-end score may say that an answer is weak, but separate metrics can reveal whether the retriever returned irrelevant chunks, missed necessary evidence, or whether the generator introduced unsupported claims.

RAGAS supports evaluation-driven development by allowing teams to build a baseline and measure the impact of every pipeline change. For example, developers can determine whether Hybrid Search improves Context Recall, whether Re-Ranking improves Context Precision, or whether a revised prompt improves Faithfulness.

It reduces dependence on manual evaluation because several metrics can operate without a reference answer. Faithfulness compares the response with retrieved evidence, while Answer Relevancy compares the response with the original question, making these metrics useful for experiments and sampled production interactions.

RAGAS is also useful for regression testing and continuous monitoring. A RAG configuration can be evaluated against a fixed dataset before deployment, and production samples can be scored periodically to detect quality degradation caused by document changes, model updates, or new query patterns

c) RAGAS Evaluation Architecture

                    RAGAS EVALUATION WORKFLOW
                    =========================

                       Evaluation Dataset
               Questions | References | Contexts
                              |
                              v
                        Execute RAG System
                              |
                              v
                  Collect Evaluation Records

                 User Input
                 Generated Response
                 Retrieved Contexts
                 Reference Answer
                 Reference Contexts
                              |
                              v
                    Select RAGAS Metrics
           +------------------+------------------+
           |                  |                  |
           v                  v                  v
   Context Precision   Context Recall      Faithfulness
     Retrieval noise   Missing evidence   Claim support
           |                  |                  |
           +------------------+------------------+
                              |
                              v
                     Answer Relevancy
                    Question alignment
                              |
                              v
                Individual and Aggregate Scores
                              |
                              v
              Diagnose Failures and Compare Systems
                              |
                              v
                 Improve and Re-evaluate RAG

d) Core Components

1. Evaluation Dataset
The evaluation dataset contains realistic questions representing the application’s expected usage. Depending on the metrics, it may include reference answers, reference contexts, generated responses, and retrieved chunks, with enough examples to cover direct, conversational, ambiguous, and multi-document queries.

2. RAG Evaluation Sample
A RAGAS sample represents one interaction and can contain fields such as user_input, response, retrieved_contexts, reference, and reference_contexts. Each metric declares which fields it needs, so incomplete samples cannot be used with metrics requiring missing ground truth.

3. Evaluator LLM and Embeddings
LLM-based metrics use an evaluator model to extract claims, determine context relevance, generate artificial questions, or judge entailment. Embedding-based metrics convert text into vectors and use semantic similarity to compare questions, answers, or contexts.

4. Metrics and Experiment Results
Metrics calculate individual scores for each sample, while aggregate results summarize performance across the evaluation dataset. Teams should preserve sample-level results because averages alone can hide failures in particular topics, languages, document types, or query categories.

5. Major RAGAS Metrics

Context Precision
Context Precision evaluates whether retrieved chunks are relevant and whether relevant evidence appears before irrelevant evidence. It is useful for identifying noisy retrieval, weak ranking, duplicate passages, and situations where answer-bearing documents appear too late in the result list.

Context Recall
Context Recall measures how much of the evidence required by the reference answer is present in the retrieved contexts. It identifies missing evidence and is especially useful for multi-document or multi-part questions where several facts are required for a complete response.

Faithfulness
Faithfulness checks whether the factual claims in the generated response are supported by the retrieved contexts. It evaluates the generator’s use of evidence and detects unsupported, contradicted, or hallucinated statements introduced after retrieval.

Answer Relevancy
Answer Relevancy measures how directly the generated response addresses the user’s original question. It penalizes incomplete, off-topic, overly general, or unnecessarily repetitive responses without directly determining whether their factual content is correct.

e) Code Example

import asyncio
from openai import AsyncOpenAI
from ragas.llms import llm_factory
from ragas.embeddings.base import embedding_factory

from ragas.metrics.collections import (
    AnswerRelevancy,
    ContextPrecision,
    ContextRecall,
    Faithfulness,
)


# =========================================================
# 1. DEFINE THE EVALUATION SAMPLE
# =========================================================

user_question = (
    "What is the annual professional certification "
    "reimbursement limit, and what approval is required?"
)

generated_response = (
    "Employees may claim reimbursement of up to 25,000 "
    "rupees per financial year for approved professional "
    "certifications. Manager approval is required before "
    "registration."
)

reference_answer = (
