                    Multimodal LLM
                           |
                           v
                Grounded Response with Sources

d) Core Components

i. Multimodal Data Parser

The parser extracts different content types from enterprise documents. It may perform text extraction, OCR, image extraction, table recognition, layout analysis, audio transcription, key-frame extraction, and document-structure detection.

Parsing should preserve relationships among modalities. Separating a chart from its legend or a table from its column headers can destroy the meaning required for reliable retrieval and generation.

ii. Text and Image Nodes

A text node stores a paragraph, section, caption, or table representation together with its metadata. An image node stores a native image or an image reference, plus its caption, source document, page number, and surrounding context.

iii. Multimodal Embedding Models

Text embedding models convert text into vectors, while image embedding models convert visual content into vectors. Cross-modal models place text and images in a shared representation space, enabling a text query such as "revenue chart" to retrieve a related image.

Image captions can also be embedded as text. Caption-based retrieval is easy to implement, but it may lose visual details that were omitted or incorrectly described during caption generation.

iv. Multimodal Vector Stores

A multimodal application may use separate vector stores or collections for each modality. The text collection contains text embeddings, while the image collection contains image embeddings and references to the native image files.

LlamaIndex demonstrates a StorageContext containing separate text and image vector stores and uses MultiModalVectorStoreIndex to coordinate them.

v. Cross-Modal Retriever

The retriever searches one or more modalities based on the query. A text question may retrieve a paragraph and chart, while an image query may retrieve visually similar images and semantically related text.

Retrieval results should contain comparable scores or pass through a Re-Ranking layer before context construction. Raw text and image similarity values may not be directly comparable when they originate from different models.

vi. Multimodal LLM

A multimodal LLM receives text and images together and reasons over both. It may extract values from charts, understand diagrams, inspect screenshots, compare visual elements, and combine those observations with accompanying text.

Multimodal RAG extends the standard indexing, retrieval, and synthesis stages to images. Depending on the application, input, retrieved context, synthesis, and final output can include text or images.

vii. Metadata and Provenance Manager

Metadata preserves the relationship between a modality and its original source. Important fields include document ID, page number, section, image ID, file path, caption, coordinates, timestamp, and document version.

Provenance is essential because the final answer may combine a value extracted from a chart with an explanation taken from a paragraph. Each claim should indicate which modality and source supported it.

e) Types of Multimodal RAG

i. Text-to-Image RAG
The user submits a text question, and the retriever searches for semantically related images. The image and its metadata are then supplied to a multimodal model.

ii.Image-to-Text RAG
The user provides an image, and the system retrieves related textual descriptions, manuals, policies, or knowledge articles. This is useful when a screenshot, product photograph, or technical diagram must be connected to supporting documentation.

iii. Text-and-Image Retrieval

The system searches both text and image collections for one query. The final response is generated from the combined text and visual evidence.

This pattern is common for complex PDFs and slide presentations where part of the answer exists in the narrative text and another part exists in a chart, figure, or table.


iv. Caption-Based Multimodal RAG

Images are converted into captions or detailed descriptions during ingestion. Those captions are indexed as text, and the corresponding image is retrieved whenever its caption matches the user’s question.

Caption-based retrieval can use an ordinary text vector store, making implementation simpler. However, any visual information omitted from the caption becomes unavailable to the retriever.

v. Native-Image Multimodal RAG

Native images are indexed using image embeddings and supplied directly to the multimodal model. This preserves visual details such as layout, colour, shapes, chart trends, diagram links, and screenshot components.

Native-image retrieval provides richer evidence but requires image storage, visual embedding models, secure image access, and a multimodal model capable of processing retrieved visual content.

vi. Audio and Video RAG

Audio RAG transcribes recordings and may also preserve speaker identity, timestamps, tone, and acoustic events. Video RAG may extract transcripts, scene descriptions, key frames, objects, and temporal segments.

Retrieval can return both a transcript segment and the corresponding time range or video frame. This allows the system to answer using spoken and visual evidence while citing the relevant timestamp.

f) Code Example

from typing import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE TEXT KNOWLEDGE
# =========================================================

text_knowledge_base = [
    {
        "id": "TEXT-001",
        "content": (
            "Revenue increased after the launch of Product Nova."
        ),
        "source": "quarterly_report_2026.txt",
        "page": 5,
        "keywords": [
            "revenue",
            "increase",
            "product nova",
            "launch",
        ],
    },
    {
        "id": "TEXT-002",
        "content": (
            "The employee learning programme was expanded "
            "during the final quarter."
        ),
        "source": "quarterly_report_2026.txt",
        "page": 8,
        "keywords": [
            "learning",
            "employee",
            "programme",
        ],
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 2. CREATE IMAGE KNOWLEDGE
# =========================================================

image_knowledge_base = [
    {
        "id": "IMAGE-001",
        "file_name": "quarterly_revenue_chart.png",
        "description": (
            "A quarterly revenue bar chart showing "
            "Q1 revenue of 12 million, "
            "Q2 revenue of 15 million, "
            "Q3 revenue of 19 million, and "
            "Q4 revenue of 24 million."
        ),
        "source": "quarterly_report_2026.pdf",
        "page": 6,
        "keywords": [
            "revenue",
            "q1",
            "q2",
            "q3",
            "q4",
            "chart",
            "24 million",
        ],
    },
    {
        "id": "IMAGE-002",
        "file_name": "employee_training_photo.png",
        "description": (
            "A photograph showing employees attending "
            "a classroom training session."
        ),
        "source": "quarterly_report_2026.pdf",
        "page": 8,
        "keywords": [
            "employee",
            "training",
            "classroom",
        ],
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class MultimodalRAGState(TypedDict):
    question: str

    retrieved_texts: list[dict]
    retrieved_images: list[dict]

    combined_evidence: list[dict]
    final_answer: str


# =========================================================
# 4. CREATE A SIMPLE RELEVANCE SCORE
# =========================================================

def calculate_keyword_score(
    question: str,
    keywords: list[str],
) -> int:
    """
    Count how many record keywords occur in the question.

    A production application can replace this with text,
    image, or cross-modal embeddings.
    """

    normalized_question = question.lower()

    return sum(
        keyword.lower() in normalized_question
        for keyword in keywords
    )


# =========================================================
# 5. RETRIEVE RELEVANT TEXT
# =========================================================

def retrieve_text_node(
    state: MultimodalRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Search the text knowledge base.
    """

    scored_records = []

    for record in text_knowledge_base:
        score = calculate_keyword_score(
            question=state["question"],
            keywords=record["keywords"],
        )

        if score > 0:
            scored_records.append(
                {
                    **record,
                    "modality": "text",
                    "score": score,
                }
            )

    scored_records.sort(
        key=lambda item: item["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_texts": scored_records[:2],
    }


# =========================================================
# 6. RETRIEVE RELEVANT IMAGES
# =========================================================

def retrieve_image_node(
    state: MultimodalRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Search image descriptions and metadata.

    In a native multimodal implementation, this node could
    use CLIP or another vision-language embedding model.
    """

    scored_records = []

    for record in image_knowledge_base:
        score = calculate_keyword_score(
            question=state["question"],
            keywords=record["keywords"],
        )

        if score > 0:
            scored_records.append(
                {
                    **record,
                    "modality": "image",
                    "score": score,
                }
            )

    scored_records.sort(
        key=lambda item: item["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_images": scored_records[:2],
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. COMBINE TEXT AND IMAGE EVIDENCE
# =========================================================

def combine_evidence_node(
    state: MultimodalRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Merge evidence from both modalities.
    """

    combined_evidence = (
        state["retrieved_texts"]
        + state["retrieved_images"]
    )

    combined_evidence.sort(
        key=lambda item: item["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return {
        "combined_evidence": combined_evidence,
    }


# =========================================================
# 8. FIND A FACT IN RETRIEVED EVIDENCE
# =========================================================

def find_evidence_containing(
    evidence: list[dict],
    phrase: str,
) -> dict | None:
    """
    Find the first evidence item containing a phrase.
    """

    phrase_lower = phrase.lower()

    for item in evidence:
        searchable_content = (
            item.get("content", "")
            + " "
            + item.get("description", "")
        ).lower()

        if phrase_lower in searchable_content:
            return item

    return None


# =========================================================
# 9. GENERATE A MULTIMODAL ANSWER
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: MultimodalRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Generate a response from both text and image evidence.
    """

    evidence = state["combined_evidence"]

    revenue_chart = find_evidence_containing(
        evidence=evidence,
        phrase="q4 revenue of 24 million",
    )

    product_launch_text = find_evidence_containing(
        evidence=evidence,
        phrase="launch of product nova",
    )

    if (
        revenue_chart is None
        and product_launch_text is None
    ):
        return {
            "final_answer": (
                "The requested information was not found in "
                "the available text or image evidence."
            )
        }

    answer_parts = []
    source_parts = []

    if revenue_chart is not None:
        answer_parts.append(
            "The revenue chart shows that Q4 revenue "
            "was 24 million."
        )

        source_parts.append(
            (
                f"Image source: "
                f"{revenue_chart['source']}, "
                f"page {revenue_chart['page']}, "
                f"file {revenue_chart['file_name']}"
            )
        )

    if product_launch_text is not None:
        answer_parts.append(
            "The report associates the revenue increase "
            "with the launch of Product Nova."
        )

        source_parts.append(
            (
                f"Text source: "
                f"{product_launch_text['source']}, "
                f"page {product_launch_text['page']}"
            )
        )

    final_answer = (
        " ".join(answer_parts)
        + "\n\nSources:\n"
        + "\n".join(
            f"{index}. {source}"
            for index, source in enumerate(
                source_parts,
                start=1,
            )
        )
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": final_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

workflow = StateGraph(
    MultimodalRAGState
)

workflow.add_node(
    "retrieve_text",
    retrieve_text_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "retrieve_images",
    retrieve_image_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "combine_evidence",
    combine_evidence_node,
)

workflow.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)


workflow.add_edge(
    START,
    "retrieve_text",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "retrieve_text",
    "retrieve_images",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "retrieve_images",
    "combine_evidence",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "combine_evidence",
    "generate_answer",
)

workflow.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)


multimodal_rag_graph = workflow.compile()


# =========================================================
# 11. RUN THE MULTIMODAL RAG WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

def run_multimodal_rag(
    question: str,
) -> None:
    initial_state: MultimodalRAGState = {
        "question": question,
        "retrieved_texts": [],
        "retrieved_images": [],
        "combined_evidence": [],
        "final_answer": "",
    }

    result = multimodal_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("RETRIEVED TEXT EVIDENCE")
    print("=" * 70)

    if result["retrieved_texts"]:
        for item in result["retrieved_texts"]:
            print(f"\nID: {item['id']}")
            print(f"Content: {item['content']}")
            print(f"Source: {item['source']}")
            print(f"Page: {item['page']}")
            print(f"Score: {item['score']}")
    else:
        print("No text evidence was retrieved.")

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("RETRIEVED IMAGE EVIDENCE")
    print("=" * 70)

    if result["retrieved_images"]:
        for item in result["retrieved_images"]:
            print(f"\nID: {item['id']}")
            print(f"File: {item['file_name']}")
            print(f"Description: {item['description']}")
            print(f"Source: {item['source']}")
            print(f"Page: {item['page']}")
            print(f"Score: {item['score']}")
    else:
        print("No image evidence was retrieved.")

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("FINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 12. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_multimodal_rag(
        "What was the Q4 revenue, and what caused the "
        "revenue increase after the Product Nova launch?"
    )

g) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Multimodal RAG?

Multimodal RAG is an advanced RAG architecture that retrieves and reasons over multiple types of information, such as text, images, charts, tables, audio, and video.

The pipeline can accept text or media as input, retrieve both text and visual evidence, and provide that evidence to a multimodal model for grounded response generation.

Q2. Why is text-only RAG insufficient for some enterprise documents?

Many enterprise documents contain important information in charts, diagrams, screenshots, tables, scanned forms, and page layouts. Plain-text extraction may omit these elements or remove the structural relationship between them.

For example, a paragraph may explain the cause of revenue growth, while the exact quarterly amount appears only in a chart. A text-only retriever cannot reliably answer the complete question.

Q3. What are the main components of Multimodal RAG?

The main components are a multimodal parser, OCR or vision model, text and image nodes, modality-specific embeddings, multimodal vector stores, a cross-modal retriever, Re-Ranking, a multimodal context builder, a multimodal LLM, and source-provenance management.

Production systems additionally require modality-specific security checks, caching, media storage, score normalization, evaluation, and access control.

Q4. What is cross-modal retrieval?

Cross-modal retrieval allows a query in one modality to retrieve information stored in another modality. For example, a text question can retrieve a relevant chart, or an uploaded screenshot can retrieve a related troubleshooting document.

It is commonly implemented using a shared vision-language embedding space or by generating textual captions for visual content and indexing those captions.

Q5. What is the difference between OCR and Multimodal RAG?

OCR converts visible characters in images or scanned documents into text. It is useful for extracting words and numbers but does not necessarily understand chart trends, diagrams, photographs, colours, or spatial relationships.

Multimodal RAG can use OCR as one ingestion component while also preserving native images and reasoning over visual structure through multimodal models.

Q6. How are text and images stored in Multimodal RAG?

Text and images may be stored in separate vector collections using modality-specific embeddings. Metadata connects each item to its source document, page, caption, section, and related content.

Alternatively, a shared cross-modal embedding model can place text and images in one semantic space. LlamaIndex supports separate text and image vector stores within a multimodal storage context.

Q7. How do you evaluate Multimodal RAG?

Evaluation should measure text retrieval quality, image retrieval quality, cross-modal evidence coverage, visual Groundedness, modality-attribution accuracy, answer completeness, Faithfulness, and Answer Relevance.

The retriever and generator should be evaluated separately. A correct image may be retrieved but interpreted incorrectly, while the model may reason correctly from an irrelevant image.

Q8. What is caption-based image retrieval?

Caption-based retrieval generates a textual description for each image and indexes that description using an ordinary text embedding model. When the caption matches the user’s question, the system retrieves both the caption and corresponding image.

It is simple to implement but depends heavily on caption quality. Information omitted from the generated caption may not be discoverable through text retrieval.

Q9. What are the main risks of Multimodal RAG?

The main risks are OCR errors, incorrect image interpretation, detached captions, missing page relationships, cross-modal score imbalance, conflicting modalities, sensitive visual information, and high processing cost.

Every important visual claim should be linked to its native evidence and verified for labels, values, units, time periods, and source metadata.

Q10. When should Multimodal RAG be used?

Multimodal RAG should be used when users need answers from visually rich files such as financial reports, presentations, technical manuals, diagrams, scanned contracts, research papers, product catalogues, audio, or video.

It may be unnecessary when the knowledge base consists entirely of clean text and visual evidence does not affect answer quality. The added complexity should be justified through retrieval and response evaluation.


Topic 5: Federated RAG

a) Definition

Federated RAG is a distributed Retrieval-Augmented Generation architecture that retrieves information from multiple autonomous knowledge sources without requiring all source data to be consolidated into one central repository. A central coordinator sends the query to selected sources and combines their results for generation.

Each participating source can retain control over its own documents, indexes, retrieval technology, access policies, and update schedule. One source may use vector search, another may expose SQL, while another may provide a knowledge graph, enterprise search API, or SaaS connector.

Federated RAG is more than sending every query to several retrievers. A complete architecture includes source discovery, source selection, query routing, local retrieval, policy enforcement, score normalization, cross-source Re-Ranking, evidence fusion, provenance tracking, and grounded generation.

The term can sometimes refer to systems combining Federated Learning with RAG, particularly in research on privacy-sensitive distributed clients. In enterprise architecture, however, it commonly refers more broadly to federated retrieval across autonomous and policy-controlled information sources

b) Purpose

The main purpose of Federated RAG is to provide a unified natural-language interface over enterprise information that cannot be stored in one location. Knowledge may be distributed across content-management systems, databases, data lakes, knowledge graphs, regional platforms, and departmental repositories.

Federated RAG preserves source autonomy. Departments can continue using their own storage and indexing systems while exposing a controlled retrieval interface to the federated coordinator. This avoids forcing every source owner to migrate data into one common vector store.

It supports privacy, data sovereignty, and access-control requirements by allowing information to remain within its original trust boundary. Each source can apply local authorization and return only evidence that the requesting user is permitted to access.

Federated RAG is useful when data is heterogeneous, rapidly changing, independently managed, or subject to different governance requirements. The trade-off is additional complexity in routing, latency control, score comparison, failure handling, evidence deduplication, and cross-source security.

c) Architecture Diagram

                    FEDERATED RAG ARCHITECTURE
                    ==========================

                         User Question
                               |
                               v
                    Identity and Access Context
                  User | Role | Region | Department
                               |
                               v
                   Federated Query Coordinator
                               |
                               v
                       Source Selection
              Which eligible sources may contain evidence?
                               |
             +-----------------+-----------------+
             |                 |                 |
             v                 v                 v
      HR Gateway         Project Gateway      IT Gateway
             |                 |                 |
             v                 v                 v
      HR Retriever       Project Retriever    IT Retriever
      Vector Search      SQL or Search API    Hybrid Search
             |                 |                 |
             v                 v                 v
      Local Policy       Local Policy        Local Policy
       Enforcement        Enforcement         Enforcement
             |                 |                 |
             +-----------------+-----------------+
                               |
                               v
                    Partial Retrieval Results
                               |
                               v
                 Normalize Scores and Metadata
                               |
                               v
                  Cross-Source Re-Ranking
                               |
                               v
                 Deduplicate and Fuse Evidence
                               |
                               v
                         LLM Generator
                               |
                               v
             Groundedness and Attribution Validation
                               |
                               v
                   Final Answer with Sources

d) Core Components

1. Federated Query Coordinator

The coordinator orchestrates the complete request. It receives the question, determines eligible sources, dispatches retrieval operations, manages timeouts, collects partial results, and sends the final evidence set to the answer generator.

It should not automatically receive unrestricted access to every repository. Instead, it sends the user’s verified authorization context to each source, and each source independently decides what evidence can be returned.

2. Source Registry
The Source Registry describes the available repositories and their capabilities. It can store each source’s domain, endpoint, retrieval method, supported query types, expected latency, access requirements, region, and health status.

3. Source Selector and Query Router
The Source Selector chooses the repositories required for the current question. Selection can use deterministic rules, semantic classification, an LLM with structured output, or a hybrid of these methods.

A compound request may select several sources. For example, a question about project ownership and certification reimbursement requires both the project and HR sources, while an MFA question should normally select only the IT repository.

4.Local Retrieval Gateways
Each source exposes a controlled retrieval interface. The gateway translates a common federated request into the source’s native operation, such as vector search, BM25, SQL, Graph Query, or an authenticated API call.

Gateways isolate source-specific implementation details from the central coordinator. A department can change its internal search technology without forcing the entire federated application to change its query interface.

5. Local Policy Enforcement
Every source applies authorization, tenant isolation, region, data classification, document status, and field-level restrictions locally. The source should not assume that a request is authorized merely because it came from the central coordinator.

Local enforcement is important when sources belong to different trust domains. Federated enterprise architectures emphasize source autonomy, fine-grained authorization, and policy-aware evidence exchange.

6. Result Normalizer
Different retrievers may produce incompatible results. A vector store may return cosine similarity, BM25 may return an unbounded lexical score, and a database may return an exact record without any relevance score.

The normalizer converts results into a common evidence format containing content, normalized relevance, source, timestamp, authorization status, document identifier, and retrieval method.

7. Cross-Source Re-Ranker
A Cross-Source Re-Ranker compares evidence returned by different repositories and produces one combined ranking. It considers query relevance, source authority, freshness, authorization, and sometimes evidence diversity.

Raw local scores should not be compared directly unless they have been calibrated. A score of 0.85 from a cosine-similarity retriever does not necessarily mean the same thing as 0.85 from another model or search system.

8. Evidence Fusion Layer
The fusion layer removes duplicate passages, combines complementary facts, identifies conflicts, and preserves provenance. It should avoid presenting the same policy copied across three repositories as three independent pieces of confirming evidence.

If equally authoritative sources conflict, the system should apply rules based on status, version, effective date, jurisdiction, or ownership. It should expose unresolved conflicts rather than selecting one result silently.

9. Grounded Generator
The generator receives the user question and normalized federated evidence. It creates one readable answer while distinguishing facts obtained from different repositories.

Every important claim should retain source attribution. A generated answer should not combine two source statements in a way that changes their meaning or removes an important regional or departmental condition.

e) Types of Federated RAG

1. Brokered Federated RAG
A central broker selects data sources, dispatches requests, collects results, and constructs the final context. The broker has a global view of available source capabilities but does not necessarily store their raw data.

2. Hierarchical Federated RAG
A top-level coordinator routes the request to regional or departmental coordinators, which then select local repositories. This architecture can scale across large organizations containing several independent business units.

3. Peer-to-Peer Federated RAG
Sources communicate or forward queries without relying on one permanent central broker. Each node may determine whether it can answer the question or whether another participant should receive the request.

Peer-to-peer designs avoid a single central coordinator but are harder to govern, observe, secure, and terminate. Query loops and inconsistent policies become important operational risks.

4. Privacy-Preserving Federated RAG
Privacy-preserving federation minimizes the raw information exchanged among participants. Sources may return approved excerpts, embeddings, encrypted results, summaries, or locally generated answers instead of complete documents.

Research on Federated RAG also considers its relationship with Federated Learning, secure aggregation, distributed clients, cross-client heterogeneity, and privacy-sensitive retrieval.

5. Agentic Federated RAG
An agent dynamically decides which sources to query, how to decompose the request, whether another retrieval step is required, and how to respond when a source is unavailable.

Agentic routing provides flexibility but requires strict tool allowlists, authorization, maximum iterations, timeout controls, state tracking, and validation of evidence returned by each source.

f) Code Example

from typing import Literal, TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. AUTONOMOUS PROJECT REPOSITORY
# =========================================================

project_repository = {
    "project orion": {
        "id": "PROJECT-001",
        "project": "Project Orion",
        "department": "Data Engineering",
        "source": "project_registry_2026.txt",
        "required_roles": [
            "employee",
            "manager",
        ],
        "local_score": 100.0,
        "maximum_score": 100.0,
        "authority_score": 0.95,
        "available": True,
    }
}


# =========================================================
# 2. AUTONOMOUS HR REPOSITORY
# =========================================================

hr_repository = {
    "data engineering": {
        "id": "HR-001",
        "department": "Data Engineering",
        "policy": "Advanced Learning Policy",
        "annual_limit": "25,000 rupees",
        "period": "per financial year",
        "approval": (
            "Manager approval is required before registration."
        ),
        "source": "hr_learning_policy_2026.txt",
        "required_roles": [
            "employee",
            "manager",
        ],
        "local_score": 15.0,
        "maximum_score": 20.0,
        "authority_score": 1.0,
        "available": True,
    }
}


# =========================================================
# 3. COMMON FEDERATED EVIDENCE FORMAT
# =========================================================

class FederatedEvidence(TypedDict):
    source_system: str
    source_id: str
    source_name: str
    content: str

    local_score: float
    normalized_score: float
    authority_score: float
    federated_score: float


# =========================================================
# 4. LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class FederatedState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    user_role: str

    need_project_source: bool
    need_hr_source: bool

    project_name: str
    department: str

    project_evidence: list[FederatedEvidence]
    hr_evidence: list[FederatedEvidence]

    source_errors: list[str]
    fused_evidence: list[FederatedEvidence]

    answer_complete: bool
    final_answer: str


# =========================================================
# 5. AUTHORIZATION HELPER
# =========================================================

def is_authorized(
    user_role: str,
    required_roles: list[str],
) -> bool:
    """
    Check whether the local repository permits the user role.
    """

    return user_role in required_roles


# =========================================================
# 6. SCORE NORMALIZATION
# =========================================================

def normalize_score(
    local_score: float,
    maximum_score: float,
) -> float:
    """
    Convert a local source score to the range 0 to 1.
    """

    if maximum_score <= 0:
        return 0.0

    normalized_score = (
        local_score / maximum_score
    )

    return max(
        0.0,
        min(normalized_score, 1.0),
    )


# =========================================================
# 7. FEDERATED SCORE
# =========================================================

def calculate_federated_score(
    normalized_score: float,
    authority_score: float,
) -> float:
    """
    Combine relevance and source authority.
    """

    score = (
        0.70 * normalized_score
        + 0.30 * authority_score
    )

    return round(score, 4)


# =========================================================
# 8. QUERY PLANNER
# =========================================================

def plan_query_node(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Determine which repositories are required.
    """

    question = state["question"].lower()

    need_project_source = any(
        term in question
        for term in [
            "project orion",
            "project",
            "owner",
            "department",
        ]
    )

    need_hr_source = any(
        term in question
        for term in [
            "certification",
            "reimbursement",
            "learning policy",
        ]
    )

    project_name = (
        "Project Orion"
        if "project orion" in question
        else ""
    )

    return {
        "need_project_source": need_project_source,
        "need_hr_source": need_hr_source,
        "project_name": project_name,
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. ROUTE AFTER PLANNING
# =========================================================

def route_after_planning(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> Literal[
    "retrieve_project",
    "retrieve_hr",
    "fuse_evidence",
]:
    if state["need_project_source"]:
        return "retrieve_project"

    if state["need_hr_source"]:
        return "retrieve_hr"

    return "fuse_evidence"


# =========================================================
# 10. PROJECT REPOSITORY RETRIEVER
# =========================================================

def retrieve_project_node(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve the project owner from the autonomous
    Project Repository.
    """

    project_key = state["project_name"].lower()

    record = project_repository.get(
        project_key
    )

    if record is None:
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + ["Project record was not found."]
            )
        }

    if not record["available"]:
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + ["Project Repository is unavailable."]
            )
        }

    if not is_authorized(
        user_role=state["user_role"],
        required_roles=record["required_roles"],
    ):
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + ["Project Repository access was denied."]
            )
        }

    normalized_score = normalize_score(
        local_score=record["local_score"],
        maximum_score=record["maximum_score"],
    )

    evidence: FederatedEvidence = {
        "source_system": "Project Repository",
        "source_id": record["id"],
        "source_name": record["source"],
        "content": (
            f"{record['project']} is owned by the "
            f"{record['department']} department."
        ),
        "local_score": record["local_score"],
        "normalized_score": normalized_score,
        "authority_score": record["authority_score"],
        "federated_score": calculate_federated_score(
            normalized_score=normalized_score,
            authority_score=record["authority_score"],
        ),
    }

    return {
        "department": record["department"],
        "project_evidence": [evidence],
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. ROUTE AFTER PROJECT RETRIEVAL
# =========================================================

def route_after_project(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> Literal[
    "retrieve_hr",
    "fuse_evidence",
]:
    if state["need_hr_source"]:
        return "retrieve_hr"

    return "fuse_evidence"


# =========================================================
# 12. HR REPOSITORY RETRIEVER
# =========================================================

def retrieve_hr_node(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve the department policy from HR.

    The department comes from the Project Repository,
    demonstrating dependent federated retrieval.
    """

    if not state["department"]:
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + [
                    "HR retrieval could not continue because "
                    "the department was not identified."
                ]
            )
        }

    department_key = state["department"].lower()

    record = hr_repository.get(
        department_key
    )

    if record is None:
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + [
                    f"No HR policy was found for "
                    f"{state['department']}."
                ]
            )
        }

    if not record["available"]:
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + ["HR Repository is unavailable."]
            )
        }

    if not is_authorized(
        user_role=state["user_role"],
        required_roles=record["required_roles"],
    ):
        return {
            "source_errors": (
                state["source_errors"]
                + ["HR Repository access was denied."]
            )
        }

    normalized_score = normalize_score(
        local_score=record["local_score"],
        maximum_score=record["maximum_score"],
    )

    evidence: FederatedEvidence = {
        "source_system": "HR Repository",
        "source_id": record["id"],
        "source_name": record["source"],
        "content": (
            f"The {record['department']} department follows "
            f"the {record['policy']}. Employees may claim "
            f"up to {record['annual_limit']} "
            f"{record['period']} for approved professional "
            f"certifications. {record['approval']}"
        ),
        "local_score": record["local_score"],
        "normalized_score": normalized_score,
        "authority_score": record["authority_score"],
        "federated_score": calculate_federated_score(
            normalized_score=normalized_score,
            authority_score=record["authority_score"],
        ),
    }

    return {
        "hr_evidence": [evidence],
    }


# =========================================================
# 13. FUSE AND RE-RANK EVIDENCE
# =========================================================

def fuse_evidence_node(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Combine source results, remove duplicates, and rank
    them using the common federated score.
    """

    all_evidence = (
        state["project_evidence"]
        + state["hr_evidence"]
    )

    unique_evidence = {}

    for item in all_evidence:
        evidence_key = (
            item["source_system"],
            item["source_id"],
        )

        unique_evidence[evidence_key] = item

    fused_evidence = list(
        unique_evidence.values()
    )

    fused_evidence.sort(
        key=lambda item: item["federated_score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return {
        "fused_evidence": fused_evidence,
    }


# =========================================================
# 14. CHECK EVIDENCE COMPLETENESS
# =========================================================

def check_evidence_node(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Check whether each required repository returned evidence.
    """

    project_requirement_met = (
        not state["need_project_source"]
        or bool(state["project_evidence"])
    )

    hr_requirement_met = (
        not state["need_hr_source"]
        or bool(state["hr_evidence"])
    )

    return {
        "answer_complete": (
            project_requirement_met
            and hr_requirement_met
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 15. GENERATE FINAL ANSWER
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: FederatedState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Generate a response using authorized federated evidence.
    """

    if not state["fused_evidence"]:
        details = (
            "\n".join(state["source_errors"])
            if state["source_errors"]
            else "No matching evidence was found."
        )

        return {
            "final_answer": (
                "No authorized evidence was available.\n\n"
                f"Details:\n{details}"
            )
        }

    evidence_text = " ".join(
        item["content"]
        for item in state["fused_evidence"]
    )

    source_text = "\n".join(
        (
            f"{index}. {item['source_system']}: "
            f"{item['source_name']} "
            f"[{item['source_id']}]"
        )
        for index, item in enumerate(
            state["fused_evidence"],
            start=1,
        )
    )

    final_answer = (
        f"{evidence_text}\n\n"
        f"Sources:\n{source_text}"
    )

    if not state["answer_complete"]:
        final_answer += (
            "\n\nWarning: This is a partial answer because "
            "one or more required repositories did not return "
            "authorized evidence."
        )

    if state["source_errors"]:
        error_text = "\n".join(
            f"- {error}"
            for error in state["source_errors"]
        )

        final_answer += (
            f"\n\nSource issues:\n{error_text}"
        )

    return {
        "final_answer": final_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 16. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

builder = StateGraph(FederatedState)

builder.add_node(
    "plan_query",
    plan_query_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_project",
    retrieve_project_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_hr",
    retrieve_hr_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "fuse_evidence",
    fuse_evidence_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "check_evidence",
    check_evidence_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)


builder.add_edge(
    START,
    "plan_query",
)

builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "plan_query",
    route_after_planning,
    {
        "retrieve_project": "retrieve_project",
        "retrieve_hr": "retrieve_hr",
        "fuse_evidence": "fuse_evidence",
    },
)

builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "retrieve_project",
    route_after_project,
    {
        "retrieve_hr": "retrieve_hr",
        "fuse_evidence": "fuse_evidence",
    },
)

builder.add_edge(
    "retrieve_hr",
    "fuse_evidence",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "fuse_evidence",
    "check_evidence",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "check_evidence",
    "generate_answer",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)

federated_rag_graph = builder.compile()


# =========================================================
# 17. RUN THE WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

def run_federated_rag(
    question: str,
    user_role: str,
) -> None:
    initial_state: FederatedState = {
        "question": question,
        "user_role": user_role,

        "need_project_source": False,
        "need_hr_source": False,

        "project_name": "",
        "department": "",

        "project_evidence": [],
        "hr_evidence": [],

        "source_errors": [],
        "fused_evidence": [],

        "answer_complete": False,
        "final_answer": "",
    }

    result = federated_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\nSOURCE PLAN")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(
        "Project Repository:",
        result["need_project_source"],
    )
    print(
        "HR Repository:",
        result["need_hr_source"],
    )

    print("\nINTERMEDIATE RESULT")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(
        "Department:",
        result["department"] or "Not identified",
    )

    print("\nFEDERATED EVIDENCE")
    print("-" * 70)

    for item in result["fused_evidence"]:
        print(
            f"\nSource: {item['source_system']}"
        )
        print(
            f"Local score: {item['local_score']}"
        )
        print(
            f"Normalized score: "
            f"{item['normalized_score']}"
        )
        print(
            f"Federated score: "
            f"{item['federated_score']}"
        )
        print(
            f"Content: {item['content']}"
        )

    print("\nEVIDENCE COMPLETE")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(result["answer_complete"])

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 18. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_federated_rag(
        question=(
            "Which department owns Project Orion, and what "
            "certification reimbursement policy applies "
            "to that department?"
        ),
        user_role="employee",
    )


g) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Federated RAG?

Federated RAG is a distributed RAG architecture that retrieves evidence from multiple autonomous knowledge sources without centralizing all raw enterprise data.

A coordinator selects relevant sources, sends retrieval requests, collects authorized results, normalizes and fuses the evidence, and generates one grounded response with source attribution.

Q2. How is Federated RAG different from centralized RAG?

Centralized RAG stores documents in one main repository and generally uses one retrieval and authorization system.

Federated RAG leaves information in independently managed sources. It coordinates retrieval across those repositories while preserving local permissions, schemas, update cycles, and data-residency requirements.

Q3. How are results from different retrievers combined?

Each source returns a common evidence object containing its content, source metadata, local score, authority, and authorization status.

Local scores are normalized or converted into ranks. The coordinator can then use weighted fusion, Reciprocal Rank Fusion, or a global Re-Ranker to create one combined ranking.

Q4. How does Federated RAG enforce security?

The coordinator obtains trusted user identity information and sends delegated authorization context to selected repositories.

Every repository independently applies its local permissions. Only authorized evidence is returned, and the coordinator preserves tenant, region, source, and classification metadata throughout generation.

Q5. How should source failures be handled?

The coordinator can use timeouts, bounded retries, circuit breakers, approved fallback sources, or valid cached results.

If a required source remains unavailable, the system should return a clearly labelled partial answer or abstain. It must not invent the information that the failed source was expected to provide.


Topic 6: Real-Time RAG

i) Definition

Real-Time RAG is a RAG architecture that continuously ingests, processes, indexes, and retrieves newly created or updated information with minimal delay. New events become searchable within seconds or minutes instead of waiting for a scheduled daily or weekly indexing job.

It commonly uses event-streaming platforms, Change Data Capture, stream processors, embedding services, and vector databases that support incremental updates. An event-driven pipeline may consume live records, generate embeddings, and upsert them into a searchable index as they arrive.

Real-Time RAG is not defined only by fast answer generation. It requires both fresh knowledge ingestion and low-latency retrieval. A system that responds quickly from yesterday’s index is fast RAG, but it is not necessarily Real-Time RAG.

The term usually means near-real-time operation because every pipeline has some delay. The important measurement is freshness lag, which represents the time between a source update and that update becoming available to retrieval.

ii) Purpose

Prevent Stale Answers
In ordinary batch RAG, a document updated after the last indexing job may not appear in retrieval. Real-Time RAG reduces this gap by detecting and indexing changes continuously.

Support Dynamic Enterprise Information
It is useful for information that changes frequently, such as project status, service incidents, application availability, inventory, tickets, transactions, and operational alerts.

Reduce Reindexing Cost
Instead of rebuilding an entire index after every update, the pipeline incrementally inserts, updates, or deletes only the affected records.

Enable Immediate Evidence-Based Decisions
Users can ask questions about recent events and receive answers grounded in the latest indexed facts. AWS describes real-time streaming RAG architectures using Change Data Capture, streaming services, processing pipelines, embedding stores, and generative models.

iii) Architectural Diagram

                    REAL-TIME RAG ARCHITECTURE
                    ==========================

                    Live Enterprise Sources
             Databases | APIs | Logs | Events | Files
                              |
                              v
                   Change and Event Capture
                 CDC | Webhooks | Message Events
                              |
                              v
                     Message Broker
                    Kafka | Event Stream
                              |
                              v
                     Stream Processor
           Validate | Clean | Chunk | Enrich | Deduplicate
                              |
                              v
                     Embedding Service
                              |
                              v
                   Incremental Index Update
                  Insert | Update | Delete
                              |
                              v
                      Vector Database
                    Vectors + Metadata
                              |
                         User Question
                              |
                              v
                      Freshness-Aware
                         Retrieval
                              |
                              v
                    Re-Ranking and Filtering
                              |
                              v
                       Grounded Generator
                              |
                              v
              Answer + Sources + Freshness Timestamp


iv) Core Components

1. Change Data Capture or Event Producer
Detects changes in the original source and publishes structured events. Each event should contain a record identifier, operation type, content, version, source, and event timestamp.

2. 2. Message Broker
Buffers incoming events and decouples ingestion from indexing. Kafka-compatible platforms are commonly used for high-throughput streaming pipelines because consumers can process events independently and replay them when required.

3. Stream Processor
Cleans and validates events, removes duplicates, handles ordering, creates chunks, attaches metadata, and calls the embedding model.

4. Incremental Indexer
Applies individual inserts, updates, and deletions without rebuilding the entire index. It must ensure that an older delayed event does not overwrite a newer record version.

5. Freshness-Aware Retriever
Retrieves semantically relevant evidence while considering version, event time, source authority, and active status. When duplicate versions exist, the latest valid version should normally be preferred.

6. Grounded Generator
Generates an answer from the selected current evidence and includes source and freshness metadata. If the latest information cannot be verified, it should not present an older cached value as current without a warning.

v) Code Example

from datetime import datetime
from typing import Literal, TypedDict

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. CREATE THE REAL-TIME KNOWLEDGE INDEX
# =========================================================

real_time_index: dict[str, dict] = {}


# =========================================================
# 2. DEFINE THE EVENT TYPE
# =========================================================

class SourceEvent(TypedDict):
    operation: Literal[
        "insert",
        "update",
        "delete",
    ]

    record_id: str
    entity_name: str
    content: str
    source: str
    version: int
    event_time: str


# =========================================================
# 3. DEFINE THE LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class RealTimeRAGState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    event: SourceEvent | None

    event_status: str
    retrieved_records: list[dict]

    final_answer: str


# =========================================================
# 4. VALIDATE AND PROCESS THE INCOMING EVENT
# =========================================================

def process_event_node(
    state: RealTimeRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Insert, update, or delete a record in the live index.

    Older events are ignored so they cannot overwrite
    more recent information.
    """

    event = state["event"]

    if event is None:
        return {
            "event_status": (
                "No new event was supplied."
            )
        }

    record_id = event["record_id"]
    operation = event["operation"]

    existing_record = real_time_index.get(
        record_id
    )

    # -----------------------------------------------------
    # Handle delete events.
    # -----------------------------------------------------

    if operation == "delete":
        if existing_record is None:
            return {
                "event_status": (
                    f"Record {record_id} was already absent."
                )
            }

        if (
            event["version"]
            <= existing_record["version"]
        ):
            return {
                "event_status": (
                    "Outdated delete event was ignored."
                )
            }

        del real_time_index[record_id]

        return {
            "event_status": (
                f"Record {record_id} was deleted."
            )
        }

    # -----------------------------------------------------
    # Ignore an old or duplicate event.
    # -----------------------------------------------------

    if (
        existing_record is not None
        and event["version"]
        <= existing_record["version"]
    ):
        return {
            "event_status": (
                f"Event version {event['version']} was ignored. "
                f"The index already contains version "
                f"{existing_record['version']}."
            )
        }

    # -----------------------------------------------------
    # Perform insert or update.
    # -----------------------------------------------------

    real_time_index[record_id] = {
        "record_id": record_id,
        "entity_name": event["entity_name"],
        "content": event["content"],
        "source": event["source"],
        "version": event["version"],
        "event_time": event["event_time"],
        "indexed_at": datetime.now().isoformat(
            timespec="seconds"
        ),
    }

    return {
        "event_status": (
            f"Record {record_id} version "
            f"{event['version']} was indexed."
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 5. RETRIEVE THE LATEST RELEVANT RECORD
# =========================================================

def retrieve_latest_node(
    state: RealTimeRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve current records relevant to the question.

    This educational example uses entity-name matching.
    A production system would normally use vector search,
    metadata filtering, and freshness-aware Re-Ranking.
    """

    question = state["question"].lower()
    matching_records = []

    for record in real_time_index.values():
        entity_name = record[
            "entity_name"
        ].lower()

        if entity_name in question:
            matching_records.append(record)

    matching_records.sort(
        key=lambda item: (
            item["version"],
            item["event_time"],
        ),
        reverse=True,
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_records": matching_records,
    }


# =========================================================
# 6. GENERATE THE FINAL GROUNDED ANSWER
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: RealTimeRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Generate an answer using the latest indexed record.
    """

    if not state["retrieved_records"]:
        return {
            "final_answer": (
                "No current information was found for "
                "the requested entity."
            )
        }

    latest_record = state[
        "retrieved_records"
    ][0]

    final_answer = (
        f"{latest_record['content']}\n\n"
        f"Source: {latest_record['source']}\n"
        f"Record ID: {latest_record['record_id']}\n"
        f"Version: {latest_record['version']}\n"
        f"Source event time: "
        f"{latest_record['event_time']}\n"
        f"Indexed at: {latest_record['indexed_at']}"
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": final_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

builder = StateGraph(RealTimeRAGState)

builder.add_node(
    "process_event",
    process_event_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_latest",
    retrieve_latest_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

builder.add_edge(
    START,
    "process_event",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "process_event",
    "retrieve_latest",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "retrieve_latest",
    "generate_answer",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    END,
)

real_time_rag_graph = builder.compile()


# =========================================================
# 8. RUN ONE REAL-TIME RAG REQUEST
# =========================================================

def run_real_time_rag(
    question: str,
    event: SourceEvent | None,
) -> None:
    initial_state: RealTimeRAGState = {
        "question": question,
        "event": event,

        "event_status": "",
        "retrieved_records": [],

        "final_answer": "",
    }

    result = real_time_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("EVENT PROCESSING")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["event_status"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("QUESTION")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["question"])

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("RETRIEVED RECORDS")
    print("=" * 70)

    if result["retrieved_records"]:
        for record in result[
            "retrieved_records"
        ]:
            print(
                f"ID={record['record_id']}, "
                f"Version={record['version']}, "
                f"Event Time={record['event_time']}"
            )
    else:
        print("No records were retrieved.")

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("FINAL ANSWER")
    print("=" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 9. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # -----------------------------------------------------
    # Initial event:
    # Project Orion is currently in progress.
    # -----------------------------------------------------

    first_event: SourceEvent = {
        "operation": "insert",
        "record_id": "PROJECT-001",
        "entity_name": "Project Orion",
        "content": (
            "Project Orion is currently In Progress."
        ),
        "source": "project_status_stream",
        "version": 1,
        "event_time": "2026-09-09T10:00:00",
    }

    run_real_time_rag(
        question=(
            "What is the current status of Project Orion?"
        ),
        event=first_event,
    )

    print("\n\n")

    # -----------------------------------------------------
    # Real-time update:
    # The project becomes blocked.
    # -----------------------------------------------------

    second_event: SourceEvent = {
        "operation": "update",
        "record_id": "PROJECT-001",
        "entity_name": "Project Orion",
        "content": (
            "Project Orion is currently Blocked because "
            "required approval is pending."
        ),
        "source": "project_status_stream",
        "version": 2,
        "event_time": "2026-09-09T10:05:00",
    }

    run_real_time_rag(
        question=(
            "What is the current status of Project Orion?"
        ),
        event=second_event,
    )

vi) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is Real-Time RAG?

Real-Time RAG is an architecture that continuously captures source changes, processes and indexes them incrementally, and makes updated evidence available for retrieval with minimal delay.

It commonly uses Change Data Capture, event streams, message brokers, stream processors, embedding services, incrementally updated indexes, and freshness-aware retrieval.

Q2. What is the difference between fast RAG and Real-Time RAG?

Fast RAG refers to low query and response latency. It answers quickly, but the underlying index may still contain outdated information.

Real-Time RAG additionally minimizes knowledge freshness lag. It continuously updates the retrieval index so that fast answers are based on recently changed information.

Q3. What is Change Data Capture in Real-Time RAG?

Change Data Capture identifies database inserts, updates, and deletions and publishes these changes as events. The RAG pipeline consumes those events and updates only the affected index records.

This is more efficient than repeatedly scanning and rebuilding the complete corpus. AWS’s real-time streaming RAG architecture uses database migration and Debezium-style CDC connectors to stream database changes into processing services.

Q4. How do you prevent an old event from overwriting new information?

Every event and indexed record should contain a monotonically increasing version number, sequence number, or reliable source timestamp.

The indexer compares the incoming version with the stored version. Older or duplicate events are ignored, making processing idempotent and resistant to out-of-order message delivery.

Q5. How do you evaluate Real-Time RAG?

Important metrics include source-to-index freshness lag, event-processing throughput, update success rate, stale-answer rate, retrieval latency, end-to-end response latency, Context Precision, Context Recall, Faithfulness, and citation correctness.

Chapter 10: Production RAG Systems

Topic 1: Scalable RAG Architecture

a) Definition

Scalable RAG Architecture is a production design that allows a RAG system to handle growing document collections, concurrent users, query volume, and model traffic without unacceptable degradation in latency, reliability, security, or answer quality.

It separates the system into independently scalable services such as ingestion, chunking, embedding, indexing, retrieval, Re-Ranking, generation, caching, and evaluation. This prevents one overloaded component from blocking the complete application.

A scalable design normally contains two major pipelines: an offline or asynchronous ingestion pipeline that prepares knowledge and an online inference pipeline that processes user queries. Microsoft’s RAG architecture guidance similarly separates the data pipeline from the application query flow.

Scalability is not limited to increasing server count. The system must preserve retrieval relevance, authorization, freshness, Groundedness, source attribution, and predictable costs while operating under larger workloads.

b) Purpose

Handle Increasing Traffic
Production RAG applications may receive many concurrent requests. The architecture should scale stateless API, orchestration, retrieval, Re-Ranking, and generation components according to demand without creating one central bottleneck.

Support Large Knowledge Bases
Enterprise collections may contain millions of chunks and embeddings. The retrieval layer must maintain acceptable recall and latency through suitable index design, partitioning, replicas, metadata filtering, and efficient vector-search algorithms.

Improve Reliability
External model APIs, embedding services, vector databases, and document sources may temporarily fail or become slow. A scalable design uses queues, retries, timeouts, circuit breakers, caching, and graceful fallbacks to prevent one service failure from affecting the complete system.

Scale Components Independently
Ingestion, retrieval, and generation have different resource patterns. Embedding is compute-intensive, vector retrieval is memory- and search-intensive, and LLM generation is token- and latency-intensive. Separating these components allows resources to be assigned according to the actual bottleneck.

c) Architecture Diagram

                 SCALABLE PRODUCTION RAG ARCHITECTURE
                 ====================================

                         Enterprise Sources
                  Files | APIs | Databases | Events
                               |
                               v
                    Ingestion API or Connectors
                               |
                               v
                         Message Queue
                               |
              +----------------+----------------+
              |                |                |
              v                v                v
          Parser Workers   Chunking Workers  Metadata Workers
              |                |                |
              +----------------+----------------+
                               |
                               v
                       Embedding Workers
                               |
                               v
                 Vector Index + Document Store
                               |
================================================================

                          User Request
                               |
                               v
                  API Gateway and Authentication
                               |
                               v
                    Load-Balanced RAG API
                               |
                               v
                      Query Orchestrator
                               |
                   +-----------+-----------+
                   |                       |
                   v                       v
              Query Cache              Query Processing
                                           |
                                           v
                                  Distributed Retrieval
                              Keyword + Vector + Filters
                                           |
                                           v
                                      Re-Ranker
                                           |
                                           v
                                     Context Builder
                                           |
                                           v
                                       LLM Gateway
                                           |
                                           v
                                Validation and Citations
                                           |
                                           v
                                      Final Response
                                           |
                                           v
                             Logs | Metrics | Traces | Evals

The ingestion pipeline operates asynchronously. Documents are pushed into a queue, allowing several parser, chunking, metadata, and embedding workers to process them independently without blocking user-facing queries.

The query pipeline begins behind an API gateway and load balancer. Stateless RAG API instances can be added or removed according to traffic, while conversation state and cached results remain in external storage.

Retrieval is performed against a scalable search platform using vector, keyword, hybrid, or semantic retrieval. Modern enterprise retrieval must balance query understanding, multi-source access, limited context windows, response time, and granular access control.

Centralized observability captures metrics, logs, traces, and evaluation results across all components. This allows teams to identify whether latency or quality problems originate from retrieval, Re-Ranking, generation, or another dependency.


d) Two Major Pipelines

i. Ingestion and Indexing Pipeline


Documents
    ↓
Validation
    ↓
Parsing and OCR
    ↓
Cleaning
    ↓
Chunking
    ↓
Metadata Enrichment
    ↓
Embedding
    ↓
Vector and Document Storage


Documents are validated and parsed before being divided into semantically meaningful chunks. Metadata such as source, title, department, version, status, permissions, and timestamp is attached to every chunk.

Embedding workers convert chunks into vectors and store them in the search index. The raw or processed chunk text is stored with stable source metadata so retrieved evidence can later be cited.

The pipeline should support incremental insert, update, and delete operations. Rebuilding the complete index for every document change becomes impractical as the knowledge base grows.

Microsoft’s architecture guidance identifies chunking, metadata enrichment, embedding, and index persistence as the main stages in a RAG data pipeline.

ii. Online Query Pipeline

User Query
    ↓
Authentication
    ↓
Query Processing
    ↓
Retrieval
    ↓
Re-Ranking
    ↓
Context Construction
    ↓
LLM Generation
    ↓
Validation
    ↓
Response

The query is authenticated and enriched with tenant, role, department, region, and conversation context before retrieval.

Retrieval may combine semantic vector search with keyword search and metadata filters. Hybrid search is useful when questions contain both conceptual language and exact terms such as names, identifiers, or error codes.

A Re-Ranker orders the candidate evidence, after which the Context Builder selects the smallest sufficient set that fits within the model’s token budget.

The generated response passes through Groundedness, citation, safety, and format validation before it is returned to the user.

e) Core Components

1. API Gateway and Load Balancer
The API gateway authenticates users, enforces rate limits, validates requests, and assigns a correlation ID. The load balancer distributes traffic across multiple stateless application instances.

Query state should not exist only in one server’s memory. Storing sessions and conversation state externally allows any healthy application instance to process the next request.

2. Message Queue
The queue decouples source ingestion from document processing. It absorbs traffic bursts and prevents slow OCR, parsing, or embedding operations from blocking the source connector.

Failed messages can be retried or moved into a dead-letter queue for investigation instead of being silently lost.

3. Distributed Worker Pool
Parser, chunking, metadata, and embedding workers can scale independently. If embedding becomes the primary bottleneck, additional embedding workers can be added without scaling unrelated services.

4. Vector Database and Document Store
The vector database performs similarity retrieval over embeddings, while the corresponding document store preserves chunk text and metadata. Depending on the platform, these responsibilities may exist in one search service.

A scalable retrieval platform should support partitioning, replicas, filters, index updates, and resilient query serving. Storage and compute capacity must grow while maintaining acceptable recall and response time.

5. Query Orchestrator
The orchestrator coordinates query transformation, retrieval, Re-Ranking, context construction, model invocation, and output validation. It also applies timeouts and fallback policies around downstream services.

6. LLM Gateway
The gateway provides one controlled interface to one or more model deployments. It can manage rate limits, retries, token budgets, model routing, concurrency limits, fallback models, and cost tracking.

7. Cache Layer
Caching avoids repeated retrieval, embedding, and generation work. Common layers include query-embedding cache, retrieval cache, response cache, and document-processing cache.

Cached results must include tenant, permission, model, prompt, index version, and freshness information to prevent incorrect or unauthorized reuse.

8. Observability and Evaluation
Production systems should capture request latency, retrieval scores, selected chunks, token usage, errors, cache hits, model calls, sources, and quality metrics.

Evaluation should measure both component quality and end-to-end answer quality. A low-latency system is not successful if retrieval quality or Groundedness declines under load.

f) Code Example

import time
from threading import Lock
from typing import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. SHARED KNOWLEDGE INDEX
# =========================================================

knowledge_index = [
    {
        "id": "DOC-001",
        "content": (
            "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees "
            "per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "reimbursement",
            "limit",
            "annual",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-002",
        "content": (
            "Manager approval is required before registering "
            "for an approved professional certification."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "manager",
            "approval",
            "registration",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-003",
        "content": (
            "Employees receive twelve paid sick-leave days "
            "during each calendar year."
        ),
        "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "leave",
            "sick",
            "days",
        },
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 2. SHARED CACHE AND LOCK
# =========================================================

retrieval_cache: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
cache_lock = Lock()


# =========================================================
# 3. LANGGRAPH STATE
