# =========================================================

class ScalableRAGState(TypedDict):
    request_id: str
    question: str

    cache_hit: bool
    retrieved_documents: list[dict]
    final_answer: str

    retrieval_attempts: int
    request_started_at: float
    request_latency_ms: float


# =========================================================
# 4. QUERY NORMALIZATION
# =========================================================

def normalize_query(
    question: str,
) -> str:
    """
    Create a stable query-cache key.
    """

    return " ".join(
        question.lower().strip().split()
    )


# =========================================================
# 5. SIMPLE RETRIEVAL FUNCTION
# =========================================================

def search_index(
    question: str,
    top_k: int = 2,
) -> list"""
    Rank documents using keyword overlap.

    This can be replaced by a distributed vector database
    without changing the workflow structure.
    """

    query_terms = set(
        normalize_query(question).split()
    )

    scored_documents = []

    for document in knowledge_index:
        overlap_score = len(
            query_terms.intersection(
                document["keywords"]
            )
        )

        if overlap_score > 0:
            scored_documents.append(
                {
                    **document,
                    "score": overlap_score,
                }
            )

    scored_documents.sort(
        key=lambda document: document["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return scored_documents[:top_k]


# =========================================================
# 6. CACHE LOOKUP NODE
# =========================================================

def cache_lookup_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Read retrieved evidence from a shared cache.
    """

    cache_key = normalize_query(
        state["question"]
    )

    with cache_lock:
        cached_documents = retrieval_cache.get(
            cache_key
        )

    if cached_documents is not None:
        return {
            "cache_hit": True,
            "retrieved_documents": cached_documents,
        }

    return {
        "cache_hit": False,
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. ROUTE AFTER CACHE LOOKUP
# =========================================================

def route_after_cache(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> str:
    """
    Skip retrieval when reusable evidence is cached.
    """

    if state["cache_hit"]:
        return "generate_answer"

    return "retrieve_documents"


# =========================================================
# 8. RETRIEVAL NODE WITH BOUNDED RETRY
# =========================================================

def retrieve_documents_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve documents with a maximum of three attempts.
    """

    maximum_attempts = 3
    last_error = None

    for attempt in range(
        1,
        maximum_attempts + 1,
    ):
        try:
            documents = search_index(
                question=state["question"],
                top_k=2,
            )

            cache_key = normalize_query(
                state["question"]
            )

            with cache_lock:
                retrieval_cache[
                    cache_key
                ] = documents

            return {
                "retrieved_documents": documents,
                "retrieval_attempts": attempt,
            }

        except RuntimeError as error:
            last_error = error
            time.sleep(0.05 * attempt)

    raise RuntimeError(
        "Retrieval failed after retries."
    ) from last_error


# =========================================================
# 9. GROUNDED ANSWER-GENERATION NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Create a deterministic answer from retrieved evidence.
    """

    documents = state[
        "retrieved_documents"
    ]

    if not documents:
        return {
            "final_answer": (
                "No relevant evidence was found in the "
                "authorized knowledge index."
            )
        }

    evidence_text = " ".join(
        document["content"]
        for document in documents
    )

    unique_sources = sorted(
        {
            document["source"]
            for document in documents
        }
    )

    source_text = "\n".join(
        f"- {source}"
        for source in unique_sources
    )

    final_answer = (
        f"{evidence_text}\n\n"
        f"Sources:\n{source_text}"
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": final_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. METRICS NODE
# =========================================================

def record_metrics_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Calculate end-to-end request latency.
    """

    elapsed_seconds = (
        time.perf_counter()
        - state["request_started_at"]
    )

    return {
        "request_latency_ms": round(
            elapsed_seconds * 1000,
            2,
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

builder = StateGraph(
    ScalableRAGState
)

builder.add_node(
    "cache_lookup",
    cache_lookup_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_documents",
    retrieve_documents_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "record_metrics",
    record_metrics_node,
)


builder.add_edge(
    START,
    "cache_lookup",
)

builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "cache_lookup",
    route_after_cache,
    {
        "generate_answer": "generate_answer",
        "retrieve_documents": "retrieve_documents",
    },
)

builder.add_edge(
    "retrieve_documents",
    "generate_answer",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    "record_metrics",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "record_metrics",
    END,
)

scalable_rag_graph = builder.compile()


# =========================================================
# 12. RUN ONE RAG REQUEST
# =========================================================

def run_rag_request(
    request_id: str,
    question: str,
) -> None:
    initial_state: ScalableRAGState = {
        "request_id": request_id,
        "question": question,

        "cache_hit": False,
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "final_answer": "",

        "retrieval_attempts": 0,
        "request_started_at": (
            time.perf_counter()
        ),
        "request_latency_ms": 0.0,
    }

    result = scalable_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("REQUEST")
    print("=" * 70)

    print(
        "Request ID:",
        result["request_id"],
    )

    print(
        "Question:",
        result["question"],
    )

    print(
        "Cache hit:",
        result["cache_hit"],
    )

    print(
        "Retrieval attempts:",
        result["retrieval_attempts"],
    )

    print(
        "Latency:",
        result["request_latency_ms"],
        "ms",
    )

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 13. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    user_question = (
        "What is the annual certification reimbursement "
        "limit and what approval is required?"
    )

    # First request performs retrieval.
    run_rag_request(
        request_id="REQ-001",
        question=user_question,
    )

    print("\n\n")

    # Second identical request reuses cached evidence.
    run_rag_request(
        request_id="REQ-002",
        question=user_question,
    )

g) Important Scalability Techniques

Horizontal Scaling
Horizontal scaling adds more service instances rather than only increasing the resources of one server. Stateless API services, workers, and Re-Ranking services are suitable candidates for horizontal scaling.

Asynchronous Processing
Long-running ingestion work should run asynchronously through queues and worker pools. User queries should not wait for unrelated documents to be parsed or embedded.

Batch Embedding
Sending chunks to the embedding model in batches generally reduces per-request overhead. Batch size must remain within provider limits and worker memory capacity.

Index Partitioning and Replication
Partitions distribute index data and storage across nodes.
Replicas provide additional query-serving capacity and availability.
The correct combination depends on corpus size, query traffic, indexing rate, availability requirements, and retrieval platform.

Hybrid Retrieval and Re-Ranking
Hybrid retrieval combines keyword and vector search to improve candidate coverage. A Re-Ranker then produces a smaller, higher-quality final evidence set, helping control context-window and generation costs.

Backpressure
Backpressure prevents producers from overwhelming consumers. When embedding workers cannot keep up, the queue should buffer events, slow producers, or reject low-priority ingestion instead of exhausting system resources.

h) Code Example

import time

from threading import Lock
from typing import TypedDict

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. SHARED KNOWLEDGE INDEX
# =========================================================

knowledge_index = [
    {
        "id": "DOC-001",
        "content": (
            "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees "
            "per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "reimbursement",
            "limit",
            "annual",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-002",
        "content": (
            "Manager approval is required before registering "
            "for an approved professional certification."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "manager",
            "approval",
            "registration",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-003",
        "content": (
            "Employees receive twelve paid sick-leave days "
            "during each calendar year."
        ),
        "source": "leave_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "leave",
            "sick",
            "days",
        },
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 2. SHARED CACHE AND LOCK
# =========================================================

retrieval_cache: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
cache_lock = Lock()


# =========================================================
# 3. LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class ScalableRAGState(TypedDict):
    request_id: str
    question: str

    cache_hit: bool
    retrieved_documents: list[dict]
    final_answer: str

    retrieval_attempts: int
    request_started_at: float
    request_latency_ms: float


# =========================================================
# 4. QUERY NORMALIZATION
# =========================================================

def normalize_query(
    question: str,
) -> str:
    """
    Create a stable query-cache key.
    """

    return " ".join(
        question.lower().strip().split()
    )


# =========================================================
# 5. SIMPLE RETRIEVAL FUNCTION
# =========================================================

def search_index(
    question: str,
    top_k: int = 2,
) -> list"""
    Rank documents using keyword overlap.

    This can be replaced by a distributed vector database
    without changing the workflow structure.
    """

    query_terms = set(
        normalize_query(question).split()
    )

    scored_documents = []

    for document in knowledge_index:
        overlap_score = len(
            query_terms.intersection(
                document["keywords"]
            )
        )

        if overlap_score > 0:
            scored_documents.append(
                {
                    **document,
                    "score": overlap_score,
                }
            )

    scored_documents.sort(
        key=lambda document: document["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return scored_documents[:top_k]


# =========================================================
# 6. CACHE LOOKUP NODE
# =========================================================

def cache_lookup_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Read retrieved evidence from a shared cache.
    """

    cache_key = normalize_query(
        state["question"]
    )

    with cache_lock:
        cached_documents = retrieval_cache.get(
            cache_key
        )

    if cached_documents is not None:
        return {
            "cache_hit": True,
            "retrieved_documents": cached_documents,
        }

    return {
        "cache_hit": False,
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. ROUTE AFTER CACHE LOOKUP
# =========================================================

def route_after_cache(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> str:
    """
    Skip retrieval when reusable evidence is cached.
    """

    if state["cache_hit"]:
        return "generate_answer"

    return "retrieve_documents"


# =========================================================
# 8. RETRIEVAL NODE WITH BOUNDED RETRY
# =========================================================

def retrieve_documents_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve documents with a maximum of three attempts.
    """

    maximum_attempts = 3
    last_error = None

    for attempt in range(
        1,
        maximum_attempts + 1,
    ):
        try:
            documents = search_index(
                question=state["question"],
                top_k=2,
            )

            cache_key = normalize_query(
                state["question"]
            )

            with cache_lock:
                retrieval_cache[
                    cache_key
                ] = documents

            return {
                "retrieved_documents": documents,
                "retrieval_attempts": attempt,
            }

        except RuntimeError as error:
            last_error = error
            time.sleep(0.05 * attempt)

    raise RuntimeError(
        "Retrieval failed after retries."
    ) from last_error


# =========================================================
# 9. GROUNDED ANSWER-GENERATION NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Create a deterministic answer from retrieved evidence.
    """

    documents = state[
        "retrieved_documents"
    ]

    if not documents:
        return {
            "final_answer": (
                "No relevant evidence was found in the "
                "authorized knowledge index."
            )
        }

    evidence_text = " ".join(
        document["content"]
        for document in documents
    )

    unique_sources = sorted(
        {
            document["source"]
            for document in documents
        }
    )

    source_text = "\n".join(
        f"- {source}"
        for source in unique_sources
    )

    final_answer = (
        f"{evidence_text}\n\n"
        f"Sources:\n{source_text}"
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": final_answer,
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. METRICS NODE
# =========================================================

def record_metrics_node(
    state: ScalableRAGState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Calculate end-to-end request latency.
    """

    elapsed_seconds = (
        time.perf_counter()
        - state["request_started_at"]
    )

    return {
        "request_latency_ms": round(
            elapsed_seconds * 1000,
            2,
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

builder = StateGraph(
    ScalableRAGState
)

builder.add_node(
    "cache_lookup",
    cache_lookup_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_documents",
    retrieve_documents_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "record_metrics",
    record_metrics_node,
)


builder.add_edge(
    START,
    "cache_lookup",
)

builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "cache_lookup",
    route_after_cache,
    {
        "generate_answer": "generate_answer",
        "retrieve_documents": "retrieve_documents",
    },
)

builder.add_edge(
    "retrieve_documents",
    "generate_answer",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    "record_metrics",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "record_metrics",
    END,
)

scalable_rag_graph = builder.compile()


# =========================================================
# 12. RUN ONE RAG REQUEST
# =========================================================

def run_rag_request(
    request_id: str,
    question: str,
) -> None:
    initial_state: ScalableRAGState = {
        "request_id": request_id,
        "question": question,

        "cache_hit": False,
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "final_answer": "",

        "retrieval_attempts": 0,
        "request_started_at": (
            time.perf_counter()
        ),
        "request_latency_ms": 0.0,
    }

    result = scalable_rag_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("REQUEST")
    print("=" * 70)

    print(
        "Request ID:",
        result["request_id"],
    )

    print(
        "Question:",
        result["question"],
    )

    print(
        "Cache hit:",
        result["cache_hit"],
    )

    print(
        "Retrieval attempts:",
        result["retrieval_attempts"],
    )

    print(
        "Latency:",
        result["request_latency_ms"],
        "ms",
    )

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 13. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    user_question = (
        "What is the annual certification reimbursement "
        "limit and what approval is required?"
    )

    # First request performs retrieval.
    run_rag_request(
        request_id="REQ-001",
        question=user_question,
    )

    print("\n\n")

    # Second identical request reuses cached evidence.
    run_rag_request(
        request_id="REQ-002",
        question=user_question,
    )

i) Production Scaling Metrics

Requests per second
Concurrent requests
P50, P95, and P99 latency
Retrieval latency
LLM time to first token
Total generation latency
Indexing throughput
Embedding queue depth
Cache hit rate
Error and timeout rate
Token usage
Cost per request
Context Precision
Context Recall
Faithfulness
Answer Relevance

Percentile latency is more informative than average latency because a good average can hide a smaller set of extremely slow requests.

Operational metrics must be monitored together with quality metrics. Aggressive caching, smaller Top-K, or reduced Re-Ranking may improve latency while lowering retrieval completeness or answer quality.

j) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is a Scalable RAG Architecture?

A Scalable RAG Architecture is a production design that supports growth in users, queries, documents, embeddings, and model traffic while maintaining acceptable latency, reliability, security, and answer quality.

It normally separates ingestion and query processing and decomposes the workflow into independently scalable services such as parsing, embedding, retrieval, Re-Ranking, generation, caching, and evaluation.

Q2. Why should ingestion and query pipelines be separated?

Ingestion contains slow and resource-intensive operations such as OCR, parsing, chunking, metadata enrichment, and embedding. Query processing requires predictable low latency for retrieval and generation.

Separating them prevents ingestion spikes from consuming resources needed by user queries. It also allows ingestion workers and online query services to scale independently. Microsoft’s RAG reference flow similarly treats the data pipeline and application query flow as distinct workflows.

Q3. How would you scale the ingestion pipeline?

I would place incoming document events into a durable message queue and use independent worker pools for parsing, chunking, metadata enrichment, embedding, and index updates.

Workers would process idempotent tasks, use batch embedding where appropriate, retry transient errors, and send permanent failures to a dead-letter queue. Autoscaling could use queue depth and processing lag.

Q4. How would you scale the online query pipeline?

I would deploy stateless RAG API instances behind an API gateway and load balancer, store conversational state externally, and use a distributed cache for repeated computations.

The retrieval layer would use partitions and replicas, metadata filters, Hybrid Search, and Re-Ranking. The LLM gateway would manage model concurrency, token budgets, timeouts, retry policies, and fallback deployments.

Q5. What are the major bottlenecks in production RAG?

Common bottlenecks include document parsing, embedding throughput, vector-search latency, expensive Re-Ranking, excessive context construction, LLM rate limits, generation latency, and repeated work caused by missing caches.

The bottleneck should be identified through end-to-end traces and component-level metrics. Scaling every component equally wastes resources and may not improve the actual slowest stage.

Topic 2: Caching

a) Definition

Caching in RAG is the process of storing reusable intermediate results or final responses so repeated requests do not execute the complete pipeline again. A cached request may skip query embedding, vector retrieval, Re-Ranking, prompt construction, or LLM generation.

RAG applications can cache multiple stages independently, including document embeddings, query embeddings, retrieved contexts, generated responses, and evaluator results. This is called multi-level caching because each cache avoids a different type of repeated work.

Traditional caching uses exact keys and returns a result only when the new key matches an existing key. Semantic caching uses embeddings and vector similarity to reuse results for questions that have different wording but the same meaning.

Caching improves latency and reduces cost, but incorrect implementation can return stale, unauthorized, or contextually unsuitable answers. Cache keys and validation rules must therefore include document version, permissions, tenant, conversation context, prompt version, and model configuration.

b) Purpose

Reduce Response Latency
Retrieval and LLM generation may take several seconds. A cache hit avoids some or all of these operations and can return reusable information considerably faster.

Reduce Model and Infrastructure Cost
Repeated embedding and LLM calls consume tokens and computing resources. Semantic caching is particularly useful because it can recognize differently worded questions expressing the same intent.

Reduce Load on Dependencies
Caching reduces repeated traffic to vector databases, Re-Rankers, LLM providers, APIs, and enterprise knowledge sources. This helps a production system support more concurrent users.

Improve Availability
A valid cached result can sometimes provide a temporary fallback when a downstream dependency is unavailable. However, the response must clearly indicate its timestamp when freshness matters.

c) Caching Architecture

                         User Question
                               |
                               v
                    Normalize and Authorize
                               |
                               v
                      Exact Response Cache
                         /             \
                       Hit             Miss
                        |                |
                        v                v
                 Return Response   Semantic Cache
                                      /       \
                                    Hit       Miss
                                     |          |
                                     v          v
                              Validate Entry  Query Embedding
                                     |          |
                                     |          v
                                     |    Retrieval Cache
                                     |       /       \
                                     |     Hit       Miss
                                     |      |          |
                                     |      |          v
                                     |      |    Vector Search
                                     |      |          |
                                     |      +----+-----+
                                     |           |
                                     |           v
                                     |       Re-Ranking
                                     |           |
                                     +-----------+
                                                 |
                                                 v
                                         LLM Generation
                                                 |
                                                 v
                                    Store Approved Cache Entry
                                                 |
                                                 v
                                           Final Response

Architecture Explanation

The query is normalized and combined with security and version information before cache lookup. This prevents results created for one tenant or permission set from being returned to another user.

The system first performs an inexpensive exact-match lookup. If no exact entry exists, a semantic cache can compare the new query embedding with embeddings of previously answered questions.

When both response caches miss, the system can check a retrieval cache before querying the vector database. This saves search and Re-Ranking work while still allowing the LLM to generate a new answer.

Newly generated results are validated before they are cached. Ungrounded, unsafe, incomplete, or incorrectly cited responses should never be placed in a shared response cache.


d) Core Components

i. Cache Key Generator
The Cache Key Generator creates a deterministic identifier from the information that affects the result. A secure RAG cache key should generally contain more than the normalized question.

If any important field is excluded, the cache may return a response created under different knowledge, permissions, or generation instructions.

ii. Cache Store
The cache stores the key, reusable value, creation time, expiry time, and validation metadata. Production systems commonly use a distributed cache so all RAG API instances can access the same entries.

iii. Cache Policy
The cache policy determines what can be cached, how long it remains valid, and when it should be removed. Frequently changing operational data normally requires a shorter lifetime than stable reference documentation.

iv. Semantic Matcher
The semantic matcher embeds the new query and searches for similar cached-query embeddings. A cached result is reused only when its similarity exceeds a carefully evaluated threshold.

v. Validation Layer
Before returning a cached result, the validator checks expiry, index version, permissions, tenant, document version, and other requirements. A semantic match alone is not enough to establish safe reuse.

vi. Cache Monitoring
Cache monitoring tracks hit rate, miss rate, eviction count, stale-result rate, lookup latency, memory usage, and estimated cost savings. It should also detect whether aggressive caching is lowering answer quality.

e) Important Types of RAG Caching

i.Document Embedding Cache
Stores embeddings generated for document chunks. When an unchanged document is processed again, the system can reuse its existing embeddings instead of calling the embedding model.

ii. Query Embedding Cache
Stores the vector generated for a normalized user query. Repeated queries can skip the embedding call and proceed directly to vector retrieval.

iii.Retrieval Cache
Stores the document chunks returned for a query, along with their rankings and source metadata. It avoids repeated searches and Re-Ranking operations.

iv. Exact Response Cache
Stores the final validated response for an exact normalized query. It is simple and predictable but cannot reuse answers when users express the same request differently.

v. Semantic Response Cache
Stores query embeddings and validated responses. A new query can reuse a cached response when its meaning is sufficiently similar to an existing query.
Azure Cosmos DB documentation describes semantic caching as vector similarity search over prior prompt embeddings, allowing similar prompts to reuse earlier completions and reduce LLM latency and cost.

vi. Cache Eviction and Invalidation

Time-to-Live
A Time-to-Live value defines how long an entry remains reusable. After the TTL expires, the system removes the item or treats it as a cache miss.

Least Recently Used
LRU removes entries that have not been accessed recently when storage reaches capacity. It retains frequently used entries while controlling memory growth.

Version-Based Invalidation.
Each entry records the knowledge-index, prompt, model, and embedding versions. A mismatch invalidates the entry even when its TTL has not expired.

Event-Based Invalidation
When a source document changes, the ingestion pipeline publishes an invalidation event. Cache entries depending on that document are deleted immediately instead of waiting for TTL expiration.

f) Code Example

import hashlib
import time
from typing import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. KNOWLEDGE BASE
# =========================================================

knowledge_base = [
    {
        "id": "DOC-001",
        "content": (
            "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees "
            "per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "reimbursement",
            "limit",
            "claim",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-002",
        "content": (
            "Manager approval is required before registering "
            "for an approved professional certification."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "manager",
            "approval",
            "registration",
        },
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 2. IN-MEMORY RESPONSE CACHE
# =========================================================

response_cache: dict[str, dict] = {}

cache_statistics = {
    "hits": 0,
    "misses": 0,
}


# =========================================================
# 3. APPLICATION VERSIONS
# =========================================================

INDEX_VERSION = "index-v1"
PROMPT_VERSION = "prompt-v1"
MODEL_VERSION = "model-v1"

CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60


# =========================================================
# 4. LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class CacheState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    tenant_id: str
    permission_scope: str

    cache_key: str
    cache_hit: bool

    retrieved_documents: list[dict]
    final_answer: str

    processing_time_ms: float


# =========================================================
# 5. NORMALIZE THE QUERY
# =========================================================

def normalize_question(
    question: str,
) -> str:
    return " ".join(
        question.lower().strip().split()
    )


# =========================================================
# 6. GENERATE A SECURE, VERSIONED CACHE KEY
# =========================================================

def create_cache_key(
    question: str,
    tenant_id: str,
    permission_scope: str,
) -> str:
    raw_key = "|".join(
        [
            normalize_question(question),
            tenant_id,
            permission_scope,
            INDEX_VERSION,
            PROMPT_VERSION,
            MODEL_VERSION,
        ]
    )

    return hashlib.sha256(
        raw_key.encode("utf-8")
    ).hexdigest()


# =========================================================
# 7. CACHE LOOKUP NODE
# =========================================================

def cache_lookup_node(
    state: CacheState,
) -> dict:
    cache_key = create_cache_key(
        question=state["question"],
        tenant_id=state["tenant_id"],
        permission_scope=state["permission_scope"],
    )

    cached_entry = response_cache.get(
        cache_key
    )

    if cached_entry is None:
        cache_statistics["misses"] += 1

        return {
            "cache_key": cache_key,
            "cache_hit": False,
        }

    entry_age = (
        time.time()
        - cached_entry["created_at"]
    )

    if entry_age > CACHE_TTL_SECONDS:
        del response_cache[cache_key]
        cache_statistics["misses"] += 1

        return {
            "cache_key": cache_key,
            "cache_hit": False,
        }

    cache_statistics["hits"] += 1

    return {
        "cache_key": cache_key,
        "cache_hit": True,
        "retrieved_documents": (
            cached_entry["retrieved_documents"]
        ),
        "final_answer": (
            cached_entry["final_answer"]
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 8. ROUTE AFTER CACHE LOOKUP
# =========================================================

def route_after_cache(
    state: CacheState,
) -> str:
    if state["cache_hit"]:
        return "record_metrics"

    return "retrieve_documents"


# =========================================================
# 9. RETRIEVAL NODE
# =========================================================

def retrieve_documents_node(
    state: CacheState,
) -> dict:
    query_terms = set(
        normalize_question(
            state["question"]
        ).split()
    )

    scored_documents = []

    for document in knowledge_base:
        score = len(
            query_terms.intersection(
                document["keywords"]
            )
        )

        if score > 0:
            scored_documents.append(
                {
                    **document,
                    "score": score,
                }
            )

    scored_documents.sort(
        key=lambda item: item["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    return {
        "retrieved_documents": (
            scored_documents[:2]
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. ANSWER-GENERATION NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: CacheState,
) -> dict:
    documents = state[
        "retrieved_documents"
    ]

    if not documents:
        return {
            "final_answer": (
                "No relevant evidence was found."
            )
        }

    evidence = " ".join(
        document["content"]
        for document in documents
    )

    sources = sorted(
        {
            document["source"]
            for document in documents
        }
    )

    source_text = "\n".join(
        f"- {source}"
        for source in sources
    )

    return {
        "final_answer": (
            f"{evidence}\n\n"
            f"Sources:\n{source_text}"
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. CACHE-WRITE NODE
# =========================================================

def cache_write_node(
    state: CacheState,
) -> dict:
    response_cache[
        state["cache_key"]
    ] = {
        "final_answer": state["final_answer"],
        "retrieved_documents": (
            state["retrieved_documents"]
        ),
        "created_at": time.time(),
        "index_version": INDEX_VERSION,
        "prompt_version": PROMPT_VERSION,
        "model_version": MODEL_VERSION,
    }

    return {}


# =========================================================
# 12. METRICS NODE
# =========================================================

def record_metrics_node(
    state: CacheState,
) -> dict:
    elapsed_time = (
        time.perf_counter()
        - request_start_times[
            state["cache_key"]
        ]
    )

    return {
        "processing_time_ms": round(
            elapsed_time * 1000,
            3,
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 13. BUILD THE LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

builder = StateGraph(CacheState)

builder.add_node(
    "cache_lookup",
    cache_lookup_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_documents",
    retrieve_documents_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "cache_write",
    cache_write_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "record_metrics",
    record_metrics_node,
)

builder.add_edge(
    START,
    "cache_lookup",
)

builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "cache_lookup",
    route_after_cache,
    {
        "retrieve_documents": "retrieve_documents",
        "record_metrics": "record_metrics",
    },
)

builder.add_edge(
    "retrieve_documents",
    "generate_answer",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    "cache_write",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "cache_write",
    "record_metrics",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "record_metrics",
    END,
)

cache_graph = builder.compile()


# =========================================================
# 14. REQUEST START-TIME STORAGE
# =========================================================

request_start_times: dict[str, float] = {}


# =========================================================
# 15. RUN ONE REQUEST
# =========================================================

def run_cached_rag(
    question: str,
    tenant_id: str,
    permission_scope: str,
) -> None:
    cache_key = create_cache_key(
        question=question,
        tenant_id=tenant_id,
        permission_scope=permission_scope,
    )

    request_start_times[
        cache_key
    ] = time.perf_counter()

    initial_state: CacheState = {
        "question": question,
        "tenant_id": tenant_id,
        "permission_scope": permission_scope,

        "cache_key": cache_key,
        "cache_hit": False,

        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "final_answer": "",

        "processing_time_ms": 0.0,
    }

    result = cache_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("RAG CACHE RESULT")
    print("=" * 70)

    print("Question:", result["question"])
    print("Cache hit:", result["cache_hit"])

    print(
        "Processing time:",
        result["processing_time_ms"],
        "ms",
    )

    print(
        "Cache hits:",
        cache_statistics["hits"],
    )

    print(
        "Cache misses:",
        cache_statistics["misses"],
    )

    print("\nAnswer:")
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 16. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    question = (
        "What is the certification reimbursement "
        "limit and what approval is required?"
    )

    # First request populates the cache.
    run_cached_rag(
        question=question,
        tenant_id="tenant-a",
        permission_scope="employee",
    )

    print("\n\n")

    # Second identical request uses the cache.
    run_cached_rag(
        question=question,
        tenant_id="tenant-a",
        permission_scope="employee",
    )

vii. Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What is caching in a RAG system?

Caching stores reusable results from one or more stages of the RAG pipeline. A repeated request may reuse a query embedding, retrieved contexts, Re-Ranked evidence, or final response instead of executing the complete workflow again.

It improves latency, lowers model and infrastructure cost, and reduces dependency load. However, entries must be validated for freshness, permissions, tenant, and application version before reuse.

Q2. What are the main cache levels in RAG?

The main cache levels are document-embedding cache, query-embedding cache, retrieval cache, Re-Ranking cache, response cache, and evaluator cache.

Each level removes different work. Query-embedding caching avoids embedding calls, retrieval caching avoids vector search, and response caching can avoid most of the RAG pipeline.

Q3. What is semantic caching?

Semantic caching uses embeddings and vector similarity to determine whether a new query has the same meaning as a previously answered query. It can therefore reuse results even when the wording is different.

It requires a carefully calibrated similarity threshold and additional checks for conversation context, permissions, source version, and freshness.

Q4. How do you prevent stale cached answers?

Use TTL expiration, index and document versions, event-based invalidation, source timestamps, and dependency tracking. When a source document changes, the application should invalidate every cache entry derived from that document.

Frequently changing operational data should use shorter TTL values or bypass response caching entirely when current accuracy is essential.

Q5. What should be included in a RAG response-cache key?

A secure cache key should include the normalized query, tenant ID, permission scope, conversation context, language, retrieval filters, index version, prompt version, and model version.

Omitting these fields may reuse a response under the wrong data scope, knowledge version, retrieval configuration, or generation behaviour.

Topic 3: Monitoring

a) Definition

Monitoring is the continuous collection, measurement, and analysis of signals that describe the health and performance of a production RAG system. It helps teams detect failures, quality degradation, unexpected costs, and changes in user traffic.

RAG monitoring covers multiple layers, including ingestion, retrieval, Re-Ranking, generation, caching, infrastructure, and final response quality. Monitoring only the LLM endpoint is insufficient because the failure may originate from stale data, poor retrieval, or an unhealthy vector database.

Monitoring generally begins after deployment, while evaluation is primarily performed during development and testing. However, both use similar quality, cost, and latency measurements to determine whether the RAG system meets application requirements.

A production monitoring system should use dashboards, automated alerts, and defined response procedures. Collecting metrics without thresholds or operational actions does not help the team respond when the system degrades.

b) Purpose

Detect Operational Failures
Monitoring identifies unavailable services, failed ingestion jobs, vector-search errors, LLM rate limits, API timeouts, and unhealthy worker queues before they affect a large number of users.

Detect Quality Degradation
A RAG service may remain technically available while retrieving irrelevant documents or generating unsupported answers. Monitoring should therefore track Context Precision, Context Recall, Groundedness, Answer Relevance, and user feedback.

Control Latency and Cost
Token consumption, model calls, search requests, cache-miss rate, and Re-Ranking usage directly affect operational cost. Monitoring helps detect expensive query patterns and unexpected consumption.

Support Incident Response
Alerts notify the responsible team when a metric crosses its accepted threshold. The team can investigate the affected component, apply a fallback, roll back a deployment, or temporarily disable an unhealthy dependency.

c) Monitoring Architecture

                    RAG MONITORING ARCHITECTURE
                    ===========================

                         User Requests
                               |
                               v
                      Production RAG System
                               |
           +-------------------+-------------------+
           |                   |                   |
           v                   v                   v
      Ingestion Layer     Retrieval Layer     Generation Layer
           |                   |                   |
           v                   v                   v
     Queue and Index      Search and Rank     LLM and Validation
         Metrics             Metrics              Metrics
           |                   |                   |
           +-------------------+-------------------+
                               |
                               v
                      Telemetry Collection
                  Metrics | Logs | Quality Scores
                               |
                               v
                    Central Monitoring Platform
                               |
                  +------------+------------+
                  |                         |
                  v                         v
             Dashboards                  Alerts
                  |                         |
                  v                         v
          Trend Analysis            Incident Response

d). Core Components

1. Instrumentation

Instrumentation adds metric and event collection to application components. Each ingestion job, retrieval call, model request, cache lookup, and validation operation should record its execution outcome and duration.

Instrumentation should be lightweight and asynchronous where possible. Excessive telemetry processing inside the user request path can increase response latency.

2. Metrics Store

The metrics store collects numerical time-series measurements such as request volume, error rate, cache hit rate, queue depth, token consumption, and retrieval latency.

Metrics should include labels or dimensions for environment, region, model, tenant, source, endpoint, and deployment version.

3. Quality Evaluator

The evaluator scores sampled production responses for Groundedness, relevance, citation correctness, and other quality measures. Evaluating every response may be expensive, so production systems often evaluate a representative sample.

Component metrics and end-to-end metrics are both required because a RAG application contains several interacting components whose individual changes can affect overall quality.

4. Monitoring Dashboard
Dashboards summarize health, traffic, latency, retrieval quality, generation quality, token usage, and cost. Separate views can be created for engineering, operations, quality, and business teams.

5. Alert Manager
The Alert Manager compares metrics with static or dynamic thresholds and sends notifications through email, chat, incident-management, or on-call systems.

6. Incident Runbook
A runbook describes how engineers should investigate and resolve each alert. It includes initial checks, responsible owners, fallback options, rollback procedures, and escalation paths.

e) Important Monitoring Metrics

1. Application Metrics
Requests per second, Concurrent requests, Success rate, Error rate, Timeout rate, Availability, P50, P95, and P99.
Average latency alone is insufficient because a small number of very slow requests can be hidden by many fast requests. Percentile measurements reveal the experience of users affected by tail latency.

2. Ingestion Metrics
Documents processed, Documents failed, Processing duration, Queue depth, Oldest queued-message age, Embedding throughput, Indexing lag, Dead-letter queue size.

Increasing queue depth indicates that data is arriving faster than workers can process it. Indexing lag helps determine whether the searchable knowledge base is becoming stale.

3. Retrieval Metrics
Search latency, Empty-result rate, Top-K similarity distribution, Context Precision, Context Recall, Re-Ranking latency, Filter rejection rate, Retrieved-source distribution.

A sudden increase in empty results may indicate a broken index, incorrect metadata filters, or query-transformation failure.

Search latency and availability should be monitored together with retrieval quality. A faster retriever is not an improvement if it consistently misses required evidence.

4. LLM Metrics 
Model-call latency, Time to first token, Input tokens, Output tokens, Rate-limit errors, Timeouts, Blocked requests, Generation failures, Fallback-model usage.

Microsoft’s baseline alerts for RAG systems include signals such as total calls, errors, latency, success rate, token transactions, blocked calls, and model utilization.

5. Response-Quality Metrics
Groundedness, Faithfulness, Answer Relevance, Answer Correctness, Citation correctness, Citation coverage, Abstention rate, Hallucination rate.

Quality monitoring is necessary because an application may remain technically healthy but return weak answers. Microsoft Foundry includes RAG-specific evaluators for measurements such as Groundedness and relevance.

6. Cache Matrics
Cache hit rate, Cache miss rate, Cache lookup latency, Cache eviction count, Expired-entry count.

                 Number of cache hits
Cache Hit Rate = --------------------
                 Total cache lookups

A falling hit rate can increase retrieval and LLM cost. An unusually high hit rate may indicate overly broad semantic matching or incorrect cache reuse.

7. Cost Metrics 

Input tokens per request, Output tokens per request, Embedding calls, Re-Ranking calls, Vector-search operations, LLM cost per request, Daily model cost
Cost per successful answer.

Cost should be analyzed by model, tenant, feature, query category, and environment. A single aggregate cost figure may hide one workflow responsible for most consumption.

f) Code Example

import time
from typing import TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. KNOWLEDGE BASE
# =========================================================

knowledge_base = [
    {
        "id": "DOC-001",
        "content": (
            "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees "
            "per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "reimbursement",
            "limit",
            "claim",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-002",
        "content": (
            "Manager approval is required before registering "
            "for an approved professional certification."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "manager",
            "approval",
            "registration",
        },
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 2. SIMPLE RETRIEVAL CACHE
# =========================================================

retrieval_cache: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}


# =========================================================
# 3. MONITORING METRICS
# =========================================================

monitoring_metrics = {
    "total_requests": 0,
    "successful_requests": 0,
    "failed_requests": 0,
    "empty_retrievals": 0,
    "cache_hits": 0,
    "cache_misses": 0,
    "total_retrieval_ms": 0.0,
    "total_generation_ms": 0.0,
    "total_request_ms": 0.0,
}


# =========================================================
# 4. LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class MonitoringState(TypedDict):
    request_id: str
    question: str

    request_started_at: float
    retrieval_started_at: float
    generation_started_at: float

    cache_hit: bool
    retrieved_documents: list[dict]
    final_answer: str

    retrieval_latency_ms: float
    generation_latency_ms: float
    total_latency_ms: float

    request_successful: bool
    alerts: list[str]


# =========================================================
# 5. QUERY NORMALIZATION
# =========================================================

def normalize_question(
    question: str,
) -> str:
    return " ".join(
        question.lower().strip().split()
    )


# =========================================================
# 6. START REQUEST-MONITORING NODE
# =========================================================

def start_request_node(
    state: MonitoringState,
) -> dict:
    monitoring_metrics[
        "total_requests"
    ] += 1

    return {
        "request_started_at": (
            time.perf_counter()
        )
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. CACHE-LOOKUP NODE
# =========================================================

def cache_lookup_node(
    state: MonitoringState,
) -> dict:
    cache_key = normalize_question(
        state["question"]
    )

    cached_documents = retrieval_cache.get(
        cache_key
    )

    if cached_documents is not None:
        monitoring_metrics[
            "cache_hits"
        ] += 1

        return {
            "cache_hit": True,
            "retrieved_documents": (
                cached_documents
            ),
        }

    monitoring_metrics[
        "cache_misses"
    ] += 1

    return {
        "cache_hit": False,
    }


# =========================================================
# 8. ROUTE AFTER CACHE LOOKUP
# =========================================================

def route_after_cache(
    state: MonitoringState,
) -> str:
    if state["cache_hit"]:
        return "generate_answer"

    return "retrieve_documents"


# =========================================================
# 9. RETRIEVAL NODE
# =========================================================

def retrieve_documents_node(
    state: MonitoringState,
) -> dict:
    retrieval_started_at = (
        time.perf_counter()
    )

    query_terms = set(
        normalize_question(
            state["question"]
        ).split()
    )

    scored_documents = []

    for document in knowledge_base:
        score = len(
            query_terms.intersection(
                document["keywords"]
            )
        )

        if score > 0:
            scored_documents.append(
                {
                    **document,
                    "score": score,
                }
            )

    scored_documents.sort(
        key=lambda item: item["score"],
        reverse=True,
    )

    retrieved_documents = (
        scored_documents[:2]
    )

    retrieval_latency_ms = (
        time.perf_counter()
        - retrieval_started_at
    ) * 1000

    cache_key = normalize_question(
        state["question"]
    )

    retrieval_cache[
        cache_key
    ] = retrieved_documents

    monitoring_metrics[
        "total_retrieval_ms"
    ] += retrieval_latency_ms

    if not retrieved_documents:
        monitoring_metrics[
            "empty_retrievals"
        ] += 1

    return {
        "retrieval_started_at": (
            retrieval_started_at
        ),
        "retrieved_documents": (
            retrieved_documents
        ),
        "retrieval_latency_ms": round(
            retrieval_latency_ms,
            3,
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 10. GENERATION NODE
# =========================================================

def generate_answer_node(
    state: MonitoringState,
) -> dict:
    generation_started_at = (
        time.perf_counter()
    )

    documents = state[
        "retrieved_documents"
    ]

    if not documents:
        final_answer = (
            "No relevant evidence was found."
        )

        request_successful = False
    else:
        evidence = " ".join(
            document["content"]
            for document in documents
        )

        sources = sorted(
            {
                document["source"]
                for document in documents
            }
        )

        source_text = "\n".join(
            f"- {source}"
            for source in sources
        )

        final_answer = (
            f"{evidence}\n\n"
            f"Sources:\n{source_text}"
        )

        request_successful = True

    generation_latency_ms = (
        time.perf_counter()
        - generation_started_at
    ) * 1000

    monitoring_metrics[
        "total_generation_ms"
    ] += generation_latency_ms

    return {
        "generation_started_at": (
            generation_started_at
        ),
        "generation_latency_ms": round(
            generation_latency_ms,
            3,
        ),
        "final_answer": final_answer,
        "request_successful": (
            request_successful
        ),
    }


# =========================================================
# 11. COMPLETE METRICS AND CREATE ALERTS
# =========================================================

def complete_monitoring_node(
    state: MonitoringState,
) -> dict:
    total_latency_ms = (
        time.perf_counter()
        - state["request_started_at"]
    ) * 1000

    monitoring_metrics[
        "total_request_ms"
    ] += total_latency_ms

    if state["request_successful"]:
        monitoring_metrics[
            "successful_requests"
        ] += 1
    else:
        monitoring_metrics[
            "failed_requests"
        ] += 1

    alerts = []

    if total_latency_ms > 1000:
        alerts.append(
            "WARNING: Request latency exceeded 1 second."
        )

    if not state["retrieved_documents"]:
        alerts.append(
            "CRITICAL: Retrieval returned no evidence."
        )

    if (
        state["retrieval_latency_ms"]
        > 500
    ):
        alerts.append(
            "WARNING: Retrieval latency exceeded 500 ms."
        )

    return {
        "total_latency_ms": round(
            total_latency_ms,
            3,
        ),
        "alerts": alerts,
    }


# =========================================================
# 12. BUILD LANGGRAPH WORKFLOW
# =========================================================

builder = StateGraph(MonitoringState)

builder.add_node(
    "start_request",
    start_request_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "cache_lookup",
    cache_lookup_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "retrieve_documents",
    retrieve_documents_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "generate_answer",
    generate_answer_node,
)

builder.add_node(
    "complete_monitoring",
    complete_monitoring_node,
)

builder.add_edge(
    START,
    "start_request",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "start_request",
    "cache_lookup",
)

builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "cache_lookup",
    route_after_cache,
    {
        "retrieve_documents": "retrieve_documents",
        "generate_answer": "generate_answer",
    },
)

builder.add_edge(
    "retrieve_documents",
    "generate_answer",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "generate_answer",
    "complete_monitoring",
)

builder.add_edge(
    "complete_monitoring",
    END,
)

monitoring_graph = builder.compile()


# =========================================================
# 13. RUN ONE MONITORED REQUEST
# =========================================================

def run_monitored_request(
    request_id: str,
    question: str,
) -> None:
    initial_state: MonitoringState = {
        "request_id": request_id,
        "question": question,

        "request_started_at": 0.0,
        "retrieval_started_at": 0.0,
        "generation_started_at": 0.0,

        "cache_hit": False,
        "retrieved_documents": [],
        "final_answer": "",

        "retrieval_latency_ms": 0.0,
        "generation_latency_ms": 0.0,
        "total_latency_ms": 0.0,

        "request_successful": False,
        "alerts": [],
    }

    result = monitoring_graph.invoke(
        initial_state
    )

    print("=" * 70)
    print("REQUEST MONITORING")
    print("=" * 70)

    print(
        "Request ID:",
        result["request_id"],
    )

    print(
        "Cache hit:",
        result["cache_hit"],
    )

    print(
        "Retrieval latency:",
        result["retrieval_latency_ms"],
        "ms",
    )

    print(
        "Generation latency:",
        result["generation_latency_ms"],
        "ms",
    )

    print(
        "Total latency:",
        result["total_latency_ms"],
        "ms",
    )

    print(
        "Successful:",
        result["request_successful"],
    )

    print("\nALERTS")
    print("-" * 70)

    if result["alerts"]:
        for alert in result["alerts"]:
            print(alert)
    else:
        print("No alerts generated.")

    print("\nFINAL ANSWER")
    print("-" * 70)
    print(result["final_answer"])


# =========================================================
# 14. DISPLAY AGGREGATED METRICS
# =========================================================

def display_monitoring_summary() -> None:
    total_requests = monitoring_metrics[
        "total_requests"
    ]

    cache_lookups = (
        monitoring_metrics["cache_hits"]
        + monitoring_metrics["cache_misses"]
    )

    cache_hit_rate = (
        monitoring_metrics["cache_hits"]
        / cache_lookups
        if cache_lookups > 0
        else 0.0
    )

    success_rate = (
        monitoring_metrics[
            "successful_requests"
        ]
        / total_requests
        if total_requests > 0
        else 0.0
    )

    average_latency = (
        monitoring_metrics[
            "total_request_ms"
        ]
        / total_requests
        if total_requests > 0
        else 0.0
    )

    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print("MONITORING SUMMARY")
    print("=" * 70)

    print(
        "Total requests:",
        total_requests,
    )

    print(
        "Successful requests:",
        monitoring_metrics[
            "successful_requests"
        ],
    )

    print(
        "Failed requests:",
        monitoring_metrics[
            "failed_requests"
        ],
    )

    print(
        "Success rate:",
        round(success_rate, 2),
    )

    print(
        "Cache hit rate:",
        round(cache_hit_rate, 2),
    )

    print(
        "Empty retrievals:",
        monitoring_metrics[
            "empty_retrievals"
        ],
    )

    print(
        "Average latency:",
        round(average_latency, 3),
        "ms",
    )


# =========================================================
# 15. PROGRAM ENTRY POINT
# =========================================================

if __name__ == "__main__":
    question = (
        "What is the certification reimbursement "
        "limit and what approval is required?"
    )

    run_monitored_request(
        request_id="REQ-001",
        question=question,
    )

    print("\n\n")

    run_monitored_request(
        request_id="REQ-002",
        question=question,
    )

    display_monitoring_summary()

g) Interview Questions and Answers

Q1. What should be monitored in a production RAG system?

A production RAG system should monitor application traffic, latency, availability, errors, ingestion throughput, indexing lag, retrieval performance, LLM token usage, cache behaviour, response quality, and infrastructure utilization.

It should include component-level and end-to-end metrics because a healthy API can still produce poor responses when retrieval or generation quality degrades.

Q2. What is the difference between monitoring and evaluation?

Evaluation is mainly used during development and testing to compare configurations and determine whether the RAG system satisfies quality requirements.

Monitoring continuously measures the deployed application using real production traffic. However, they overlap because production monitoring can apply evaluation metrics such as Groundedness, relevance, and correctness to sampled responses.

Q3. Which latency measurements are important?

Important measurements include end-to-end request latency, query-processing time, retrieval latency, Re-Ranking latency, time to first token, generation duration, and validation latency.

P50, P95, and P99 should be tracked rather than only the average. Percentiles reveal the experience of slower requests and help identify tail-latency problems.

Q4. How do you monitor RAG response quality in production?

Sample production requests and evaluate their retrieved contexts and responses using Context Precision, Context Recall, Groundedness, Faithfulness, Answer Relevance, citation correctness, and application-specific metrics.

Automated evaluators should be calibrated against human-reviewed examples. User feedback and failed interactions should be added to the permanent evaluation dataset.

Q5. How should alerts be designed for a RAG system?

Alerts should be tied to actionable failures and service objectives. Examples include high P95 latency, increased empty retrievals, excessive model errors, indexing lag, reduced Groundedness, or abnormal token consumption.

Each alert should have a severity, responsible owner, investigation runbook, and escalation path. Related signals should be combined where possible to reduce false alarms.

Topic 4: Observability

a) Definition

Observability is the ability to understand the internal behaviour of a RAG system by examining the telemetry it produces. It allows engineers to reconstruct how a user query moved through query processing, retrieval, Re-Ranking, context construction, LLM generation, and validation.

RAG observability is built primarily around logs, metrics, and distributed traces. These signals help developers investigate slow requests, empty retrievals, irrelevant chunks, model errors, unsupported answers, excessive token use, and unexpected workflow decisions.

Distributed tracing represents one request as a trace containing multiple spans. A span records one operation, such as retrieval or generation, including its start time, duration, status, inputs, outputs, and relevant metadata.

Microsoft Foundry supports observability using evaluation, production monitoring, and distributed tracing. Its tracing integrations can capture model calls, retrieval operations, tool execution, and LangGraph node execution using OpenTelemetry-compatible telemetry.

b) Purpose

Root-Cause Analysis
Observability helps determine whether a weak answer was caused by query transformation, retrieval, Re-Ranking, context construction, generation, or validation. Without detailed traces, teams may see only the incorrect final response.

Performance Debugging
Span timings show which operation caused high latency. For example, a five-second request may contain a fast vector search but a slow Re-Ranker or LLM call.

Quality Investigation
Observability can record retrieved document IDs, relevance scores, selected context, model and prompt versions, and evaluation scores. This makes it possible to explain why the generator produced a particular answer.

Cost Investigation
Prompt tokens, completion tokens, model deployments, cache decisions, and repeated calls can be associated with a single trace. This helps identify workflows generating abnormal costs.

c) Architectural Diagram

                    RAG OBSERVABILITY ARCHITECTURE
                    ==============================

                         User Request
                               |
                               v
                      Start Root Trace
                               |
              +----------------+----------------+
              |                |                |
              v                v                v
        Query Span       Retrieval Span    Cache Span
              |                |                |
              |                v                |
              |         Retrieved Documents    |
              |         Scores and Sources     |
              |                |                |
              +----------------+----------------+
                               |
                               v
                        Re-Ranking Span
                               |
                               v
                    Context Construction Span
                               |
                               v
                       LLM Generation Span
                 Model | Tokens | Latency | Status
                               |
                               v
                       Validation Span
                 Groundedness | Citations | Safety
                               |
                               v
                        Final Response
                               |
                               v
                  Telemetry Export and Storage
               Logs | Metrics | Traces | Evaluations
                               |
                               v
                    Trace Explorer and Dashboard

d) Core Components

1. Trace
A trace represents the complete lifecycle of one request. It begins when the request enters the application and ends after answer generation and validation.

2. Span
A span represents one operation inside a trace. Every span normally contains a name, parent span, start time, duration, status, attributes, and optional error information.

3. Structured Logs
Structured logs store events as consistent fields instead of unstructured sentences. This makes them easier to filter and correlate.

4. Metrics
Metrics provide aggregated numerical measurements such as request rate, model latency, retrieval latency, token usage, failure rate, and cache-hit rate.
Metrics show trends and trigger alerts, while traces provide the detailed execution path needed to investigate those alerts.

5. Context Propagation
Context propagation passes trace identifiers across services. The API, retriever, Re-Ranker, LLM gateway, and validator must use the same trace context so their spans appear under one request.

6. Trace Backend
The backend stores, searches, and visualizes telemetry. It should support trace trees, duration analysis, error filtering, attribute search, and integration with monitoring dashboards.

e) Code Example

import time
import uuid
from typing import Literal, TypedDict
from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph


# =========================================================
# 1. KNOWLEDGE BASE
# =========================================================

knowledge_base = [
    {
        "id": "DOC-001",
        "content": (
            "Employees may claim up to 25,000 rupees "
            "per financial year for approved professional "
            "certifications."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "reimbursement",
            "limit",
            "claim",
        },
    },
    {
        "id": "DOC-002",
        "content": (
            "Manager approval is required before registering "
            "for an approved professional certification."
        ),
        "source": "certification_policy_2026.txt",
        "keywords": {
            "certification",
            "manager",
            "approval",
            "registration",
        },
    },
]


# =========================================================
# 2. RETRIEVAL CACHE
# =========================================================

retrieval_cache: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}


# =========================================================
# 3. TRACE STORAGE
# =========================================================

trace_store: dict[str, dict] = {}


# =========================================================
# 4. LANGGRAPH STATE
# =========================================================

class ObservabilityState(TypedDict):
    request_id: str
    trace_id: str
    question: str

    cache_hit: bool
    retrieved_documents: list[dict]
    final_answer: str

    trace_successful: bool
    trace_error: str


# =========================================================
# 5. QUERY NORMALIZATION
# =========================================================

def normalize_question(question: str) -> str:
    """
    Normalize a question for exact cache lookup.
    """

    return " ".join(
        question.lower().strip().split()
    )


# =========================================================
# 6. CREATE THE ROOT TRACE
# =========================================================

def start_trace_node(
    state: ObservabilityState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Create the root trace for the complete RAG request.
    """

    trace_id = str(uuid.uuid4())

    trace_store[trace_id] = {
        "trace_id": trace_id,
        "request_id": state["request_id"],
        "question": state["question"],
        "started_at": time.perf_counter(),
        "ended_at": None,
        "duration_ms": 0.0,
        "status": "running",
        "spans": [],
    }

    return {
        "trace_id": trace_id,
    }


# =========================================================
# 7. SPAN HELPER
# =========================================================

def record_span(
    trace_id: str,
    span_name: str,
    started_at: float,
    status: Literal["success", "error"],
    attributes: dict,
    error: str = "",
) -> None:
    """
    Record one completed span inside the root trace.
    """

    ended_at = time.perf_counter()

    duration_ms = (
        ended_at - started_at
    ) * 1000

    span = {
        "span_id": str(uuid.uuid4()),
        "name": span_name,
        "status": status,
        "duration_ms": round(
            duration_ms,
            3,
        ),
        "attributes": attributes,
        "error": error,
    }

    trace_store[
        trace_id
    ]["spans"].append(span)


# =========================================================
# 8. CACHE LOOKUP NODE
# =========================================================

def cache_lookup_node(
    state: ObservabilityState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Check the retrieval cache and record a cache span.
    """

    span_started_at = time.perf_counter()

    cache_key = normalize_question(
        state["question"]
    )

    cached_documents = retrieval_cache.get(
        cache_key
    )

    cache_hit = (
        cached_documents is not None
    )

    record_span(
        trace_id=state["trace_id"],
        span_name="cache_lookup",
        started_at=span_started_at,
        status="success",
        attributes={
            "cache.key": cache_key,
            "cache.hit": cache_hit,
        },
    )

    if cache_hit:
        return {
            "cache_hit": True,
            "retrieved_documents": cached_documents,
        }

    return {
        "cache_hit": False,
    }


# =========================================================
# 9. ROUTE AFTER CACHE LOOKUP
# =========================================================

def route_after_cache(
    state: ObservabilityState,
) -> Literal[
    "retrieve_documents",
    "generate_answer",
]:
    """
    Skip retrieval when cached evidence exists.
    """

    if state["cache_hit"]:
        return "generate_answer"

    return "retrieve_documents"


# =========================================================
# 10. RETRIEVAL NODE
# =========================================================

def retrieve_documents_node(
    state: ObservabilityState,
) -> dict:
    """
    Retrieve documents and record retrieval attributes.
    """

    span_started_at = time.perf_counter()

    try:
        query_terms = set(
            normalize_question(
                state["question"]
            ).split()
        )

        scored_documents = []

        for document in knowledge_base:
            score = len(
                query_terms.intersection(
                    document["keywords"]
                )
            )

            if score > 0:
                scored_documents.append(
                    {
                        **document,
                **      "score": score,
